## A Princess’s Vengeance / 公主的复仇

===Original===
“The girl is young, pretty, friendless and a foreigner, you say, and has disappeared as completely as if the earth had opened to receive her,” said Miss Brooke, making a résumé of the facts that Mr. Dyer had been relating to her. “Now, will you tell me why two days were allowed to elapse before the police were communicated with?”

“Mrs. Druce, the lady to whom Lucie Cunier acted as amanuensis,” answered Mr. Dyer, “took the matter very calmly at first and said she felt sure that the girl would write to her in a day or so, explaining her extraordinary conduct. Major Druce, her son, the gentleman who came to me this morning, was away from home, on a visit, when the girl took flight. Immediately on his return, however, he communicated the fullest particulars to the police.”

===Chinese===
“您说，这姑娘年轻、漂亮、无亲无故，又是个外国人，失踪得彻彻底底，就好像大地张开嘴把她吞了下去，”布鲁克小姐说，把戴尔先生方才向她讲述的案情作了个概括。“那么请问您，为何要拖上两天才通知警方？”

“露西·屈尼耶（Lucie Cunier）给德鲁斯太太做文书助手，”戴尔先生答道，“起初她对此事十分镇定，说她确信这姑娘过一两天就会写信来，解释自己那番异乎寻常的举动。德鲁斯少校是她的儿子，就是今早来找我那位先生，姑娘出逃时他正离家在外做客。不过他一回来，便立即把最详尽的情形报告了警方。”

===Original===
“They do not seem to have taken up the case very heartily at Scotland Yard.”

“No, they have as good as dropped it. They advised Major Druce to place the matter in my hands, saying that they considered it a case for private rather than police investigation.”

“I wonder what made them come to that conclusion.”

“I think I can tell you, although the Major seemed quite at a loss on the matter. It seems he had a photograph of the missing girl, which he kept in a drawer of his writing-table. (By-the-way, I think the young man is a good deal ‘gone’ on this Mdlle. Cunier, in spite of his engagement to another lady.) Well, this portrait he naturally thought would be most useful in helping to trace the girl, and he went to his drawer for it, intending to take it with him to Scotland Yard. To his astonishment, however, it was nowhere to be seen, and, although he at once instituted a rigorous search, and questioned his mother and the servants, one and all, on the matter, it was all to no purpose.”

===Chinese===
“苏格兰场那边，似乎并不怎么热心接手这案子。”

“是的，他们等于把它搁下了。他们劝德鲁斯少校把这事交到我手里，说他们认为这案子更适合由私人侦查，而非警方调查。”

“不知他们是怎么得出这个结论的。”

“我想我能告诉您，虽说少校本人对此一头雾水。据说他手头有一张那失踪姑娘的照片，收在书桌的抽屉里。（顺便说一句，我看这年轻人对这位屈尼耶小姐可‘着迷’得很，尽管他已经跟另一位女士订了婚。）他自然觉得这张照片对追查姑娘下落最有用处，便去抽屉里取，打算带它上苏格兰场。谁知却怎么也找不着，他虽然立刻严加搜查，又把母亲和仆人们一个个都盘问了一遍，却全然无济于事。”

===Original===
Loveday thought for a moment.

“Well, of course,” she said presently “that photograph must have been stolen by someone in the house, and, equally of course, that someone must know more on the matter than he or she cares to avow, and, most probably, has some interest in throwing obstacles in the way of tracing the girl. At the same time, however, the fact in no way disproves the possibility that a crime, and a very black one, may underlie the girl’s disappearance.”

“The Major himself appears confident that a crime of some sort has been committed, and he grew very excited and a little mixed in his statements more than once just now.”

“What sort of woman is the Major’s mother?”

===Chinese===
洛芙迪沉思了片刻。

“嗯，当然啦，”她过了一会儿说道，“那张照片一定是被屋里的人偷走的；同样当然的是，那人对此事所知的，一定远比他或她愿意承认的多，十有八九还在追查姑娘下落这件事上有意从中作梗。不过话说回来，这一事实丝毫不曾推翻一种可能：姑娘的失踪背后，也许正藏着一桩罪行，而且是一桩极恶劣的罪行。”

“少校本人似乎深信已经发生了某种罪行，他方才情绪很激动，不止一次把话说得有些颠三倒四。”

“少校的母亲是怎样一位太太？”

===Original===
“Mrs. Druce? She is rather a well-known personage in certain sets. Her husband died about ten years ago, and since his death she has posed as promoter and propagandist of all sorts of benevolent, though occasionally somewhat visionary ideas; theatrical missions, magic-lantern and playing cards missions, societies for providing perpetual music for the sick poor, for supplying cabmen with comforters, and a hundred other similar schemes have in turn occupied her attention. Her house is a rendezvous for faddists of every description. The latest fad, however, seems to have put all others to flight; it is a scheme for alleviating the condition of ‘our sisters in the East,’ so she puts it in her prospectus; in other words a Harem Mission on somewhat similar, but I suppose broader lines than the old-fashioned Zenana Mission. This Harem Mission has gathered about her a number of Turkish and Egyptian potentates resident in or visiting London, and has thus incidentally brought about the engagement of her son, Major Druce, with the Princess Dullah-Veih. This Princess is a beauty and an heiress, and although of Turkish parentage, has been brought up under European influence in Cairo.”

===Chinese===
“德鲁斯太太？在某种圈子里她算是个颇有些名气的人物。她丈夫大约十年前去世，自那以后她便以各种慈善——虽说有时颇为异想天开——的主张的提倡者与宣传者自居：什么戏剧传教团、幻灯片和扑克牌传教团啦，为贫病之人长奏音乐的组织啦，给马车夫供应围巾的团体啦，还有上百个诸如此类的计划，先后占据过她的心思。她的宅子成了形形色色标新立异之人的聚会之所。不过近来这一桩新花样似乎把其余的都挤到一边去了；照她在章程里所写的，这是为‘改善我们东方姐妹的处境’而出力的计划；换句话说，是一个内闱传教团（Harem Mission），路子与旧式的闺阁传教团（Zenana Mission）大致相仿，不过我想规模更大一些。这个内闱传教团在她周围聚拢了一批常驻或来访伦敦的土耳其与埃及权贵，由此也顺带促成了她儿子德鲁斯少校与杜拉-维公主（Princess Dullah-Veih）的婚约。这位公主既是美人，又有遗产，虽是土耳其血统，却在开罗受了欧式教育长大。”

===Original===
“Is anything known of the antecedents of Mdlle. Cunier?”

“Very little. She came to Mrs. Druce from a certain Lady Gwynne, who had brought her to England from an orphanage for the daughters of jewellers and watchmakers at Echallets, in Geneva. Lady Gwynne intended to make her governess to her young children, but when she saw that the girl’s good looks had attracted her husband’s attention, she thought better of it, and suggested to Mrs. Druce that Mademoiselle might be useful to her in conducting her foreign correspondence. Mrs. Druce accordingly engaged the young lady to act as her secretary and amanuensis, and appears, on the whole, to have taken to the girl, and to have been on a pleasant, friendly footing with her. I wonder if the Princess Dullah-Veih was on an equally pleasant footing with her when she saw, as no doubt she did, the attention she received at the Major’s hands.” (Mr. Dyer shrugged his shoulders.) “The Major’s suspicions do not point in that direction, in spite of the fact which I elicited from him by judicious questioning, that the Princess has a violent and jealous temper, and has at times made his life a burden to him. His suspicions centre solely upon a certain Hafiz Cassimi, son of the Turkish-Egyptian banker of that name. It was at the house of these Cassimis that the Major first met the Princess, and he states that she and young Cassimi are like brother and sister to each other. He says that this young man has had the run of his mother’s house and made himself very much at home in it for the past three weeks, ever since, in fact, the Princess came to stay with Mrs. Druce, in order to be initiated into the mysteries of English family life. Hafiz Cassimi, according to the Major’s account, fell desperately in love with the little Swiss girl almost at first sight and pestered her with his attentions, and off and on there appear to have passed hot words between the two young men.”

===Chinese===
“关于屈尼耶小姐的身世，可有知道些什么？”

“所知甚少。她是由一位格温夫人（Lady Gwynne）介绍到德鲁斯太太这里来的。格温夫人把她从日内瓦附近埃沙莱（Echallets）一所专收珠宝匠与钟表匠女儿的孤儿院带到了英国。格温夫人原想让她做自己幼小孩子的家庭教师，可当她发觉姑娘的美貌引得了丈夫的注意，便改了主意，向德鲁斯太太提议，说这位小姐也许能替她处理外文信件，派上用场。德鲁斯太太于是聘了这位年轻女士做她的秘书兼文书助手，整体看来，对这姑娘颇为赏识，彼此相处得融洽友好。我倒想知道，当杜拉-维公主亲眼看见——她必定看见了——姑娘从少校那里领受的殷勤时，她与姑娘之间是否也同样融洽友好。”（戴尔先生耸了耸肩。）“少校的疑心并未指向那一头，尽管我经一番审慎的盘问，从他口中得知公主性情暴烈、好妒，有时简直把他折腾得苦不堪言。他的疑心全然集中在一个哈菲兹·卡西米（Hafiz Cassimi）身上，此人正是那位同名的土耳其-埃及银行家之子。少校正是在这些卡西米家中初次结识公主的，据他说，公主与年轻的卡西米情同兄妹。他又说，这三个星期以来——其实也就是公主为着熟悉英国家庭生活的种种门道，住到德鲁斯太太家里以来——这年轻人一直在他母亲家中出入自如，简直把那儿当成了自己的家。据少校所述，哈菲兹·卡西米几乎对这小个子的瑞士姑娘一见倾心，死缠着献殷勤，于是这两个年轻人之间，断断续续地，似乎也不止一次起过口角。”

===Original===
“One could scarcely expect a princess with Eastern blood in her veins to be a quiet and passive spectator to such a drama of cross-purposes.”

“Scarcely. The Major, perhaps, hardly takes the Princess sufficiently into his reckoning. According to him, young Cassimi is a thoroughgoing Iago, and he begs me to concentrate attention entirely on him. Cassimi, he says, has stolen the photograph. Cassimi has inveigled the girl out of the house on some pretext—perhaps out of the country also, and he suggests that it might be as well to communicate with the police at Cairo, with as little delay as possible.”

“And it hasn’t so much as entered his mind that his Princess might have a hand in such a plot as that!”

“Apparently not. I think I told you that Mademoiselle had taken no luggage—not so much as a handbag—with her. Nothing, beyond her coat and hat, has disappeared from her wardrobe. Her writing-desk, and, in fact, all her boxes and drawers, have been opened and searched, but no letters or papers of any sort have been found that throw any light upon her movements.”

===Chinese===
“血管里流着东方血液的公主，面对这样一出各怀心思的戏，怕是不能指望她做一个安安静静的旁观者吧。”

“怕是不能。少校也许没把公主充分算进他的盘算里。据他说，年轻的卡西米是个彻头彻尾的伊阿古（Iago，《奥赛罗》里搬弄是非的奸人），他求我把注意力全集中在这人身上。他说，是卡西米偷了那张照片；是卡西米用某个借口把姑娘骗出了宅子——说不定还骗出了国；他并且建议，不如尽快与开罗警方取得联系。”

“而他竟压根儿没想到，他的公主殿下说不定也插了一手这样的阴谋！”

“显然没有。我想我跟您说过，这位小姐走时没有带走任何行李——连一只手提包都没有。她的衣柜里，除了外套和帽子，什么也没少。她的写字匣，事实上，她所有的箱子和抽屉都已打开搜查过，却没找到任何一封信件或纸张，能稍稍说明她的去向。”

===Original===
“At what hour in the day is the girl supposed to have left the house?”

“No one can say for certain. It is conjectured that it was some time in the afternoon of the second of this month—a week ago today. It was one of Mrs. Druce’s big reception days, and with a stream of people going and coming, a young lady, more or less, leaving the house would scarcely be noticed.”

“I suppose,” said Loveday, after a moment’s pause, “this Princess Dullah-Veih has something of a history. One does not often get a Turkish princess in London.”

“Yes, she has a history. She is only remotely connected with the present reigning dynasty in Turkey, and I dare say her princess-ship has been made the most of. All the same, however, she has had an altogether exceptional career for an Oriental lady. She was left an orphan at an early age, and was consigned to the guardianship of the elder Cassimi by her relatives. The Cassimis, both father and son, seem to be very advanced and European in their ideas, and by them she was taken to Cairo for her education. About a year ago they ‘brought her out’ in London, where she made the acquaintance of Major Druce. The young man, by-the-way, appears to be rather hotheaded in his lovemaking, for within six weeks of his introduction to her their engagement was announced. No doubt it had Mrs. Druce’s fullest approval, for knowing her son’s extravagant habits and his numerous debts, it must have been patent to her that a rich wife was a necessity to him. The marriage, I believe, was to have taken place this season; but taking into consideration the young man’s ill-advised attentions to the little Swiss girl, and the fervour he is throwing into the search for her, I should say it was exceedingly doubtful whether—”

===Chinese===
“据推测，姑娘是在一天中哪个时辰离开宅子的？”

“谁也说不大准。据推测，是本月二日下午某个时候——也就是一星期前的今天。那天正赶上德鲁斯太太大型会客的日子之一，来来往往的人络绎不绝，多一位少一位年轻小姐走出宅子，几乎不会被人留意。”

“我想，”洛芙迪顿了顿说，“这位杜拉-维公主多少是有些来历的。一位土耳其公主出现在伦敦，可不是常有的事。”

“是的，她确有来历。她与现今土耳其在位的王朝只是沾着一点远亲关系，我猜她这公主的身份也被充分利用了。话虽如此，对一位东方女士而言，她的经历仍可谓独一无二。她早年便成了孤儿，由亲属托付给老卡西米做监护人。卡西米父子似乎思想都很开明、很欧化，他们把她带到开罗受教育。大约一年前，他们在伦敦为她‘引见社交界’，她便在那里结识了德鲁斯少校。顺便提一句，这年轻人在谈情说爱上似乎颇为性急，因为结识她不出六个星期，便宣布了订婚。这场婚事无疑得到了德鲁斯太太的全力赞成，因为深知儿子挥金如土的习气与累累的债务，她必定看得清清楚楚：他非得娶一位阔太太不可。婚礼，我相信，原定于本社交季举行；不过，把这年轻人对那小瑞士姑娘那番失当的殷勤、以及他此刻寻她那股子热火朝天的劲头都算在内，我要说，这婚事能不能成，实在是大可怀疑——”

===Original===
“Major Druce, sir, wishes to see you,” said a clerk at that moment, opening the door leading from the outer office.

“Very good; show him in,” said Mr. Dyer. Then he turned to Loveday.

“Of course I have spoken to him about you, and he is very anxious to take you to his mother’s reception this afternoon, so that you may have a look round and—”

He broke off, having to rise and greet Major Druce, who at that moment entered the room.

===Chinese===
“先生，德鲁斯少校想见您，”这时一个职员说着，推开了通往外间办公室的那扇门。

“很好；请他进来，”戴尔先生说。接着他转向洛芙迪。

“我当然已经跟他说过您了，他急着要带您去参加他母亲今天下午的茶会，好让您四处瞧瞧——”

他话没说完，因为德鲁斯少校在这当口走了进来，他只得起身相迎。

===Original===
He was a tall, handsome young fellow of about seven or eight and twenty, “well turned out” from head to foot, moustache waxed, orchid in buttonhole, light kid gloves, and patent leather boots. There was assuredly nothing in his appearance to substantiate his statement to Mr. Dyer that he “hadn’t slept a wink all night, that in fact another twenty-four hours of this terrible suspense would send him into his grave.”

Mr. Dyer introduced Miss Brooke, and she expressed her sympathy with him on the painful matter that was filling his thoughts.

“It is very good of you, I’m sure,” he replied, in a slow, soft drawl, not unpleasant to listen to. “My mother receives this afternoon from half past four to half past six, and I shall be very glad if you will allow me to introduce you to the inside of our house, and to the very ill-looking set that we have somehow managed to gather about us.”

===Chinese===
他是个高挑英俊的年轻人，二十七八岁模样，从头到脚“收拾得体体面面”：八字胡上了蜡，钮孔里插一朵兰花，戴着浅色小羊皮手套，脚蹬漆皮靴。论外表，实在看不出丝毫足以印证他向戴尔先生所说的话——他“整夜不曾合眼，事实上，再这样煎熬二十四小时，就要了他的命了”。

戴尔先生介绍了布鲁克小姐，她便就那桩占满他心思的痛心事，向他表达了同情。

“您真是太好了，”他答道，语调慢悠悠、软绵绵的，拖着长腔，倒也不难听。“家母今天下午四点半到六点会客，若蒙您允许，我很乐意把您引进我们家屋里瞧瞧，也瞧瞧我们不知怎的竟招惹到身边的这帮模样不善的家伙。”

===Original===
“The ill-looking set?”

“Yes; Jews, Turks, heretics and infidels—all there. And they’re on the increase too, that’s the worst of it. Every week a fresh importation from Cairo.”

“Ah, Mrs. Druce is a large-hearted, benevolent woman,” interposed Mr. Dyer; “all nationalities gather within her walls.”

“Was your mother a large-hearted, benevolent woman?” said the young man, turning upon him. “No! well then, thank Providence that she wasn’t; and admit that you know nothing at all on the matter. Miss Brooke,” he continued, turning to Loveday, “I’ve brought round my hansom for you; it’s nearly half past four now, and it’s a good twenty minutes’ drive from here to Portland Place. If you’re ready, I’m at your service.”

===Chinese===
“模样不善的家伙？”

“是的；犹太人、土耳其人、异端分子、不信教的人——全在那儿。而且还在与日俱增，这才是最糟的。每个礼拜都从开罗新进一批货。”

“啊，德鲁斯太太是位心胸宽广、热心慈善的女士，”戴尔先生插话道，“四海之人皆聚于她门下。”

“您母亲是位心胸宽广、热心慈善的女士吗？”年轻人转过头来冲着他说。“不是吧！那么，谢天谢地她不是；也请您承认，这事您压根儿一窍不通。布鲁克小姐，”他接着转向洛芙迪，“我把我的双轮马车绕过来了；这会儿快四点半了，从这儿到波特兰广场（Portland Place）足足要赶二十分钟的车。您若准备好了，听凭差遣。”

===Original===
Major Druce’s hansom was, like himself, in all respects “well turned out,” and the India rubber tires round its wheels allowed an easy flow of conversation to be kept up during the twenty minutes’ drive from Lynch Court to Portland Place.

The Major led off the talk in frank and easy fashion.

“My mother,” he said, “prides herself on being cosmopolitan in her tastes, and just now we are very cosmopolitan indeed. Even our servants represent divers nationalities; the butler is French, the two footmen Italians, the maids, I believe, are some of them German, some Irish; and I’ve no doubt if you penetrated to the kitchen-quarters, you’d find the staff there composed in part of Scandinavians, in part of South Sea Islanders. The other quarters of the globe you will find fully represented in the drawing-room.”

===Chinese===
德鲁斯少校的双轮马车，和他本人一样，处处“收拾得体体面面”，轮子上的橡胶轮胎使车行平稳，于是从林奇大院到波特兰广场这二十分钟的车程里，两人可以轻松地攀谈下去。

少校爽朗随和地挑起了话头。

“家母，”他说，“以自己趣味博通万国为傲，眼下我们家也确实万国来朝得很。连我们的仆人都五方杂处：男管家是法国人，两个男仆是意大利人，女佣嘛，我想有的是德国人，有的是爱尔兰人；我毫不怀疑，您若深入到厨房那一带，准会发现那里的差役一部分是斯堪的纳维亚人，一部分是南太平洋岛民。其余各大洲的人，您可在客厅里尽览无遗。”

===Original===
Loveday had a direct question to ask.

“Are you certain that Mdlle. Cunier had no friends in England?” she said.

“Positive. She hadn’t a friend in the world outside my mother’s four walls, poor child! She told me more than once that she was ‘seule sur la terre.’” He broke off for a moment, as if overcome by a sad memory, then added: “But I’ll put a bullet into him, take my word for it, if she isn’t found within another twenty-four hours. Personally I should prefer settling the brute in that fashion to handing him over to the police.”

His face flushed a deep red, there came a sudden flash to his eye, but for all that, his voice was as soft and slow and unemotional, as though he were talking of nothing more serious than bringing down a partridge.

===Chinese===
洛芙迪有一个直截了当的问题要问。

“您能肯定屈尼耶小姐在英国没有任何朋友吗？”她说。

“千真万确。除了我母亲这四面墙之外，她在世上再没有一个朋友，可怜的孩子！她不止一次跟我说，她‘孑然一身’（*seule sur la terre*）。”他顿了顿，像被一段伤心的回忆攫住了，又接着说：“可要是再过二十四小时还找不着她，您信我的话，我非给他一枪不可。就我个人而言，我倒宁可那样了结这畜生，也不愿把他交给警察。”

他脸上泛起一片深红，眼里倏地闪过一道光，可纵然如此，他的嗓音仍那么软、那么慢、那么不带感情，仿佛所谈的无非是打下了一只鹧鸪之类的小事。

===Original===
There fell a brief pause; then Loveday asked another question.

“Is Mademoiselle Catholic or Protestant, can you tell me?”

The Major thought for a moment, then replied:

“’Pon my word, I don’t know. She used sometimes to attend a little charge in South Savile Street—I’ve walked with her occasionally to the church door—but I couldn’t for the life of me say whether it was a Catholic, Protestant, or Pagan place of worship. But—but you don’t think those confounded priests have—”

“Here, we are in Portland place,” interrupted Loveday. “Mrs. Druce’s rooms are already full, to judge from that long line of carriages!”

===Chinese===
短暂地静了一阵；接着洛芙迪又问了一个问题。

“这位小姐信的是天主教还是新教，您能告诉我吗？”

少校想了想，答道：

“凭良心说，我不知道。她从前偶尔会去南萨维尔街（South Savile Street）一处小小的礼拜堂——我有几回陪她走到教堂门口——可我说什么也讲不清那究竟是天主教、新教，还是异教徒做礼拜的地方。可——可您总不至于以为，是那些该死的教士——”

“到了，我们到波特兰广场了，”洛芙迪打断他。“瞧那一长串马车，德鲁斯太太的会客厅怕是已经挤满了！”

===Original===
“Miss Brooke,” said the Major suddenly, bethinking himself of his responsibilities, “how am I to introduce you? what role will you take up this afternoon? Pose as a faddist of some sort, if you want to win my mother’s heart. What do you say to having started a grand scheme for supplying Hottentots and Kaffirs with eyeglasses? My mother would swear eternal friendship with you at once.”

“Don’t introduce me at all at first,” answered Loveday. “Get me into some quiet corner, where I can see without being seen. Later on in the afternoon, when I have had time to look round a little, I’ll tell you whether it will be necessary to introduce me or not.”

“It will be a mob this afternoon, and no mistake,” said Major Druce, as side by side, they entered the house. “Do you hear that fizzing and clucking just behind us? That’s Arabic; you’ll get it in whiffs between gusts of French and German all the afternoon. The Egyptian contingent seems to be in full force today. I don’t see any Choctaw Indians, but no doubt they’ll send their representatives later on. Come in at this side door, and we’ll work our way round to that big palm. My mother is sure to be at the principal doorway.”

===Chinese===
“布鲁克小姐，”少校猛地想起自己的职责，说道，“我该怎样介绍您呢？今天下午您要扮个什么角色？您要赢得家母的心，不妨装作某种赶时髦的怪人。您说您发起了一项给霍屯督人（Hottentots）和卡菲尔人（Kaffirs）配眼镜的宏图大业，怎么样？家母准会当即与您结为莫逆之交。”

“起初压根儿别介绍我，”洛芙迪答道。“把我领到哪个安静的角落，让我能看见别人、别人看不见我。下午晚些时候，等我抽空四下打量过了，再告诉您有没有必要介绍我。”

“今天下午准是人山人海，错不了，”德鲁斯少校说着，与她并肩走进宅子。“您听见我们身后那咝咝啦啦、咕咕咯咯的声音了吗？那是阿拉伯语；整个下午您都会在一阵阵法语和德语的间隙里，一阵阵地闻到它。今天埃及那一支人马似乎倾巢出动了。我没瞧见什么乔克托印第安人（Choctaw Indians），不过他们晚些时候想必会派代表来。从这个侧门进去，咱们绕到那棵大棕榈那儿。家母准在正门口。”

===Original===
The drawing rooms were packed from end to end, and Major Druce’s progress, as he headed Loveday through the crowd, was impeded by handshaking and the interchange of civilities with his mother’s guests.

Eventually the big palm standing in a Chinese cistern was reached, and there, half screened from view by its graceful branches, he placed a chair for Miss Brooke.

From this quiet nook, as now and again the crowd parted, Loveday could command a fair view of both drawing-rooms.

===Chinese===
两间客厅从头到尾挤得水泄不通，德鲁斯少校领着洛芙迪穿过人群，一路被握手和与他母亲宾客之间的寒暄客套绊住脚步。

终于走到了那只中国瓷缸里立着的大棕榈跟前，在那里，借着它优雅枝叶半遮半掩，他为布鲁克小姐安了一把椅子。

从这个安静的角落，每当人群偶尔散开，洛芙迪便能把两间客厅尽收眼底。

===Original===
“Don’t attract attention to me by standing at my elbow,” she whispered to the Major.

He answered her whisper with another.

“There’s the Beast—Iago, I mean,” he said; “do you see him? He’s standing talking to that fair, handsome woman in pale green, with a picture hat. She’s Lady Gwynne. And there’s my mother, and there’s Dolly—the Princess I mean—alone on the sofa. Ah! you can’t see her now for the crowd. Yes, I’ll go, but if you want me, just nod to me and I shall understand.”

===Chinese===
“别站在我身边招人注意，”她低声对少校说。

他也压低声音回答她。

“那畜生就在那儿——我是说伊阿古，”他说，“您瞧见了吗？他正站着跟那个穿淡绿衣裳、漂亮丰满的金发太太说话，戴着一顶阔沿花式帽。那是格温夫人。那边是家母，还有多莉（Dolly）——我是说公主——独自坐在沙发上。啊！这会儿人挡着，您瞧不见她了。是，我走，可您要找我，只要冲我点点头，我就明白。”

===Original===
If was easy to see what had brought such a fashionable crowd to Mrs. Druce’s rooms that afternoon. Every caller, as soon as she had shaken hands with the hostess, passed on to the Princess’s sofa, and there waited patiently till opportunity presented itself for an introduction to her Eastern Highness.

Loveday found it impossible to get more than the merest glimpse of her, and so transferred her attention to Mr. Hafiz Cassimi, who had been referred to in such unceremonious language by Major Druce.

He was a swarthy, well-featured man, with bold, black eyes, and lips that had the habit of parting now and again, not to smile, but as if for no other purpose than to show a double row of gleaming white teeth. The European dress he wore seemed to accord ill with the man; and Loveday could fancy that those black eyes and that double row of white teeth would have shown to better advantage beneath a turban or a fez cap. From Cassimi, her eye wandered to Mrs. Druce—a tall, stout woman, dressed in black velvet, and with hair mounted high on her head, that had the appearance of being either bleached or powdered. She gave Loveday the impression of being that essentially modern product of modern society—the woman who combines in one person the hardworking philanthropist with the hardworking woman of fashion. As arrivals began to slacken, she left her post near the door and began to make the round of the room. From snatches of talk that came to her where she sat, Loveday could gather that with one hand, as it were, this energetic lady was organizing a grand charity concert, and with the other pushing the interests of a big ball that was shortly to be given by the officers of her son’s regiment.

===Chinese===
那天下午，究竟是什么把这等时髦的人群引到德鲁斯太太的会客厅来的，一目了然。每位来客一与女主人握过手，便移步到公主的沙发前，在那里耐心等候，直到有机会被引见给这位东方殿下。

洛芙迪发现，连多看她一眼都难，便把注意力转到哈菲兹·卡西米先生身上——方才德鲁斯少校正是用那样毫不客气的措辞提到他的。

他是个肤色黝黑、五官端正的男子，一双黑眼睛大而放肆，嘴唇时不时地咧开，可不是为了微笑，倒像是专为露出一整排光洁雪白的牙齿。他身上那身欧式装束，似乎与这人格格不入；洛芙迪不由得想象，那双黑眼睛、那一排白牙齿，若是衬在一顶头巾或一顶土耳其毡帽之下，定然更加相得益彰。从卡西米身上，她的目光又游移到德鲁斯太太身上——一位身材高大的壮硕夫人，一袭黑丝绒，头发高高盘在头顶，看上去不是褪了色，便是扑了粉。她给洛芙迪的印象，恰是现代社会所孕育的那种典型产物——一人之身，兼具了勤勉的慈善家与忙碌的时髦名媛两种身份。来客渐稀之后，她便离开门口的岗位，开始绕厅周旋。从断断续续飘到她耳边的闲谈里，洛芙迪听得出，这位精力充沛的夫人，一只手仿佛在筹办一场盛大的慈善音乐会，另一只手又在为一场即将由她儿子所在团军官们举办的大型舞会张罗着。

===Original===
It was a hot June day. In spite of closed blinds and open windows, the rooms were stifling to a degree. The butler, a small dark, slight Frenchman, made his way through the throng to a window at Loveday’s right hand, to see if a little more air could be admitted.

Major Druce followed on his heels to Loveday’s side.

“Will you come into the next room and have some tea?” he asked; “I’m sure you must feel nearly suffocated here.” He broke off, then added in a lower tone: “I hope you have kept your eyes on the Beast. Did you ever in your life see a more repulsive-looking animal?”

Loveday took his questions in their order.

“No tea, thank you,” she said, “but I shall be glad if you will tell your butler to bring me a glass of water—there he is, at your elbow. Yes, off and on I have been studying Mr. Cassimi, and I must admit I do not like his smileless smile.”

===Chinese===
那是个炎热的六月天。尽管百叶窗都合着、窗户都开着，屋里仍闷热到了极点。那位男管家，一个身材瘦小、皮肤黝黑的法国人，挤过人群走到洛芙迪右边的一扇窗前，看看能不能再多放进一点空气。

德鲁斯少校紧随其后，来到洛芙迪身边。

“您到隔壁屋里喝杯茶好吗？”他问，“您在这儿一定快闷坏了吧。”他顿了顿，又压低声音添了一句：“我希望您一直盯着那畜生。您这辈子见过比这模样更叫人恶心的动物吗？”

洛芙迪按顺序回答他的问题。

“不喝茶，谢谢，”她说，“不过麻烦您吩咐管家给我端杯水来——他就在您手边。是，断断续续地，我一直在端详卡西米先生，我不得不承认，我不喜欢他那种不带笑意的笑。”

===Original===
The butler brought the water. The Major, much to his annoyance, was seized upon simultaneously by two ladies, one eager to know if any tidings had been received of Mdlle. Cunier, the other anxious to learn if a distinguished president to the Harem Mission had been decided upon.

Soon after six the rooms began to thin somewhat, and presentations to the Princess ceasing, Loveday was able to get a full view of her.

She presented a striking picture, seated, half-reclining, on a sofa, with two white-robed, dark-skinned Egyptian maidens standing behind it. A more unfortunate sobriquet than “Dolly” could scarcely have been found by the Major for this Oriental beauty, with her olive complexion, her flashing eyes and extravagant richness of attire.

===Chinese===
管家端来了水。少校却被两位太太同时拽住了，颇为懊恼——一位急着打听屈尼耶小姐可有下落，另一位急于知道内闱传教团那位显赫的主席人选是否已经定了下来。

六点过后不久，屋里的人渐渐稀疏起来，向公主的引见也停止了，洛芙迪这才得以把她看个周全。

她坐在沙发上，半倚半躺，身后立着两个一身白袍、肤色黝黑的埃及少女，这画面着实夺目。对这样一位东方美人——橄榄色的肌肤，顾盼生辉的眼睛，华贵到近乎铺张的衣饰——少校竟给她找了“多莉”这么个再不合适不过的绰号。

===Original===
“‘Queen of Sheba’ would be far more appropriate,” thought Loveday. “She turns the commonplace sofa into a throne, and, I should say, makes every one of those ladies feel as if she ought to have donned court dress and plumes for the occasion.”

It was difficult for her, from where she sat, to follow the details of the Princess’s dress. She could only see that a quantity of soft orange-tinted silk was wound about the upper part of her arms and fell from her shoulders like drooping wings, and that here and there jewels flashed out from its folds. Her thick black hair was loosely knotted, and kept in its place by jeweled pins and a bandeau of pearls; and similar bandeaus adorned her slender throat and wrists.

“Are you lost in admiration?” said the Major, once more at her elbow, in a slightly sarcastic tone. “That sort of thing is very taking and effective at first, but after a time—”

===Chinese===
“‘示巴女王’（Queen of Sheba）贴切得多，”洛芙迪心想。“她把那张平平无奇的沙发坐成了宝座，而且我要说，她让在场的每一位太太都觉得自己仿佛该穿上朝服、戴上羽饰才配得上这场合。”

从她坐的地方，要细看公主衣饰的细节颇不容易。她只看得见，一匹柔软的橘色丝绸缠绕在公主上臂，自双肩披垂而下，宛如下垂的双翼，褶皱间处处有珠宝熠熠闪动。她那一头浓密的黑发松松地挽了个髻，用宝石发针和一道珍珠额带固定着；同样的珠带也缀在她纤秀的颈项和手腕上。

“您瞧得出了神吧？”少校又到了她身边，语调略带讥讽。“这一套初看确实很抓人、很有效，可是日子一长——”

===Original===
He did not finish his sentence, shrugged his shoulders and walked away. Half-past six chimed from a small clock on a bracket. Carriage after carriage was rolling away from the door now, and progress on the stairs was rendered difficult by a descending crowd.

A quarter to seven struck, the last handshaking had been gone through, and Mrs. Druce, looking hot and tired, had sunk into a chair at the Princess’s right hand, bending slightly forward to render conversation with her easy.

On the Princess’s left hand, Lady Gwynne had taken a chair, and sat in converse with Hafiz Cassimi, who stood beside her.

Evidently these four were on very easy and intimate terms with each other. Lady Gwynne had tossed her big picture hat on a chair at her left hand, and was fanning herself with a palm-leaf. Mrs. Druce, beckoning to the butler, desired him to bring them some claret-cup from the refreshment-room.

===Chinese===
他没有把话说完，耸了耸肩走开了。壁架上的一只小钟敲响了六点半。此时一辆接一辆的马车正从门口辚辚驶离，楼梯上的人群鱼贯而下，挤得难以前行。

差一刻七点，最后的握手也告完毕。德鲁斯太太面带倦色、热得满脸通红，在公主右手边的一把椅子上坐下，身子微微前倾，好方便与她交谈。

公主左手边，格温夫人也坐了下来，正与立在她身旁的哈菲兹·卡西米攀谈。

这四人彼此显然十分熟稔亲昵。格温夫人已把她那顶阔沿花式帽随手扔在左手边的一把椅子上，正用一柄棕榈叶扇着风。德鲁斯太太招手把管家叫来，吩咐他去茶点间取些红酒潘趣酒来。

===Original===
No one seemed to observe Loveday seated still in her nook beside the big palm.

She signaled to the Major, who stood looking discontentedly from one of the windows.

“That is a most interesting group,” she said; “now, if you like, you may introduce me to your mother.”

“Oh, with pleasure—under what name?” he asked.

“Under my own,” she answered, “and please be very distinct in pronouncing it, raise your voice slightly so that every one of those persons may hear it. And then, please add my profession, and say I am here at your request to investigate the circumstances connected with Mdlle. Cunier’s disappearance.”

===Chinese===
似乎谁也没有留意洛芙迪仍坐在大棕榈旁那个角落里。

她向少校使了个眼色——他正一脸不痛快地站在一扇窗前。

“那是个顶有意思的一伙人，”她说，“现在，您要是愿意，可以把我介绍给您母亲了。”

“哦，乐意之至——用什么名字？”他问。

“用我自己的名字，”她答道，“并且请您咬字格外清晰，稍稍提高嗓音，让那几个人个个都听见。然后，请补上我的职业，说我此刻是应您之请，来调查与屈尼耶小姐失踪一事相关的情形的。”

===Original===
Major Druce looked astounded.

“But—but,” he stammered, “have you seen anything—found out anything? If not, don’t you think it will be better to preserve your incognita a little longer.”

“Don’t stop to ask questions,” said Loveday sharply; “now, this very minute, do what I ask you, or the opportunity will be gone.”

The Major without further demur, escorted Loveday across the room. The conversation between the four intimate friends had now become general and animated, and he had to wait for a minute or so before he could get an opportunity to speak to his mother.

During that minute Loveday stood a little in his rear, with Lady Gwynne and Cassimi at her right hand.

===Chinese===
德鲁斯少校一脸惊愕。

“可——可是，”他结结巴巴地说，“您是瞧出了什么——查出了什么吗？若是还没有，您不觉得暂且再隐姓埋名一阵子更好吗？”

“别停下来问东问西，”洛芙迪厉声说，“就现在，这会儿，照我说的做，不然机会就溜走了。”

少校不再多辩，护着洛芙迪穿过客厅。那四位密友间的谈话此刻已扩展成一片热热闹闹的众人交谈，他只好等了一分钟上下，才寻得机会插话与母亲搭腔。

在那一分钟里，洛芙迪站在他稍后的位置，格温夫人和卡西米就在她右手边。

===Original===
“I want to introduce this lady to you,” said the Major, when a pause in the talk gave him his opportunity. “This is Miss Loveday Brooke, a lady detective, and she is here at my request to investigate the circumstances connected with the disappearance of Mdlle. Cunier.”

He said the words slowly and distinctly.

“There!” he said to himself complacently, as he ended; “if I had been reading the lessons in church, I couldn’t have been more emphatic.”

A blank silence for a moment fell upon the group, and even the butler, just then entering with the claret-cup, came to a standstill at the door.

Then, simultaneously, a glance flashed from Mrs. Druce to Lady Gwynne, from Lady Gwynne to Mrs. Druce, and then, also simultaneously, the eyes of both ladies rested, though only for an instant, on the big picture hat lying on the chair.

===Chinese===
“我想给您介绍这位女士，”少校趁谈话的一个停顿抓住了机会。“这位是洛芙迪·布鲁克小姐，一位女侦探，她是应我之请来此调查屈尼耶小姐失踪一事的。”

他把这几句话说得又慢又清楚。

“好啦！”他结束时自鸣得意地想，“就算我在教堂里诵读经文，也不能比这更掷地有声了。”

一时之间，这群人陷入了一阵死寂，就连正端着红酒潘趣酒进来的管家，也在门口顿住了脚。

紧接着，一道目光同时从德鲁斯太太闪向格温夫人，又从格温夫人闪向德鲁斯太太，而后，同样是同时，两位太太的目光——虽只一瞬——都落在椅子上那顶阔沿花式帽上。

===Original===
Lady Gwynne started to her feet and seized her hat, adjusting it without so much as a glance at a mirror.

“I must go at once; this very minute,” she said. “I promised Charlie I would back soon after six, and now it is past seven. Mr. Cassimi, will you take me down to my carriage?” And with the most hurried of leave-takings to the Princess and her hostess, the lady swept out of the room, followed by Mr. Cassimi.

The butler still standing at the door, drew back to allow the lady to pass, and then, claret-cup and all, followed her out of the room.

Mrs. Druce drew a long breath and bowed formally to Loveday.

“I was a little taken by surprise,” she began—

===Chinese===
格温夫人霍地起身，一把抓过帽子戴上，连照一照镜子都顾不上。

“我得马上走；这会儿就走，”她说。“我答应过查理（Charlie）六点刚过就回去，可现在已经过了七点。卡西米先生，您送我下去上车好吗？”这位夫人向公主和女主人匆匆道了别，便一阵风似地出了客厅，卡西米先生紧随其后。

那管家仍站在门口，退后一步让夫人过去，而后也顾不上那杯红酒潘趣酒了，跟着她出了客厅。

德鲁斯太太长长吸了一口气，向洛芙迪正式地鞠了一躬。

“我有点猝不及防，”她开口道——

===Original===
But here the Princess rose suddenly from the sofa.

“*Moi, je suis fatiguée*,” she said in excellent French to Mrs. Druce, and she too swept out of the room, throwing, as she passed, what seemed to Loveday a slightly scornful glance towards the Major.

Her two attendants, one carrying her fan, and the other her reclining cushions, followed.

Mrs. Druce again turned to Loveday.

“Yes, I confess I was taken a little by surprise,” she said, her manner thawing slightly. “I am not accustomed to the presence of detectives in my house; but now tell me what do you propose doing: how do you mean to begin your investigations—by going over the house and looking in all the corners, or by cross-questioning the servants? Forgive my asking, but really I am quite at a loss; I haven’t the remotest idea how such investigations are generally conducted.”

===Chinese===
可这时公主忽然从沙发上站了起来。

“*Moi, je suis fatiguée*（我累了），”她用极漂亮的法语对德鲁斯太太说，于是她也一阵风似地出了客厅，经过时朝少校投去一瞥——在洛芙迪看来，那目光略带轻蔑。

她的两个侍女跟在后面，一个替她拿着扇子，一个捧着她半躺时用的靠垫。

德鲁斯太太又转向洛芙迪。

“是的，我承认我有点猝不及防，”她说，态度稍稍和缓了些。“我不习惯家里出现侦探；不过现在请告诉我，您打算怎么做：您预备从何着手——是把整幢宅子搜一遍、各处角落都翻翻，还是盘问仆人？恕我多嘴，可我实在摸不着头脑，这类调查通常是怎么个做法，我一点概念也没有。”

===Original===
“I do not propose to do much in the way of investigation tonight,” answered Loveday as formally as she had been addressed, “for I have very important business to transact before eight o’clock this evening. I shall ask you to allow me to see Mdlle. Cunier’s room—ten minutes there will be sufficient—after that, I do not think I need further trouble you.”

“Certainly; by all means,” answered Mrs. Druce; “you’ll find the room exactly as Lucie left it, nothing has been disturbed.”

She turned to the butler, who had by this time returned and stood presenting the claret-cup, and, in French, desired him to summon her maid, and tell her to show Miss Brooke to Mdlle. Cunier’s room.

The ten minutes that Loveday had said would suffice for her survey of this room extended themselves to fifteen, but the extra five minutes assuredly were not expended by her in the investigation of drawers and boxes. The maid, a pleasant, well-spoken young woman, jingled her keys, and opened every lock, and seemed not at all disinclined to enter into the light gossip that Loveday contrived to set going.

She answered freely a variety of questions that Loveday put to her respecting Mademoiselle and her general habits, and from Mademoiselle, the talk drifted to other members of Mrs. Druce’s household.

===Chinese===
“今晚我并不打算做多少调查，”洛芙迪答道，语气同对方一样正式，“因为今晚八点之前，我有要事要办。我想请您允许我去看一看屈尼耶小姐的房间——那里十分钟便够了——之后，我想就不必再叨扰您了。”

“当然；尽管去看，”德鲁斯太太答道，“那房间还是露西走时的原样，什么都没动过。”

她转向管家——此时他已回来，正端着那杯红酒潘趣酒——用法语吩咐他去叫她的贴身女仆，让女仆带布鲁克小姐去屈尼耶小姐的房间。

洛芙迪说查看这房间十分钟便够，结果延长到了十五分钟，可多出来的那五分钟，她绝不是花在搜查抽屉和箱子上。那女仆是个模样讨喜、谈吐斯文的年轻女子，把钥匙弄得叮当响，每一把锁都打开，似乎颇为乐意与洛芙迪搭上她设法引出来的那点闲话。

洛芙迪向她提了一连串关于这位小姐及其日常起居的问题，她都一一爽快作答，话题从小姐身上，又漫到了德鲁斯太太家里其他成员。

===Original===
If Loveday had, as she had stated, important business to transact that evening, she certainly set about it in a strange fashion.

After she quitted Mademoiselle’s room, she went straight out of the house, without leaving a message of any sort for either Mrs. or Major Druce. She walked the length of Portland Place in leisurely fashion, and then, having first ascertained that her movements were not being watched, she called a hansom, and desired the man to drive her to Madame Céline’s, a fashionable milliner’s in Old Bond Street.

At Madame Céline’s she spent close upon half-an-hour, giving many and minute directions for the making of a hat, which assuredly, when finished, would compare with nothing in the way of millinery that she had ever before put upon her head.

From Madame Céline’s the hansom conveyed her to an undertaker’s shop, at the corner of South Savile Street, and here she spent a brief ten minutes in conversation with the undertaker himself in his little back parlour.

From the undertaker’s she drove home to her rooms in Gower Street, and then, before she divested herself of hat and coat, she wrote a brief note to Major Druce, requesting him to meet her on the following morning at Eglacé’s, the confectioner’s, in South Savile Street, at nine o’clock punctually.

This note she committed to the charge of the cabdriver, desiring him to deliver it at Portland Place on his way back to his stand.

===Chinese===
倘若洛芙迪当真如她所说，当晚有要事要办，那么她着手此事的方式，着实古怪。

离开小姐的房间后，她径直出了宅子，没有给德鲁斯太太或德鲁斯少校留下任何口信。她不紧不慢地走完波特兰广场整条街，在确信自己并未被人盯梢之后，才叫了一辆双轮马车，吩咐车夫送她去老庞德街（Old Bond Street）一家时髦的女帽店——赛琳夫人（Madame Céline）帽店。

在赛琳夫人店里，她待了将近半个钟头，仔仔细细地交代了一顶帽子的制作，吩咐得又多又细；这帽子做成之后，可以肯定，与她往日戴过的任何一顶帽子都全然不似。

出了赛琳夫人店，马车把她载到南萨维尔街拐角上一家殡仪馆，她在这儿花了短短十分钟，在后面的小起坐间里，跟老板本人交谈了一番。

出了殡仪馆，她乘车回到高尔街（Gower Street）自己的住处，还没脱下帽子和外套，就给德鲁斯少校写了封短信，约他次晨九点整，在南萨维尔街那家名叫埃格拉斯（Eglacé）的糖果点心店与她碰头。

她把这封信交托给车夫，请他在回车场的路上，顺道送到波特兰广场。

===Original===
“They’ve queer ways of doing things—these people!” said the Major, as he opened and read the note. “Suppose I must keep the appointment though, confound it. I can’t see that she can possibly have found out anything by just sitting still in a corner for a couple of hours! And I’m confident she didn’t give that beast Cassimi one quarter the attention she bestowed on other people.”

In spite of his grumbling, however, the Major kept his appointment, and nine o’clock the next morning saw him shaking hands with Miss Brooke on Eglacé’s doorstep.

“Dismiss your hansom,” she said to him. “I only want you to come a few doors down the street, to the French Protestant church, to which you have sometimes escorted Mdlle. Cunier.”

===Chinese===
“这些人的行事路数，真是古怪！”少校拆信读道。“不过我想，这约我还是得赴，真见鬼。我就不明白了，她不过是在角落里干坐了两个钟头，能查出个什么名堂来！而且我敢说，她瞧那畜生卡西米的工夫，连她花在别人身上的四分之一都不到。”

尽管满腹牢骚，少校还是赴了约。第二天早晨九点，他已在埃格拉斯店门口与布鲁克小姐握手了。

“把您的马车打发了吧，”她对他说。“我只要您顺这条街过去几扇门，到那座法国新教教堂去——您从前偶尔送屈尼耶小姐去的，就是那儿。”

===Original===
At the church door Loveday paused a moment.

“Before we enter,” she said, “I want you to promise that whatever you may see going on there—however greatly you may be surprised—you will make no disturbance, not so much as open your lips till we come out.”

The Major, not a little bewildered, gave the required promise; and, side by side, the two entered the church.

It was little more than a big room; at the farther end, in the middle of the nave, stood the pulpit, and immediately behind this was a low platform, enclosed by a brass rail.

Behind this brass rail, in black Geneva gown, stood the pastor of the church, and before him, on cushions, kneeled two persons, a man and a woman.

===Chinese===
到了教堂门口，洛芙迪停了片刻。

“进去之前，”她说，“我要您答应，无论您看见里头在做什么——无论您多么吃惊——您都不许声张，在我们出来之前，连嘴都不许张一下。”

少校一头雾水，还是作了所要求的承诺；于是两人并肩走进了教堂。

这教堂不过比一间大屋子稍大一点；在远远那一头，正中本堂的位置立着讲坛，讲坛正后方是一处低矮的平台，围着一道铜栏杆。

铜栏杆后面，那位本堂牧师一身黑色日内瓦长袍站着，他面前的垫子上，跪着两个人，一男一女。

===Original===
These two persons and an old man, the verger, formed the whole of the congregation. The position of the church, amid shops and narrow backyards, had necessitated the filling in of every one of its windows with stained glass; it was, consequently, so dim that, coming in from the outside glare of sunlight, the Major found it difficult to make out what was going on at the farther end.

The verger came forward and offered to show them to a seat. Loveday shook her head—they would be leaving in a minute, she said, and would prefer standing where they were.

The Major began to take in the situation.

“Why they’re being married!” he said in a loud whisper. “What on earth have you brought me in here for?”

Loveday laid her finger on her lips and frowned severely at him.

===Chinese===
这两个人，再加上一个管堂的老头儿——教堂的司事——便是全体会众了。教堂夹在店铺和狭窄的后院之间，只好把每一扇窗都镶上彩色玻璃；因此，从外头明晃晃的日光里走进来，少校一时很难看清远远那一头在做什么。

那司事走上前来，要引他们入座。洛芙迪摇摇头——她说他们一会儿就走，宁愿就地站着。

少校渐渐看出了情形。

“怎么，他们在结婚！”他压着嗓子却相当响地说。“您到底把我带到这儿来干什么？”

洛芙迪把手指按在唇上，严厉地朝他皱起眉头。

===Original===
The marriage service came to an end, the pastor extended his black-gowned arms like the wings of a bat and pronounced the benediction; the man and woman rose from their knees and proceeded to follow him into the vestry.

The woman was neatly dressed in a long dove-coloured travelling cloak. She wore a large hat, from which fell a white gossamer veil that completely hid her face from view. The man was small, dark and slight, and as he passed on to the vestry beside his bride, the Major at once identified him as his mother’s butler.

“Why, that’s Lebrun!” he said in a still louder whisper than before. “Why, in the name of all that’s wonderful, have you brought me here to see that fellow married?”

“You’d better come outside if you can’t keep quiet,” said Loveday severely, and leading the way out of the church as she spoke.

Outside, South Savile Street was busy with early morning traffic.

===Chinese===
婚礼仪式结束了，牧师伸出他那黑袍裹着的双臂，宛如蝙蝠展翅，宣告祝福；那一男一女从垫子上起身，随他走进更衣室。

那女子衣着整洁，披着一件长长的鸽灰色旅行斗篷。她戴着一顶大帽子，垂下一方洁白的轻纱面幕，把脸遮得严严实实。那男子身材瘦小、皮肤黝黑，他随着新娘往更衣室走去时，少校一眼认出，正是他家的男管家。

“哎呀，那是勒布伦（Lebrun）！”他用比方才还响的耳语说。“看在老天爷份上，您到底为什么把我带来看这家伙结婚？”

“您要是安静不下来，还是到外头去吧，”洛芙迪严厉地说，一边引路走出教堂。

外面，南萨维尔街上满是清早的车马人流。

===Original===
“Let us go back to Eglacé’s,” said Loveday, “and have some coffee. I will explain to you there all you are wishing to know.”

But before the coffee could be brought to them, the Major had asked at least a dozen questions.

Loveday put them all on one side.

“All in good time,” she said. “You are leaving out the most important question of all. Have you no curiosity to know who was the bride that Lebrun has chosen?”

“I don’t suppose it concerns me in the slightest degree,” he answered indifferently; “but since you wish me to ask the question—Who was she?”

“Lucie Cunier, lately your mother’s amanuensis.”

===Chinese===
“咱们回埃格拉斯那儿去，”洛芙迪说，“喝杯咖啡。在那儿，您想知道的，我一并解释给您听。”

可咖啡还没端上来，少校已问了一连串问题，少说也有一打。

洛芙迪把它们统统搁在一边。

“且慢，”她说，“您偏偏漏了最重要的一个问题。您就不好奇，勒布伦选的新娘是谁吗？”

“我想这事跟我八竿子打不着，”他漫不经心地答道，“可既然您要我问——她是谁？”

“露西·屈尼耶，令堂从前的文书助手。”

===Original===
“The ———!” cried the Major, jumping to his feet and uttering an exclamation that must be indicated by a blank.

“Take it calmly,” said Loveday; “don’t rave. Sit down and I’ll tell you all about it. No, it is not the doing of your friend Cassimi, so you need not threaten to put a bullet into him; the girl has married Lebrun of her own free will—no one has forced her into it.”

“Lucie has married Lebrun of her own free will!” he echoed, growing very white and taking the chair which faced Loveday at the little table.

“Will you have sugar?” asked Loveday, stirring the coffee, which the waiter at that moment brought.

===Chinese===
“那个——！”少校嚷道，霍地跳了起来，吐出一句只能用一个空白来代替的骂人话。

“冷静点，”洛芙迪说，“别嚷嚷。坐下，我把事情原原本本告诉您。不，这不是您那位朋友卡西米干的，所以您大可不必拿枪去崩他；这姑娘是心甘情愿嫁给勒布伦的——没有人逼她。”

“露西心甘情愿嫁给了勒布伦！”他跟着重复，脸刷地白了，在小桌前洛芙迪对面的椅子上坐下。

“您要加糖吗？”洛芙迪问，搅着侍者这时端上来的咖啡。

===Original===
“Yes, I repeat,” she presently resumed, “Lucie has married Lebrun of her own free will, although I conjecture she might not perhaps have been quite so willing to crown his happiness if the Princess Dullah-Veih had not made it greatly to her interest to do so.”

“Dolly made it to her interest to do so?” again echoed the Major.

“Do not interrupt me with exclamations; let me tell the story my own fashion, and then you may ask as many questions as you please. Now, to begin at the beginning, Lucie became engaged to Lebrun within a month of her coming to your mother’s house, but she carefully kept the secret from everyone, even from the servants, until about a month ago, when she mentioned the fact in confidence to Mrs. Druce in order to defend herself from the charge of having sought to attract your attention. There was nothing surprising in this engagement; they were both lonely and in a foreign land, spoke the same language, and no doubt had many things in common; and, although chance has lifted Lucie somewhat out of her station, she really belongs to the same class in life as Lebrun. Their lovemaking appears to have run along smoothly enough until you came home on leave, and the girl’s pretty face attracted your attention. Your evident admiration for her disturbed the equanimity of the Princess, who saw your devotion to herself waning; of Lebrun, who fancied Lucie’s manner to him had changed; of your mother, who was anxious that you should make a suitable marriage. Also additional complications arose from the fact that your attentions to the little Swiss girl had drawn Mr. Cassimi’s notice to her numerous attractions, and there was the danger of you two young men posing as rivals. At this juncture Lady Gwynne, as an intimate friend, and one who had herself suffered a twinge of heartache on Mademoiselle’s account, was taken into your mother’s confidence, and the three ladies in council decided that Lucie, in some fashion, must be got out of the way before you and Mr. Cassimi came to an open breach, or you had spoilt your matrimonial prospects.”

Here the Major made a slightly impatient movement.

===Chinese===
“是的，我再说一遍，”她过了一会儿接下去说，“露西是心甘情愿嫁给勒布伦的，不过我猜想，倘若不是杜拉-维公主让她觉得此事大有好处可图，她也未必就那么乐意成全他的幸福。”

“多莉让她觉得此事大有好处可图？”少校又跟着重复。

“别拿感叹话来打断我；让我照自己的路子把这故事讲完，到时候您爱问多少就问多少。那么，从头说起：露西来到令堂府上不出一个月，便与勒布伦订了婚，可她把这桩秘密对所有人都小心瞒着，连仆人也不让知道，直到大约一个月前，为了替自己辩解、撇清她有意招引您的嫌疑，才私下把这桩事悄悄告诉了德鲁斯太太。这桩婚约本没有什么可奇怪的：他们两个都是身在异国、形单影只，说着同一种语言，无疑也有许多共同之处；而且，虽说机缘把露西稍稍抬到了她原本的身份之上，她骨子里仍与勒布伦属于同一个阶层。他俩的恋情，看来一直平顺地发展着，直到您休假回家，姑娘的漂亮脸蛋引起了您的注意。您对她那份显而易见的倾慕，搅乱了公主的心境——她眼见您对她的专一日益冷淡；也搅乱了勒布伦的心境——他疑心露西对他的态度变了；还搅乱了令堂的心境——她正巴望您结一门门当户对的亲事。此外，又横生出新的麻烦：您对这小瑞士姑娘的殷勤，反倒引得卡西米先生也注意起她那许多动人之处，于是你们两个年轻人便有了互为情敌的危险。正在这当口，格温夫人作为密友——她自己也曾因这位小姐尝过一回醋意、揪过一回心——被令堂引为心腹，三位太太一合计，便决定：总得想个法子把露西弄走，免得您和卡西米先生公开闹翻，或者您把自己的好姻缘给搅黄了。”

这时少校略带不耐地动了动。

===Original===
Loveday went on: “It was the Princess who solved the question how this was to be done. Fair Rosamonds are no longer put out of the way by ‘a cup of cold poison’—golden guineas do the thing far more easily and innocently. The Princess expressed her willingness to bestow a thousand pounds on Lucie on the day that she married Lebrun, and to set her up afterwards as a fashionable milliner in Paris. After this munificent offer, everything else became mere matter of detail. The main thing was to get the damsel out of the way without your being able to trace her—perhaps work on her feelings, and induce her, at the last moment, to throw over Lebrun. Your absence from home, on a three days’ visit, gave them the wished-for opportunity. Lady Gwynne took her milliner into her confidence. Madame Céline consented to receive Lucie into her house, seclude her in a room on the upper floor, and at the same time give her an insight into the profession of a fashionable milliner. The rest I think you know. Lucie quietly walks out of the house one afternoon, taking no luggage, calling no cab, and thereby cutting off one very obvious means of being traced. Madame Céline receives and hides her—not a difficult feat to accomplish in London, more especially if the one to be hidden is a foreign amanuensis, who is seldom seen out of doors, and who leaves no photograph behind her.”

“I suppose it was Lebrun who had the confounded cheek to go to my drawer and appropriate that photograph. I wish it had been Cassimi—I could have kicked him, but—but it makes one feel rather small to have posed as rival to one’s mother’s butler.”

“I think you may congratulate yourself that Lebrun did nothing worse than go to your drawer and appropriate that photograph. I never saw a man bestow a more deadly look of hatred than he threw at you yesterday afternoon in your mother’s drawing-room; it was that look of hatred that first drew my attention to the man and set me on the track that has ended in the Swiss Protestant church this morning.”

===Chinese===
洛芙迪继续说：“究竟怎么个弄法，是公主出的主意。如今‘美丽的罗莎蒙德’（Fair Rosamond）们可不再靠‘一杯冷毒酒’来打发掉了——几枚金灿灿的几尼，办起这事来既省事，又无人受伤。公主表示，只要露西嫁给勒布伦，她便奉送一千英镑，日后还在巴黎替她开一家上等女帽店，让她体面立身。有了这桩慷慨之极的提议，其余种种便不过是细枝末节。要紧的是，得把这姑娘弄走，又不让您寻得到她——怕的就是您打动了她的心，叫她在最后一刻把勒布伦甩了。您离家三日做客，给了她们梦寐以求的机会。格温夫人把自己的女帽商也拉进了这桩秘密。赛琳夫人答应把露西接到自己家中，安置在楼上一间屋里藏起来，同时让她窥见一二上等女帽这一行的门道。剩下的，我想您也猜得到。某个下午，露西悄没声地走出了宅子，不带行李，不叫马车，从而断掉了一条再明显不过的寻人线索。赛琳夫人接收了她、藏起了她——在伦敦，这并非难事，尤其当要藏的是一位难得在户外露面、又没留下照片的外籍文书助手时。”

“我想，准是勒布伦这厚颜无耻的家伙，跑到我抽屉里把那张照片拿走的。我倒宁愿是卡西米——那样我好歹能踢他几脚，可是——可是，跟自家母亲的男管家争风吃醋，倒让人怪没面子的。”

“我想您倒该庆幸，勒布伦所做的，不过就是跑到您抽屉里拿走那张照片罢了。我从没见过一个男人投出过比他昨天下午在令堂客厅里投向您的那一眼更深切的痛恨；正是那一眼痛恨，头一个把我的注意引到了这人身上，让我循着一条线索追下去，一直追到了今早的瑞士新教教堂。”

===Original===
“Ah! let me hear about that—let me have the links in the chain, one by one, as you came upon them,” said the Major.

He was still pale—almost as the marble table at which they sat, but his voice had gone back to its normal slow, soft drawl.

“With pleasure. The look that Lebrun threw at you, as he crossed the room to open the window, was link number one. As I saw that look, I said to myself there is someone in that corner whom that man hates with a deadly hatred. Then you came forward to speak to me, and I saw that it was you that the man was ready to murder, if opportunity offered. After this, I scrutinized him closely—not a detail of his features or his dress escaped me, and I noticed, among other things, that on the fourth finger of his left hand, half hidden by a more pretentious ring, was an old fashioned curious looking silver one. That silver ring was link number two in the chain.”

“Ah, I suppose you asked for that glass of water on purpose to get a closer view of the ring?”

===Chinese===
“啊！这桩事让我听听——您一个一个地、按您查到的先后，把这条链子上的环节讲给我听，”少校说。

他仍然苍白——几乎跟他们坐着的那张大理石桌子一样白，可他的嗓音已恢复了平日那种慢悠悠、软绵绵的拖腔。

“乐意之至。勒布伦穿过客厅去开窗时，投向您的那个眼神，是第一环。我看见那眼神时，心里便说：那角落里有个人，正是这男人怀着切齿之恨所痛恨的。随后您走上前跟我说话，我才看清，那男人恨不得要谋杀的，正是您。此后我细细打量他——他五官和衣着上的每一处细节都没逃过我的眼睛，我还注意到，在他左手无名指上，半掩在一枚更张扬的戒指之下，套着一枚式样古老、模样奇特的银戒指。那枚银戒指，便是链上的第二环。”

“啊，我想您要那杯水，就是存心想凑近瞧瞧那枚戒指吧？”

===Original===
“I did, I found it was a Genevese ring of ancient make, the like of which I had not seen since I was a child and played with one, that my old Swiss bonne used to wear. Now I must tell you a little bit of Genevese history before I can make you understand how important a link that silver ring was to me. Echallets, the town in which Lucie was born, and her father had kept a watchmaker’s shop, has long been famous for its jewellery and watchmaking. The two trades, however, were not combined in one until about a hundred years ago, when the corporation of the town passed a law decreeing that they should unite in one guild for their common good. To celebrate this amalgamation of interests, the jewellers fabricated a certain number of silver rings, consisting of a plain band of silver, on which two hands, in relief, clasped each other. These rings were distributed among the members of the guild, and as time has gone on they have become scarce and valuable as relics of the past. In certain families, they have been handed down as heirlooms, and have frequently done duty as betrothal rings—the clasped hands no doubt suggesting their suitability for this purpose. Now, when I saw such a ring on Lebrun’s finger, I naturally guessed from whom he had received it, and at once classed his interests with those of your mother and the Princess, and looked upon him as their possible coadjutor.”

“What made you throw the brute Cassimi altogether out of your reckoning?”

===Chinese===
“正是。我发现那是一枚古旧的日内瓦银戒，这样的戒指，自我小时候玩过我那瑞士老保姆（bonne）戴过的一枚之后，便再没见过。现在我得跟您讲一段日内瓦的历史，您才能明白那枚银戒指于我是何等重要的一条线索。露西出生的埃沙莱镇，她父亲曾在那里开过一家钟表铺，那地方以珠宝和钟表业闻名已久。不过，这两门行当合而为一，还是大约一百年前的事：当时镇上的市政会通过一条法令，规定两业为共同利益合并为一个行会。为了纪念这一合并，珠宝匠们打造了若干枚银戒指，戒身是一圈素银，上面浮雕着两只紧握的手。这些戒指分发给了行会的成员，随着岁月流转，它们日渐稀少，成了珍贵的旧物。在某些人家，它们作为传家宝代代相传，又常被用作订婚戒指——那相握的双手，想必正暗合了此意。所以，当我在勒布伦指上看到这样一枚戒指，自然就猜出他是从谁那儿得来的，当即把他的利害归到了令堂和公主那一边，把他视作她们可能的帮手。”

“您又是什么缘故，把卡西米那畜生整个儿排除在您的盘算之外的？”

===Original===
“I did not do so at this stage of events; only, so to speak, marked him as ‘doubtful’ and kept my eye on him. I determined to try an experiment that I have never before attempted in my work. You know what that experiment was. I saw five persons, Mrs. Druce, the Princess, Lady Gwynne, Mr. Cassimi and Lebrun all in the room within a few yards of each other, and I asked you to take them by surprise and announce my name and profession, so that every one of those five persons could hear you.”

“You did. I could not, for the life of me, make out what was your motive for so doing.”

“My motive for so doing was simply, as it were, to raise the sudden cry, ‘The enemy is upon you,’ and to set every one of those five persons guarding their weak point—that is, if they had one. I’ll draw your attention to what followed. Mr. Cassimi remained nonchalant and impassive; your mother and Lady Gwynne exchanged glances, and they both simultaneously threw a nervous look at Lady Gwynne’s hat lying on the chair. Now as I had stood waiting to be introduced to Mrs. Druce, I had casually read the name of Madame Céline on the lining of the hat and I at once concluded that Madame Céline must be a very weak point indeed; a conclusion that was confirmed when Lady Gwynne hurriedly seized her hat and as hurriedly departed. Then the Princess scarcely less abruptly rose and left the room, and Lebrun on the point of entering, quitted it also. When he returned five minutes later, with the claret-cup, he had removed the ring from his finger, so I had now little doubt where his weak point lay.”

===Chinese===
“在这一阶段，我并没有那样做；我只是姑且把他标作‘存疑’，把他放在眼里盯着。我决定试一个我在工作中从没试过的法子。您知道那是个什么法子。我看见五个人——德鲁斯太太、公主、格温夫人、卡西米先生和勒布伦——都在屋里，彼此不过几步之遥，我便请您出其不意地报出我的姓名和职业，好让那五个人个个都听见。”

“您是这么做了。我可怎么也想不出您这么做的用意何在。”

“我这么做的用意，说穿了，无非是冷不丁地喊一声‘敌人扑上来了’，好叫那五个人各自去守自己的破绽——前提是他们当真有破绽。我请您留意随后发生的事。卡西米先生依旧满不在乎、不动声色；令堂和格温夫人交换了一个眼色，两人又同时紧张地瞟了一眼搁在椅子上的格温夫人的帽子。而我先前站着等候引见时，已不经意地从帽子的内衬上读到了赛琳夫人的名字，便立刻断定：赛琳夫人这处破绽，必定非同小可；待到格温夫人急急忙忙抓起帽子、又急急忙忙告辞而去，这一判断便坐实了。接着，公主也几乎同样突兀地起身离开了客厅；而勒布伦，本已到了门口正要进来，也转而退了出去。五分钟后他再端着红酒潘趣酒进来时，指上那枚戒指已经摘掉了——于是他的破绽在何处，我已再无多少怀疑。”

===Original===
“It’s wonderful; it’s like a fairy tale!,” drawled the Major. “Pray, go on.”

“After this,” continued Loveday, “my work became very simple. I did not care two straws for seeing Mademoiselle’s room, but I cared very much to have a talk with Mrs. Druce’s maid. From her I elicited the important fact that Lebrun was leaving very unexpectedly on the following day, and that his boxes were packed and labeled for Paris. After I left your house, I drove to Madame Céline’s, and there, as a sort of entrance fee, ordered an elaborate hat. I praised freely the hats they had on view, and while giving minute directions as to the one I required, I extracted the information that Madame Céline had recently taken on a new milliner who had very great artistic skill. Upon this, I asked permission to see this new milliner and give her special instructions concerning my hat. My request was referred to Madame Céline, who appeared much ruffled by it, and informed me that it would be quite useless for me to see this new milliner; she could execute no more orders, as she was leaving the next day for Paris, where she intended opening an establishment on her own account.

“Now you see the point at which I had arrived. There was Lebrun and there was this new milliner each leaving for Paris on the same day; it was not unreasonable to suppose that they might start in company, and that before so doing, a little ceremony might be gone through in the Swiss Protestant church that Mademoiselle occasionally attended. This conjecture sent me to the undertaker in South Savile Street, who combines with his undertaking the office of verger to the little church. From him I learned that a marriage was to take place at the church at a quarter to nine the next morning and that the names of the contracting parties were Pierre Lebrun and Lucie Cuénin.”

===Chinese===
“真是神了；简直像个童话！”少校拖着长腔说。“请讲下去。”

“打这以后，”洛芙迪接着说，“我的事就简单得很了。看小姐的房间，我压根儿不在乎；我倒很在乎能跟德鲁斯太太的贴身女仆攀谈攀谈。从她嘴里，我探出了一桩要紧的事实：勒布伦第二天就要走了，走得十分突然，箱子已经收拾停当，贴上了运往巴黎的标签。离开您府上之后，我乘车去了赛琳夫人店里，在那里，算是当作进门礼，订了一顶考究的帽子。我把店里陈列的帽子着实夸了一通，一边就自己要的那顶细细吩咐，一边套出了这样的消息：赛琳夫人近来新聘了一位极具艺术造诣的女帽师。我便趁机求见这位新来的女帽师，要单独就我的帽子给她些吩咐。我的请求被禀报给了赛琳夫人，她显得大为光火，告诉我要见这位新女帽师毫无用处；她不能再接任何活计，因为次日就要动身去巴黎，打算在那里自立门户，开一家自己的店。

“这下您看我查到了哪一步。一边是勒布伦，一边是这位新女帽师，两人同一天动身去巴黎；那么设想他们结伴同行，并且在动身之前，在那位小姐偶尔光顾的瑞士新教教堂里，先举行一个小小的仪式，并非全无道理。这一番推测，把我引到了南萨维尔街那家殡仪馆——那殡仪馆老板身兼那座小教堂的司事一职。从他口中我得知，第二天早晨差一刻九点，教堂里要行一桩婚礼，男女双方的名字是皮埃尔·勒布伦（Pierre Lebrun）和露西·屈南（Lucie Cuénin）。”

===Original===
“Cuénin!”

“Yes, that is the girl’s real name; it seems Lady Gwynne re-christened her Cunier, because she said the English pronunciation of Cuénin grated on her ear—people would insist upon adding a g after the n. She introduced her to Mrs. Druce under the name of Cunier, forgetting, perhaps, the girl’s real name, or else thinking it a matter of no importance. This fact, no doubt, considerably lessened Lebrun’s fear of detection in procuring his licence and transmitting it to the Swiss pastor. Perhaps you are a little surprised at my knowledge of the facts I related to you at the beginning of our conversation. I got at them through Lebrun this morning. At half-past eight I went down to the church and found him there, waiting for his bride. He grew terribly excited at seeing me, and thought I was going to bring you down on him and upset his wedding arrangements at the last moment. I assured him to the contrary, and his version of the facts I have handed on to you. Should, however, any details of the story seem to you to be lacking, I have no doubt that Mrs. Druce or the Princess will supply them, now that all necessity for secrecy has come to an end.”

The Major drew on his gloves; his colour had come back to him; he had resumed his easy suavity of manner.

“I don’t think,” he said slowly, “I’ll trouble my mother or the Princess; and I shall be glad, if you have the opportunity, if you will make people understand that I only moved in the matter at all out of—of mere kindness to a young and friendless foreigner.”

===Chinese===
“屈南！”

“是的，这才是那姑娘的真姓；看来是格温夫人给她改叫屈尼耶的，因为她说‘屈南’按英语的读法听着刺耳——人们总爱在n后面硬加个g。她以‘屈尼耶’之名把姑娘介绍给德鲁斯太太，也许是忘了姑娘的真姓，要不就是觉得这事无关紧要。这一桩，无疑大大减轻了勒布伦领结婚证、把证书转交瑞士牧师时怕被察觉的顾虑。或许您有点奇怪，方才我们一开头谈话时，我怎么知道那些情形。那是今早从勒布伦嘴里问出来的。八点半我下楼到教堂，发现他已在那儿等着新娘了。他一看见我，激动得不得了，以为我要把您领来对付他，在最后一刻搅黄他的婚事。我向他担保绝无其事，他说的那番情形，我便转述给您了。不过，这故事里若还有什么细节您觉得欠缺，我想德鲁斯太太或公主定会补全——眼下既已无须再守什么秘密了。”

少校戴上手套；他的脸色已恢复如常；那副从容圆滑的仪态也回来了。

“我想，”他慢悠悠地说，“我就不去叨扰我母亲或公主了；还有，您若有机会，劳您驾让大家明白，我当初过问这件事，无非是——无非出于对一个孤苦无依的年轻外国人的善意。”

## Drawn Daggers / 出鞘的匕首

===Original===
"I admit that the dagger business is something of a puzzle to me, but as for the lost necklace—well, I should have thought a child would have understood that," said Mr. Dyer irritably. "When a young lady loses a valuable article of jewellery and wishes to hush the matter up, the explanation is obvious."

"Sometimes," answered Miss Brooke calmly, "the explanation that is obvious is the one to be rejected, not accepted."

Off and on these two had been, so to speak, "jangling" a good deal that morning. Perhaps the fact was in part to be attributed to the biting east wind which had set Loveday's eyes watering with the gritty dust, as she had made her way to Lynch Court, and which was, at the present moment, sending the smoke, in aggravating gusts, down the chimney into Mr. Dyer's face. Thus it was, however. On the various topics that had chanced to come up for discussion that morning between Mr. Dyer and his colleague, they had each taken up, as if by design, diametrically opposite points of view.

===Chinese===
"我承认，那桩匕首的事于我多少是个谜；可丢失项链一事——唔，我原以为连小孩子都看得明白。"戴尔先生恼怒地说，"一位年轻小姐弄丢了一件值钱的首饰，又想把事情压下去，其中的原委再明显不过了。"

"有时候，"布鲁克小姐平静地答道，"那再明显不过的原委，恰恰是该被推翻、而非接受的。"

那天上午，这两个人可以说是"拌嘴"拌了好一阵子。或许，这在一定程度上要归咎于那刺骨的东风：洛芙迪一路走到林奇大院时，挟着粗砺尘沙的风吹得她双眼流泪；而此刻，这风又一阵紧似一阵地把烟囱里的烟倒灌下来，直扑戴尔先生的脸。然而情形就是这样：那天上午戴尔先生与其同僚之间凡偶然提起的话题，两人都像是约好了一般，各执一端，针锋相对。

===Original===
His temper altogether gave way now.

"If," he said, bringing his hand down with emphasis on his writing table, "you lay it down as a principle that the obvious is to be rejected in favour of the abstruse, you'll soon find yourself launched in the predicament of having to prove that two apples added to two other apples do not make four. But there, if you don't choose to see things from my point of view, that is no reason why you should lose your temper!"

===Chinese===
这一下，他的脾气彻底发作了。

"倘若，"他说着，把手用力地拍在写字台上，"你立下一条原则，说那显而易见之事都该弃之不顾、改去追捧那玄奥难解之理，那你很快就会陷入一种窘境：非得去证明两个苹果再加两个苹果并不等于四个不可。不过算了，你若不愿照我的看法看事情，那也不该为此动气呀！"

===Original===
"Mr. Hawke wishes to see you, sir," said a clerk, at that moment entering the room.

It was a fortunate diversion. Whatever might be the differences of opinion in which these two might indulge in private, they were careful never to parade those differences before their clients.

Mr. Dyer's irritability vanished in a moment.

===Chinese===
"霍克先生求见，先生。"一名办事员这时走进屋来说。

这来得正巧，是个得体的解围。无论这两人在私下里纵容自己有多少分歧，他们都十分小心，从不在主顾面前显露这些分歧。

戴尔先生的恼怒顷刻间烟消云散。

===Original===
"Show the gentleman in," he said to the clerk. Then he turned to Loveday. "This is the Rev. Anthony Hawke, the gentleman at whose house I told you that Miss Monroe is staying temporarily. He is a clergyman of the Church of England, but gave up his living some twenty years ago when he married a wealthy lady. Miss Monroe has been sent over to his guardianship from Peking by her father, Sir George Monroe, in order to get her out of the way of a troublesome and undesirable suitor."

The last sentence was added in a low and hurried tone, for Mr. Hawke was at that moment entering the room.

===Chinese===
"请那位先生进来。"他对办事员说，随即转向洛芙迪，"这位是安东尼·霍克牧师（Rev. Anthony Hawke），就是我跟你提过的，门罗小姐暂住在他府上的那位先生。他是英国国教的牧师，约二十年前娶了一位富家小姐，便辞去了圣职。门罗小姐由她父亲乔治·门罗爵士从北京送到他这里照管，为的是让她躲开一个麻烦又不宜交往的求婚者。"

最后这一句是压低了声音、匆匆说出口的，因为霍克先生此刻正走进屋来。

===Original===
He was a man close upon sixty years of age, white-haired, clean shaven, with a full, round face, to which a small nose imparted a somewhat infantine expression. His manner of greeting was urbane but slightly flurried and nervous. He gave Loveday the impression of being an easygoing, happy-tempered man who, for the moment, was unusually disturbed and perplexed.

He glanced uneasily at Loveday. Mr. Dyer hastened to explain that this was the lady by whose aid he hoped to get to the bottom of the matter now under consideration.

===Chinese===
他年近六十，一头白发，胡子刮得干干净净，一张圆润饱满的脸，配上一个小巧的鼻子，显出几分孩童般的神情。他寒暄时举止文雅，却略带慌乱与紧张。他给洛芙迪的印象是：本是个随和、好脾气的人，此刻却异乎寻常地心神不宁、六神无主。

他不安地瞟了洛芙迪一眼。戴尔先生赶忙解释说，这位便是他希望能借其援手、查明眼下这桩案子底细的那位女士。

===Original===
"In that case there can be no objection to my showing you this," said Mr. Hawke; "it came by post this morning. You see my enemy still pursues me."

As he spoke he took from his pocket a big, square envelope, from which he drew a large-sized sheet of paper.

On this sheet of paper were roughly drawn, in ink, two daggers, about six inches in length, with remarkably pointed blades.

===Chinese===
"既然如此，我拿这个给您看看，想必也无妨了。"霍克先生说，"这是今早邮寄来的。您瞧，我的仇家还在紧追着我不放呢。"

他说着，从口袋里掏出一只方方正正的大信封，从中抽出一张大幅的信纸。

这张纸上，用墨水粗略地画着两把匕首，约六英寸长，刀尖异常锋锐。

===Original===
Mr. Dyer looked at the sketch with interest.

"We will compare this drawing and its envelope with those you previously received," he said, opening a drawer of his writing-table and taking thence a precisely similar envelope. On the sheet of paper, however, that this envelope enclosed, there was drawn one dagger only.

He placed both envelopes and their enclosures side by side, and in silence compared them. Then, without a word, he handed them to Miss Brooke, who, taking a glass from her pocket, subjected them to a similar careful and minute scrutiny.

===Chinese===
戴尔先生颇有兴味地端详着这幅画。

"我们把这幅画和它的信封，与您先前收到的那几封比一比。"他说着，打开写字台的一个抽屉，从中取出一只一模一样的信封。只不过这只信封里装的那张纸上，只画了一把匕首。

他把两只信封连同内里所装的纸张并排放好，默默加以比对。然后，他一言不发地把它们递给布鲁克小姐；她从口袋里取出一面放大镜，同样仔细入微地审视了一番。

===Original===
Both envelopes were of precisely the same make, and were each addressed to Mr. Hawke's London address in a round, school-boyish, copybook sort of hand—the hand so easy to write and so difficult to being home to any writer on account of its want of individuality. Each envelope likewise bore a Cork and a London postmark.

The sheet of paper, however, that the first envelope enclosed bore the sketch of one dagger only.

Loveday laid down her glass.

===Chinese===
两只信封的质地一模一样，收件地址都是霍克先生的伦敦住址，用的是一种圆圆的、学生腔、描红簿式的笔迹——这种字迹写来容易，却因为毫无个性而极难追查到书写者。每只信封上同样都盖有科克和伦敦两地的邮戳。

然而，先前那只信封里所装的那张纸上，只有一把匕首的图样。

洛芙迪放下放大镜。

===Original===
"The envelopes," she said, "have, undoubtedly, been addressed by the same person, but these last two daggers have not been drawn by the hand that drew the first. Dagger number one was, evidently, drawn by a timid, uncertain and inartistic hand—see how the lines wave and how they have been patched here and there. The person who drew the other daggers, I should say, could do better work; the outline, though rugged, is bold and free. I should like to take these sketches home with me and compare them again at my leisure."

"Ah, I felt sure what your opinion would be!" said Mr. Dyer complacently.

Mr. Hawke seemed much disturbed.

===Chinese===
"这两只信封，"她说，"毫无疑问是同一个人写的地址；可最近这两把匕首，却不是出自画头一把匕首的那只手。头一把匕首，显然出自一只胆怯、犹疑、毫无艺术功底的手——您看这些线条多么飘忽，还有这里那里修补涂抹的痕迹。而画另两把匕首的人，我敢说，功底要好得多；轮廓虽粗犷，却遒劲自如。我想把这些图样带回去，闲暇时再细细比对一番。"

"哈，我就猜到你会这么看！"戴尔先生得意洋洋地说。

霍克先生似乎大为不安。

===Original===
"Good gracious!" he ejaculated; "you don't mean to say I have two enemies pursuing me in this fashion! What does it mean? Can it be—is it possible, do you think, that these things have been sent to me by the members of some Secret Society in Ireland—under error, of course—mistaking me for someone else? They can't be meant for me; I have never, in my whole life, been mixed up with any political agitation of any sort."

Mr. Dyer shook his head. "Members of secret societies generally make pretty sure of their ground before they send out missives of this kind," he said. "I have never heard of such an error being made. I think, too, we mustn't build any theories on the Irish postmark; the letters may have been posted in Cork for the whole and sole purpose of drawing off attention from some other quarter."

===Chinese===
"老天爷！"他失声惊呼，"您莫不是说，我有两个仇家这般纠缠我！这是怎么回事？会不会——您看有没有可能——这些东西是爱尔兰某个秘密结社的成员寄给我的——自然是弄错了——把我错认成旁人了？它们不可能是冲着我来的；我这一辈子，从不曾卷入任何形式的政治风波。"

戴尔先生摇摇头。"秘密结社的人发出这类信函之前，通常都把底细摸得清清楚楚，"他说，"我可从没听说过有这样的差池。还有，我想我们也不可单凭那爱尔兰的邮戳来立论；这些信说不定正是专程在科克投寄的，目的无非是把注意力从别处引开。"

===Original===
"Will you mind telling me a little about the loss of the necklace?" here said Loveday, bringing the conversation suddenly round from the daggers to the diamonds.

"I think," interposed Mr. Dyer, turning towards her, "that the episode of the drawn daggers—drawn in a double sense—should be treated entirely on its own merits, considered as a thing apart from the loss of the necklace. I am inclined to believe that when we have gone a little further into the matter we shall find that each circumstance belongs to a different group of facts. After all, it is possible that these daggers may have been sent by way of a joke—a rather foolish one, I admit—by some harum-scarum fellow bent on causing a sensation."

===Chinese===
"可否请您把丢失项链一事略说一说？"洛芙迪这时开口道，把话题从匕首骤然转到了钻石上。

"依我看，"戴尔先生转向她插话道，"这'画出来的匕首'一事——'画出来'在此是双关——应当完全就事论事地看待，当作与项链失窃不相干的一桩事来处理。我倾向于认为，待我们把事情再往深处查一查，便会发觉这两件事分属两组互不相干的事实。毕竟，也有可能这些匕首只是个恶作剧——我得承认，是个相当愚蠢的恶作剧——出自某个轻浮莽撞、存心要闹出点动静的家伙之手。"

===Original===
Mr. Hawke's face brightened.

"Ah! now, do you think so—really think so?" he ejaculated. "It would lift such a load from my mind if you could bring the thing home, in this way, to some practical joker. There are a lot of such fellows knocking about the world. Why, now I come to think of it, my nephew, Jack, who is a good deal with us just now, and is not quite so steady a fellow as I should like him to be, must have a good many such scamps among his acquaintances."

"A good many such scamps among his acquaintances," echoed Loveday; "that certainly gives plausibility to Mr. Dyer's supposition. At the same time, I think we are bound to look at the other side of the case, and admit the possibility of these daggers being sent in right-down sober earnest by persons concerned in the robbery, with the intention of intimidating you and preventing full investigation of the matter. If this be so, it will not signify which thread we take up and follow. If we find the sender of the daggers we are safe to come upon the thief; or, if we follow up and find the thief, the sender of the daggers will not be far off."

===Chinese===
霍克先生的面色豁然开朗。

"啊！您当真这么以为——当真这么以为？"他脱口而出，"您若能把这事这样归到某个恶作剧的人头上，我心里那块大石头可就落了地。世上到处都有这号人在外头游荡。说起来，我想起来了，我那侄子杰克（Jack），近来常常在我们家住着，这孩子又不如我所愿的那般稳重，他的熟人里头，准保有不少这种不三不四的家伙。"

"他的熟人里头准保有不少这种不三不四的家伙，"洛芙迪附和道，"这话无疑给戴尔先生的揣测增添了些分量。可与此同时，我想我们也理应看看案子的另一面，承认这样一种可能：这些匕首正是由与盗窃案有关的人一本正经、郑重其事地寄来的，意在恐吓您、阻挠此案的彻查。倘若果真如此，那我们顺着哪条线头去追都不要紧。我们若查到寄匕首的人，就笃定能揪出窃贼；或者，我们若一路追查、逮住了窃贼，那寄匕首的人也必在不远之处。"

===Original===
Mr. Hawke's face fell once more.

"It's an uncomfortable position to be in," he said slowly. "I suppose, whoever they are, they will do the regulation thing, and next time will send an instalment of three daggers, in which case I may consider myself a doomed man. It did not occur to me before, but I remember now that I did not receive the first dagger until after I had spoken very strongly to Mrs. Hawke, before the servants, about my wish to set the police to work. I told her I felt bound, in honour to Sir George, to do so, as the necklace had been lost under my roof."

"Did Mrs. Hawke object to your calling in the aid of the police?" asked Loveday.

===Chinese===
霍克先生的脸色又黯淡下来。

"这处境可真不好受，"他缓缓说道，"我猜，不管他们是谁，总会按着规矩办事，下一回便会寄来一组三把匕首，果真那样，我大概就可以算作一个在劫难逃之人了。这一点我先前未曾想到，可如今我记起来了：我是在当着仆人的面，向霍克太太（Mrs. Hawke）力陈我要请警方介入之后，才收到头一把匕首的。我对她说，念在对乔治爵士的信义，我非这么做不可，因为项链是在我家失窃的。"

"霍克太太反对您请警方介入吗？"洛芙迪问。

===Original===
"Yes, most strongly. She entirely supported Miss Monroe in her wish to take no steps in the matter. Indeed, I should not have come round as I did last night to Mr. Dyer, if my wife had not been suddenly summoned from home by the serious illness of her sister. At least," he corrected himself with a little attempt at self-assertion, "my coming to him might have been a little delayed. I hope you understand, Mr. Dyer; I do not mean to imply that I am not master in my own house."

"Oh, quite so, quite so," responded Mr. Dyer. "Did Mrs. Hawke or Miss Monroe give any reasons for not wishing you to move in the matter?"

===Chinese===
"反对，极力反对。门罗小姐不愿对此事采取任何行动，霍克太太完全站在她那一边。说实话，要不是我妻子因其胞姐病重而被骤然唤离了家，我昨晚也不会回心转意来找戴尔先生了。至少，"他略带几分自重的口吻更正道，"我上他这儿来，或许会稍稍迟些。希望您明白我的意思，戴尔先生；我并非暗示我在自家做不了主。"

"哦，自然，自然。"戴尔先生答道，"霍克太太或门罗小姐可曾给出不愿您插手的理由？"

===Original===
"All told, I should think they gave about a hundred reasons—I can't remember them all. For one thing, Miss Monroe said it might necessitate her appearing in the police courts, a thing she would not consent to do; and she certainly did not consider the necklace was worth the fuss I was making over it. And that necklace, sir, has been valued at over nine hundred pounds, and has come down to the young lady from her mother."

"And Mrs. Hawke?"

===Chinese===
"算起来，我想她们大约给了一百来条理由——我也记不全了。其一，门罗小姐说，这或许免不了要她上治安法庭，那是她万不肯做的事；她还说，那串项链实在犯不着我为它这样大动干戈。可是先生，那串项链估值在九百英镑以上，而且是由那位小姐的母亲传给她的。"

"那霍克太太呢？"

===Original===
"Mrs. Hawke supported Miss Monroe in her views in her presence. But privately to me afterwards, she gave other reasons for not wishing the police called in. Girls, she said, were always careless with their jewellery, she might have lost the necklace in Peking, and never have brought it to England at all."

"Quite so," said Mr. Dyer. "I think I understood you to say that no one had seen the necklace since Miss Monroe's arrival in England. Also, I believe it was she who first discovered it to be missing?"

===Chinese===
"当着门罗小姐的面，霍克太太是支持她的看法的。可过后私下里对我说，她又给出了旁的理由，不愿请警方插手。她说，女孩子家对待首饰向来大意，那串项链说不定早就在北京丢了，压根儿就没带进英国。"

"原来如此。"戴尔先生说，"我记得您好像说过，自门罗小姐抵英之后，再无人见过那串项链。还有，最先发现项链不见的，也是她本人吧？"

===Original===
"Yes. Sir George, when he wrote apprising me of his daughter's visit, added a postscript to his letter, saying that his daughter was bringing her necklace with her and that he would feel greatly obliged if I would have it deposited with as little delay as possible at my bankers', where it could be easily got at if required. I spoke to Miss Monroe about doing this two or three times, but she did not seem at all inclined to comply with her father's wishes. Then my wife took the matter in hand—Mrs. Hawke, I must tell you, has a very firm, resolute manner—she told Miss Monroe plainly that she would not have the responsibility of those diamonds in the house, and insisted that there and then they should be sent off to the bankers. Upon this Miss Monroe went up to her room, and presently returned, saying that her necklace had disappeared. She herself, she said, had placed it in her jewel-case and the jewel-case in her wardrobe, when her boxes were unpacked. The jewel-case was in the wardrobe right enough, and no other article of jewellery appeared to have been disturbed, but the little padded niche in which the necklace had been deposited was empty. My wife and her maid went upstairs immediately, and searched every corner of the room, but, I'm sorry to say, without any result."

"Miss Monroe, I suppose, has her own maid?"

===Chinese===
"是的。乔治爵士写信告知我他女儿要来时，在信末加了一则附笔，说他女儿会把她的项链随身带来，恳请我尽快将其存入我的银行，以便需要时随时取用。这事我跟门罗小姐提过两三次，可她似乎全无意遵从父亲的意愿。后来我妻子接手了此事——我须告诉各位，霍克太太为人极有决断、行事果决——她直截了当地告诉门罗小姐，她不愿担着这串钻石存放在家中的责任，坚持当场就要把项链送去银行。于是门罗小姐上楼回了自己房间，不多时便回来说，她的项链不见了。她说，开箱时是她亲手把项链放进首饰盒，又把首饰盒放进衣橱里的。首饰盒确在衣橱中不假，其余首饰似乎也无翻动之痕，唯独原来安放项链的那个软衬小格空了。我妻子和她的贴身女仆立时上楼，把房间每个角落都搜了一遍，可惜——一无所获。"

"门罗小姐自己有贴身女仆吧？"

===Original===
"No, she has not. The maid—an elderly native woman—who left Peking with her, suffered so terribly from seasickness that, when they reached Malta, Miss Monroe allowed her to land and remain there in charge of an agent of the P. and O. Company till an outward bound packet could take her back to China. It seems the poor woman thought she was going to die, and was in a terrible state of mind because she hadn't brought her coffin with her. I dare say you know the terror these Chinese have of being buried in foreign soil. After her departure, Miss Monroe engaged one of the steerage passengers to act as her maid for the remainder of the voyage."

"Did Miss Monroe make the long journey from Peking accompanied only by this native woman?"

===Chinese===
"她没有。从北京随她启程的那名女仆——一位上了年纪的本地妇人——晕船晕得死去活来，所以她们一到马耳他（Malta），门罗小姐便准她登岸，将她托付给半岛东方轮船公司（P. and O. Company）的一名代理人照管，等有返程班轮再送她回中国。据说那可怜的妇人自以为命将休矣，又因没把自己的棺材一并带来而惶恐万分。各位想必知道，中国人对葬身异乡有着怎样的恐惧。她走后，门罗小姐便雇了统舱里的一名乘客，在剩余的航程中充当她的女仆。"

"门罗小姐从北京一路远行，就只由这位本地妇人陪着吗？"

===Original===
"No; friends escorted her to Hong King—by far the roughest part of the journey. From Hong Kong she came on in the *Colombo*, accompanied only by her maid. I wrote and told her father I would meet her at the docks in London; the young lady, however, preferred landing at Plymouth, and telegraphed to me from there that she was coming on by rail to Waterloo, where, if I liked, I might meet her."

"She seems to be a young lady of independent habits. Was she brought up and educated in China?"

===Chinese===
"并非如此；有朋友一路护送她到香港（Hong Kong）——那是最为颠簸艰险的一段路。从香港起，她便乘'科伦坡号'（Colombo）轮船前来，身边只带着那名女仆。我去信告诉她父亲，说我会在伦敦码头接她；可这位小姐却宁愿在普利茅斯（Plymouth）登岸，从那儿给我发来电报，说她将乘火车到滑铁卢（Waterloo）车站，我若愿意，可在那儿与她相见。"

"看来她是位惯于自行其是的年轻小姐。她是在中国抚养长大、受的教育么？"

===Original===
"Yes; by a succession of French and American governesses. After her mother's death, when she was little more than a baby, Sir George could not make up his mind to part with her, as she was his only child."

"I suppose you and Sir George Monroe are old friends?"

===Chinese===
"是的；先后由一连串法国和美国籍的家庭教师教养。她母亲去世时她还不过是个襁褓婴孩，而她是乔治爵士的独女，爵士舍不得与她分离。"

"我猜您与乔治·门罗爵士是老交情了？"

===Original===
"Yes; he and I were great chums before he went out to China—now about twenty years ago—and it was only natural, when he wished to get his daughter out of the way of young Danvers's impertinent attentions, that he should ask me to take charge of her till he could claim his retiring pension and set up his tent in England."

"What was the chief objection to Mr. Danvers's attentions?"

===Chinese===
"是的；他动身去中国之前——大约二十年前——我们是要好的挚友；所以当他想把女儿从小丹弗斯（Danvers）放肆纠缠中解脱出来，自然而然地就请我代为照管，直到他领了退休金、回英国安家为止。"

"对丹弗斯先生的纠缠，主要的不满是什么？"

===Original===
"Well, he is only a boy of one-and-twenty, and has no money into the bargain. He has been sent out to Peking by his father to study the language, in order to qualify for a billet in the customs, and it may be a dozen years before he is in a position to keep a wife. Now, Miss Monroe is an heiress—will come into her mother's large fortune when she is of age—and Sir George, naturally, would like her to make a good match."

"I suppose Miss Monroe came to England very reluctantly?"

===Chinese===
"唉，他不过是个二十一岁的毛小子，又没有半文钱。他父亲把他打发到北京来学语言，好谋一个海关的差事，只怕要过上一十二年，他才有能力养家。可门罗小姐是位继承人——一到成年便可继承她母亲的一大笔财产——乔治爵士自然盼她结一门好亲事。"

"我猜门罗小姐是很不情愿才来英国的吧？"

===Original===
"I imagine so. No doubt it was a great wrench for her to leave her home and friends in that sudden fashion and come to us, who are, one and all, utter strangers to her. She is very quiet, very shy and reserved. She goes nowhere, sees no one. When some old China friends of her father's called to see her the other day, she immediately found she had a headache, and went to bed. I think, on the whole, she gets on better with my nephew than with anyone else."

"Will you kindly tell me of how many persons your household consists at the present moment?"

===Chinese===
"我想是的。这般仓促地撇下故园亲友，来到我们这些与她素昧平生的人当中，对她无疑是一大难堪。她极为沉静，极其腼腆寡言。她哪儿也不去，谁也不见。前几日她父亲几位旧日中国友人来访，她立刻就觉得头疼，回房歇着了。总的来说，我看她与我那侄子相处，比与谁都自在些。"

"可否劳烦告诉我，您府上眼下共有几口人？"

===Original===
"At the present moment we are one more than usual, for my nephew, Jack, is home with his regiment from India, and is staying with us. As a rule, my household consists of my wife and myself, butler, cook, housemaid and my wife's maid, who just now is doing double duty as Miss Monroe's maid also."

Mr. Dyer looked at his watch.

"I have an important engagement in ten minutes' time," he said, "so I must leave you and Miss Brooke to arrange details as to how and when she is to begin her work inside your house, for, of course, in a case of this sort we must, in the first instance at any rate, concentrate attention within your four walls."

===Chinese===
"眼下我们比平日多一口人，因为我那侄子杰克随团从印度回来，正住在我们这里。寻常时候，我家中便是内人与我，加上男管家、厨子、一名女佣和内人的贴身女仆——这位贴身女仆此刻身兼两职，同时照应着门罗小姐。"

戴尔先生看了看表。

"十分钟后我有一桩重要的约会，"他说，"所以只得把您与布鲁克小姐如何安排、何时开始进府查案的细节，留给您二位商定了。当然，办这类案子，我们无论如何都得先把注意力集中在您府上四壁之内。"

===Original===
"The less delay the better," said Loveday. "I should like to attack the mystery at once—this afternoon."

Mr. Hawke thought for a moment.

"According to present arrangements," he said, with a little hesitation, "Mrs. Hawke will return next Friday, that is the day after tomorrow, so I can only ask you to remain in the house till the morning of that day. I'm sure you will understand that there might be some—some little awkwardness in—"

"Oh, quite so," interrupted Loveday. "I don't see at present that there will be any necessity for me to sleep in the house at all. How would it be for me to assume the part of a lady house decorator in the employment of a West-end firm, and sent by them to survey your house and advise upon its re-decoration? All I should have to do, would be to walk about your rooms with my head on one side, and a pencil and notebook in my hand. I should interfere with no one, your family life would go on as usual, and I could make my work as short or as long as necessity might dictate."

===Chinese===
"宜早不宜迟。"洛芙迪说，"我愿即刻着手揭开这桩谜团——就在今天下午。"

霍克先生沉吟片刻。

"按眼下的安排，"他略带迟疑地说，"霍克太太下周五回来，也就是后天，所以我只能请您在府上盘桓到那天早上。您一定明白，这当中或许会有些——会有些小小的不便，于——"

"哦，那自然。"洛芙迪打断道，"我看眼下并无必要让我留宿府上。不如这样：我乔装成西区一家商号雇用的女房舍装饰师，由他们派来勘察您的府第、就重新装饰一事提出建议，您看如何？我所要做的，不过是在各间屋里踱来踱去，歪着头，手执一支铅笔一本记事簿罢了。我不会打扰任何人，您家的日子照旧，而我的活儿可长可短，全凭需要而定。"

===Original===
Mr. Hawke had no objection to offer to this. He had, however, a request to make as he rose to depart, and he made it a little nervously.

"If," he said, "by any chance there should come a telegram from Mrs. Hawke, saying she will return by an earlier train, I suppose—I hope, that is, you will make some excuse, and—and not get me into hot water, I mean."

To this, Loveday answered a little evasively that she trusted no such telegram would be forthcoming, but that, in any case, he might rely upon her discretion.

Four o'clock was striking from a neighbouring church clock as Loveday lifted the old-fashioned brass knocker of Mr. Hawke's house in Tavistock Square. An elderly butler admitted her and showed her into the drawing-room on the first floor. A single glance round showed Loveday that if her role had been real instead of assumed, she would have found plenty of scope for her talents. Although the house was in all respects comfortably furnished, it bore unmistakably the impress of those early Victorian days when aesthetic surroundings were not deemed a necessity of existence; an impress which people past middle age, and growing increasingly indifferent to the accessories of life, are frequently careless to remove.

===Chinese===
霍克先生对此并无异议。可他站起身告辞时，却又提出一个请求，说得颇为忐忑。

"要是，"他说，"万一霍克太太来了电报，说她要搭更早一班火车回来，我想——我是说，希望，您能想个什么借口，可——可别让我下不来台，我的意思是。"

对此，洛芙迪略带闪烁地答道：但愿不会有这样一封电报，可无论发生什么，他尽可信赖她的分寸。

邻近教堂的钟敲响四点时，洛芙迪正叩响霍克先生在塔维斯托克广场（Tavistock Square）那所宅子的老式铜门环。一位上了年纪的男管家把她迎进门，引她到二楼的客厅。洛芙迪只需环顾一眼便明白：倘若她这身份不是乔装而是真的，她大有用武之地。这宅子虽则处处陈设舒适，却分明留着维多利亚早期的印记——在那个年代，审美的环境尚不被视为生存之必需；这种印记，年过半百、对生活的细枝末节日渐淡漠的人，往往也懒得再去拂拭。

===Original===
