"Young life here is evidently an excrescence, not part of the home; a troop of daughters turned into this room would speedily set going a different condition of things," thought Loveday, taking stock of the faded white and gold wall paper, the chairs covered with lilies and roses in cross-stitch, and the knickknacks of a past generation that were scattered about on tables and mantelpiece.

A yellow damask curtain, half-festooned, divided the back drawing-room from the front in which she was seated. From the other side of this curtain there came to her the sound of voices—those of a man and a girl.

"Cut the cards again, please," said the man's voice. "Thank you. There you are again—the queen of hearts, surrounded with diamonds, and turning her back on a knave. Miss Monroe, you can't do better than make that fortune come true. Turn your back on the man who let you go without a word and—"

===Chinese===
"青春的生气在这里显然是多余之物，并非这家的组成部分；若是把一群女儿扔进这间屋子，情形很快就会大不一样。"洛芙迪暗想，一边打量着那褪色的白底金花墙纸、绣着十字针百合与玫瑰的椅套，以及散落在桌案与壁炉架上那一辈人留下的种种零碎小摆设。

一道半卷起的黄色锦缎帷幔，将后面那间客厅与她就坐的前厅隔开。帷幔那头传来人声——一男一女。

"请再切一次牌。"那男声说，"谢谢。您瞧，又是这张——红心王后，被钻石环围着，背对着一个杰克。门罗小姐，您不如就让这桩预言应验了吧。转过身去，背对那个一声不吭放您走的男人，然后——"

===Original===
"Hush!" interrupted the girl with a little laugh: "I heard the next room door open—I'm sure someone came in."

The girl's laugh seemed to Loveday utterly destitute of that echo of heartache that in the circumstances might have been expected.

At this moment Mr. Hawke entered the room, and almost simultaneously the two young people came from the other side of the yellow curtain and crossed towards the door.

Loveday took a survey of them as they passed.

===Chinese===
"嘘！"那姑娘轻笑着打断道，"我听见隔壁屋开门了——准是有人进来了。"

在洛芙迪听来，这姑娘的笑声里丝毫不带那般情境下本该有的、隐隐的愁绪。

这时霍克先生走进客厅，几乎与此同时，两个年轻人也从那道黄色帷幔后头走出来，朝门口走去。

两人经过时，洛芙迪将他们打量了一番。

===Original===
The young man—evidently "my nephew, Jack"—was a good-looking young fellow, with dark eyes and hair. The girl was small, slight and fair. She was perceptibly less at home with Jack's uncle than she was with Jack, for her manner changed and grew formal and reserved as she came face to face with him.

"We're going downstairs to have a game of billiards," said Jack, addressing Mr. Hawke, and throwing a look of curiosity at Loveday.

"Jack," said the old gentleman, "what would you say if I told you I was going to have the house redecorated from top to bottom, and that this lady had come to advise on the matter."

===Chinese===
那青年——显然便是"我那侄子杰克"——生得一表人才，眉眼头发都是深色的。那姑娘身材娇小纤弱，肤色白皙。她在杰克叔叔面前显然不如在杰克面前那般自在，因为一与他对面，她的神态便变了，变得拘谨而矜持。

"我们下楼去打一盘台球。"杰克对霍克先生说，同时好奇地瞟了洛芙迪一眼。

"杰克，"老绅士说，"要是我告诉你，我打算把这宅子从头到尾重新装饰一番，而这位女士正是为此而来出主意，你怎么说？"

===Original===
This was the nearest (and most Anglicé) approach to a fabrication that Mr. Hawke would allow to pass his lips.

"Well," answered Jack promptly, "I should say, 'not before its time.' That would cover a good deal."

Then the two young people departed in company.

Loveday went straight to her work.

===Chinese===
这便是霍克先生所能容许自己说出口的、最（也是最合绅士体统的）近于撒谎的话了。

"嗯，"杰克立刻答道，"我就说：'早该如此了。'这一句话能管不少事儿呢。"

说罢两个年轻人便结伴出去了。

洛芙迪径直着手办事。

===Original===
"I'll begin my surveying at the top of the house, and at once, if you please," she said. "Will you kindly tell one of your maids to show me through the bedrooms? If it is possible, let that maid be the one who waits on Miss Monroe and Mrs. Hawke."

The maid who responded to Mr. Hawke's summons was in perfect harmony with the general appearance of the house. In addition, however, to being elderly and faded, she was also remarkably sour-visaged, and carried herself as if she thought that Mr. Hawke had taken a great liberty in thus commanding her attendance.

In dignified silence she showed Loveday over the topmost story, where the servants' bedrooms were situated, and with a somewhat supercilious expression of countenance, watched her making various entries in her notebook.

===Chinese===
"请允许我即刻从宅子顶层开始我的勘察。"她说，"劳烦您吩咐哪位女仆带我过一遍各间卧房？如有可能，就请那位伺候门罗小姐和霍克太太的女仆来吧。"

应霍克先生之唤而来的那位女仆，与这宅子的整体气派可谓相得益彰。只是她除了上了年纪、容颜憔悴之外，还板着一副格外酸楚难看的脸，走起路来的神气，仿佛觉得霍克先生这般差遣她，是大大地僭越了。

她端着一副矜持的沉默，引洛芙迪看完顶层——仆人们的卧房便在那里——又带着几分倨傲的神色，看着她在记事簿上记下种种条目。

===Original===
In dignified silence, also, she led the way down to the second floor, where were the principal bedrooms of the house.

"This is Miss Monroe's room," she said, as she threw back a door of one of these rooms, and then shut her lips with a snap, as if they were never going to open again.

The room that Loveday entered was, like the rest of the house, furnished in the style that prevailed in the early Victorian period. The bedstead was elaborately curtained with pink lined upholstery; the toilet-table was befrilled with muslin and tarlatan out of all likeness to a table. The one point, however, that chiefly attracted Loveday's attention was the extreme neatness that prevailed throughout the apartment—a neatness, however, that was carried out with so strict an eye to comfort and convenience that it seemed to proclaim the hand of a first-class maid. Everything in the room was, so to speak, squared to the quarter of an inch, and yet everything that a lady could require in dressing lay ready to hand. The dressing-gown lying on the back of a chair had footstool and slippers beside it. A chair stood in front of the toilet table, and on a small Japanese table to the right of the chair were placed hairpin box, comb and brush, and hand mirror.

"This room will want money spent upon it," said Loveday, letting her eyes roam critically in all directions. "Nothing but Moorish woodwork will take off the squareness of those corners. But what a maid Miss Monroe must have. I never before saw a room so orderly and, at the same time, so comfortable."

===Chinese===
她依旧端着那副矜持的沉默，引路下到二楼——宅子的主卧便都在那一层。

"这是门罗小姐的房间。"她推开其中一间的门说道，随即把嘴唇一抿，仿佛再不肯张开似的。

洛芙迪走进的那间房，与宅中其余各处一样，都是维多利亚早期盛行的陈设风格。床架繁复地张着粉红衬里的帷幔；梳妆台缀满了细麻纱与塔拉坦纱的褶边，瞧着再不像一张台子。然而，最惹洛芙迪注意的一点，是整间屋子那极端的整洁——只是这种整洁处处着眼于舒适与方便，分明透出一位一流女仆的手笔。屋里样样东西，可以说都规整得丝毫不差；而女士梳妆所需的一切，又都触手可及。搭在椅背上的晨衣旁，备有脚凳与拖鞋。梳妆台前放着一把椅子，椅子右侧的小日式几上，摆着发夹盒、梳子、发刷和手镜。

"这间屋子要花上一笔钱才成，"洛芙迪说着，目光挑剔地四下逡巡，"只有摩尔式的木雕才能消去那些拐角生硬的棱角。不过门罗小姐的那位女仆，可真是了得。我从未见过哪间屋子能这般井井有条，同时又这般舒适。"

===Original===
This was so direct an appeal to conversation that the sour-visaged maid felt compelled to open her lips.

"I wait on Miss Monroe, for the present," she said snappishly; "but, to speak the truth, she scarcely requires a maid. I never before in my life had dealings with such a young lady."

"She does so much for herself, you mean—declines much assistance."

"She's like no one else I ever had to do with." (This was said even more snappishly than before.) "She not only won't be helped in dressing, but she arranges her room every day before leaving it, even to placing the chair in front of the looking glass."

===Chinese===
这话直截了当地引她说话，那副酸脸的女仆也只好把嘴张开了。

"眼下是我在伺候门罗小姐，"她没好气地说，"可说实话，她几乎用不着女仆。我这辈子还从未伺候过这样一位小姐。"

"您是说，凡事她都亲力亲为——不大肯让人帮忙。"

"她跟我打交道过的任何人都不同。"（这话比方才还没好气。）"她不但穿衣不要人帮忙，每天出门前还把自己房间收拾停当，连梳妆镜前那把椅子都要摆到指定位置。"

===Original===
"And to opening the lid of the hairpin box, so that she may have the pins ready to her hand," added Loveday, for a moment bending over the Japanese table, with its toilet accessories.

Another five minutes were all that Loveday accorded to the inspection of this room. Then, a little to the surprise of the dignified maid, she announced her intention of completing her survey of the bedrooms some other time, and dismissed her at the drawing-room door, to tell Mr. Hawke that she wished to see him before leaving.

Mr. Hawke, looking much disturbed and with a telegram in his hand, quickly made his appearance.

===Chinese===
"还掀开发夹盒的盖子，好让发夹随手可取。"洛芙迪补了一句，一时俯身在那摆满梳妆用具的日式小几上。

洛芙迪只再给了这间屋子五分钟的查看工夫。随后，令那位端架子的女仆略感意外的是，她宣布改日再来把卧房看完，便在客厅门口把她打发了，让她去转告霍克先生：临行前她想见他一面。

霍克先生面容大变，手里攥着一封电报，匆匆赶来。

===Original===
"From my wife, to say she'll be back tonight. She'll be at Waterloo in about half an hour from now," he said, holding up the brown envelope. "Now, Miss Brooke, what are we to do? I told you how much Mrs. Hawke objected to the investigation of this matter, and she is very—well—firm when she once says a thing, and—and—"

"Set your mind at rest," interrupted Loveday; "I have done all I wished to do within your walls, and the remainder of my investigation can be carried on just as well at Lynch Court or at my own private rooms."

"Done all you wished to do!" echoed Mr. Hawke in amazement; "why, you've not been an hour in the house, and do you mean to tell me you've found out anything about the necklace or the daggers?"

===Chinese===
"内人打来的，说她今夜便回。大约半个钟头后她就到滑铁卢了。"他举着那只棕色信封说，"如今怎么办呢，布鲁克小姐？我早跟您说过，霍克太太多么反对查这事，而她一旦开了口，便十分——嗯——十分坚决，而且——而且——"

"您把心放回肚里吧。"洛芙迪打断道，"我想在府上办的事都已办妥，余下的查访，在林奇大院或我自己的私寓里办也尽可一样。"

"想办的事都办妥了！"霍克先生惊诧地重复道，"怎么，您在屋里前后不到一个钟头，莫非您是说，关于那项链或那匕首，您已经查出了什么端倪？"

===Original===
"Don't ask me any questions just yet; I want you to answer one or two instead. Now, can you tell me anything about any letters Miss Monroe may have written or received since she has been in your house?"

"Yes, certainly, Sir George wrote to me very strongly about her correspondence, and begged me to keep a sharp eye on it, so as to nip in the bud any attempt to communicate with Danvers. So far, however, she does not appear to have made any such attempt. She is frankness itself over her correspondence. Every letter that has come addressed to her, she has shown either to me or to my wife, and they have one and all been letters from old friends of her father's, wishing to make her acquaintance now that she is in England. With regard to letter-writing, I am sorry to say she has a marked and most peculiar objection to it. Every one of the letters she has received, my wife tells, me, remain unanswered still. She has never once been seen, since she came to the house, with a pen in her hand. And if she wrote on the sly, I don't know how she would get her letters posted—she never goes outside the door by herself, and she would have no opportunity of giving them to any of the servants to post except Mrs. Hawke's maid, and she is beyond suspicion in such a matter. She has been well cautioned, and, in addition, is not the sort of person who would assist a young lady in carrying on a clandestine correspondence."

"I should imagine not! I suppose Miss Monroe has been present at the breakfast table each time that you have received your daggers through the post—you told me, I think, that they had come by the first post in the morning?"

===Chinese===
"暂且别问我什么，我倒想请您先回答一两桩。那么，关于门罗小姐住在府上期间所写或所收的信件，您可有什么可说的？"

"有，当然有。乔治爵士在信上极其郑重地嘱咐过她通信一事，求我严加留意，好把任何企图与丹弗斯通信的苗头掐灭在萌芽里。然而至今，她似乎并未有过这种企图。在通信一事上她坦荡得很。每一封寄给她的信，她都拿给我或我妻子看过，而且无一例外，都是她父亲旧友写来的，盼着既已到了英国，便与她结识。至于写信，我很遗憾地说，她对之有着一种明显而极其特别的嫌恶。据内人说，她收到的每一封信，至今都仍未回复。自打进了这个家门，谁也没见她手里拿过笔。她若要私下写信，我也不知她怎能寄出——她从不独自出门，除了霍克太太的女仆，她也没有机会把信交给任何仆人去寄，而那位女仆在这种事上毫无可疑之处。她受过严严的告诫，再说，她也不是会帮着一位小姐暗通款曲的那类人。"

"我想也不是！我猜您每次经由邮递收到那些匕首时，门罗小姐都在早餐桌前——我记得您好像说过，它们都是头一班早邮送来的吧？"

===Original===
"Yes; Miss Monroe is very punctual at meals, and has been present each time. Naturally, when I received such unpleasant missives, I made some sort of exclamation and then handed the thing round the table for inspection, and Miss Monroe was very much concerned to know who my secret enemy could be."

"No doubt. Now, Mr. Hawke, I have a very special request to make to you, and I hope you will be most exact in carrying it out."

"You may rely upon my doing so to the very letter."

===Chinese===
"是的；门罗小姐用餐极准时，每次都在场。自然，我收到这种令人不快的信件时，免不了要惊呼一声，然后把它在桌上传递给人传观，而门罗小姐也对我那暗中仇家是谁，颇为关切。"

"那自不待言。听着，霍克先生，我有一桩极其特别的请求要向您提，盼您务必一丝不苟地照办。"

"您尽可信赖我，定当字字照办。"

===Original===
"Thank you. If, then, you should receive by post tomorrow morning one of those big envelopes you already know the look of, and find that it contains a sketch of three, not two, drawn daggers—"

"Good gracious! what makes you think such a thing likely?" exclaimed Mr. Hawke, greatly disturbed. "Why am I to be persecuted in this way? Am I to take it for granted that I am a doomed man?"

He began to pace the room in a state of great excitement.

===Chinese===
"多谢。那么，要是您明早经由邮递，收到一只您已熟识的那种大信封，发现里面画着三把——不是两把——出鞘的匕首——"

"老天爷！您怎么以为这等事会发生？"霍克先生大为不安地嚷道，"我为何要受这般折磨？难道我就要认定自己是个在劫难逃之人了吗？"

他激动不已，开始在屋里来回踱步。

===Original===
"I don't think I would if I were you," answered Loveday calmly. "Pray let me finish. I want you to open the big envelope that may come to you by post tomorrow morning just you have opened the others—in full view of your family at the breakfast-table—and to hand round the sketch it may contain for inspection to your wife, your nephew and to Miss Monroe. Now, will you promise me to do this?"

"Oh, certainly; I should most likely have done so without any promising. But—but—I'm sure you'll understand that I feel myself to be in a peculiarly uncomfortable position, and I shall feel so very much obliged to you if you'll tell me—that is if you'll enter a little more fully into an explanation."

===Chinese===
"换作是我，我可不会这样想。"洛芙迪平静地答道，"请容我把话说完。我要您像拆开其余几封那样，拆开明早可能由邮递送达的那只大信封——在早餐桌上，当着您全家的面——并把里面可能装的那幅画传给您太太、您侄子和门罗小姐过目。您肯向我应承这一桩么？"

"哦，当然肯；就算不应承，我也多半会这么做的。只是——只是——我深信您能体谅，我自觉处境格外难堪，您若肯告诉我——也就是说，肯稍稍详尽地把底里说明一番，我感激不尽。"

===Original===
Loveday looked at her watch. "I should think Mrs. Hawke would be just at this moment arriving at Waterloo; I'm sure you'll be glad to see the last of me. Please come to me at my rooms in Gower Street tomorrow at twelve—here is my card. I shall then be able to enter into fuller explanations I hope. Goodbye."

The old gentleman showed her politely downstairs, and, as he shook hands with her at the front door, again asked, in a most emphatic manner, if she did not consider him to be placed in a "peculiarly unpleasant position."

Those last words at parting were to be the first with which he greeted her on the following morning when he presented himself at her rooms in Gower Street. They were, however, repeated in considerably more agitated a manner.

===Chinese===
洛芙迪看了看表。"我想霍克太太此刻正巧抵达滑铁卢；我敢说，您巴不得赶紧摆脱我呢。明日十二点，请到高尔街（Gower Street）我的寓所来找我——这是我的名片。届时我希望能向您作更详尽的说明。再见。"

老绅士客气地送她下楼，在前门与她握手道别时，又以一种极其着重的方式问她：她是否也认为他身处一种"格外难堪的境地"。

临别时这最后一句话，竟成了翌日清晨他登门造访她高尔街寓所时，对她开口的头一句。只是这回说出来时，激动得多了不知多少。

===Original===
"Was there ever a man in a more miserable position!" he exclaimed, as he took the chair that Loveday indicated. "I not only received the three daggers for which you prepared me, but I got an additional worry, for which I was totally unprepared. This morning, immediately after breakfast, Miss Monroe walked out of the house all by herself, and no one knows where she has gone. And the girl has never before been outside the door alone. It seems the servants saw her go out, but did not think it necessary to tell either me or Mrs. Hawke, feeling sure we must have been aware of the fact."

"So Mrs. Hawke has returned," said Loveday. "Well, I suppose you will be greatly surprised if I inform you that the young lady, who has so unceremoniously left your house, is at the present moment to be found at the Charing Cross Hotel, where she has engaged a private room in her real name of Miss Mary O'Grady."

"Eh! What! Private room! Real name O'Grady! I'm all bewildered!"

===Chinese===
"天底下可曾有过处境更悲惨的男人！"他在洛芙迪示意的那把椅子上落座，嚷道，"我不仅收到了您预先提醒我的那三把匕首，还凭空添了一桩烦心事，那是全然出乎我意料的。今早一吃过早饭，门罗小姐就独自走出了宅子，谁也不知她去了哪里。而这姑娘从前从不独自出门。据仆人们说，她们是看见她出去的，却觉得不必告诉我或霍克太太，以为我们一定知情。"

"这么说，霍克太太已经回来了。"洛芙迪说，"那么，我若告诉您，那位如此唐突地离开您府上的小姐，此刻就在查令十字饭店，而且用她的真名——玛丽·奥格雷迪小姐（Miss Mary O'Grady）——订了一间雅间，您怕是要大吃一惊吧。"

"啊！什么！雅间！真名奥格雷迪！我全糊涂了！"

===Original===
"It is a little bewildering; let me explain. The young lady whom you received into your house as the daughter of your old friend, was in reality the person engaged by Miss Monroe to fulfill the duties of her maid on board ship, after her native attendant had been landed at Malta. Her real name, as I have told you, is Mary O'Grady, and she has proved herself a valuable coadjutor to Miss Monroe in assisting her to carry out a programme, which she must have arranged with her lover, Mr. Danvers, before she left Peking."

"Eh! what!" again ejaculated Mr. Hawke; "how do you know all this? Tell me the whole story."

"I will tell you the whole story first, and then explain to you how I came to know it. From what has followed, it seems to me that Miss Monroe must have arranged with Mr. Danvers that he was to leave Peking within ten days of her so doing, travel by the route by which she came, and land at Plymouth, where he was to receive a note from her, apprising him of her whereabouts. So soon as she was on board ship, Miss Monroe appears to have set her wits to work with great energy; every obstacle to the carrying-out of her programme she appears to have met and conquered. Step number one was to get rid of her native maid, who, perhaps, might have been faithful to her master's interests and have proved troublesome. I have no doubt the poor woman suffered terribly from seasickness, as it was her first voyage, and I have equally no doubt that Miss Monroe worked on her fears, and persuaded her to land at Malta, and return to China by the next packet. Step number two was to find a suitable person, who for a consideration, would be willing to play the part of the Peking heiress among the heiress's friends in England, while the young lady herself arranged her private affairs to her own liking. That person was quickly found among the steerage passengers of the *Colombo* in Miss Mary O'Grady, who had come on board with her mother at Ceylon, and who, from the glimpse I had of her, must, I should conjecture, have been absent many years from the land of her birth. You know how cleverly this young lady has played her part in your house—how, without attracting attention to the matter, she has shunned the society of her father's old Chinese friends, who might be likely to involve her in embarrassing conversations; how she has avoided the use of pen and ink lest—"

===Chinese===
"是有点儿让人糊涂；容我说明。您接进家门、当作老友之女的那位小姐，其实是门罗小姐在本地女仆于马耳他登岸之后、在船上雇来履行女仆之职的那个人。她的真名，方才已说过，叫玛丽·奥格雷迪，事实证明，她是门罗小姐一个得力的帮手，协助她实行一项计划——这计划，必是她离京之前就与她的情人丹弗斯先生商定好的。"

"啊！什么！"霍克先生又失声叫道，"这些您是怎么知道的？把事情原原本本讲给我听。"

"我先原原本本讲给您听，再说明我是如何知道的。从其后发生的事来看，我想门罗小姐必是和丹弗斯先生约定：他要在她离京之后十日之内也离开北京，沿她来时的路线旅行，在普利茅斯登岸，在那里收到她的一封短笺，告知他她的下落。门罗小姐一上了船，似乎便使出浑身解数、动足了脑筋；推行计划路上的每一道障碍，她似乎都一一迎刃而解。第一步，是打发掉那位本地女仆——此人也许会对主人忠心耿耿、徒生枝节。那可怜的妇人是头一回远航，晕船晕得死去活来，这一点我毫不怀疑；我也同样毫不怀疑，门罗小姐趁势撩拨她的恐惧，劝她在马耳他登岸，搭下一班班轮返回中国。第二步，是物色一个合适的人，看在钱的份上，肯在英国扮演这位北京继承人，周旋于继承人的友人之间，而那位小姐本人则好从从容容地安排自己的私事。此人在'科伦坡号'的统舱乘客中很快便找到了，便是玛丽·奥格雷迪小姐——她是与母亲一道在锡兰（Ceylon）上船的，而据我所见她那一面来推测，她离开故土想必已有许多年了。您是知道的，这位小姐在您府上把戏演得何等巧妙——她如何在不引人注目的情形下，躲避着她父亲那些旧日中国友人的来往，以免被卷入尴尬的盘问；她又如何避用笔墨，唯恐——"

===Original===
"Yes, yes," interrupted Mr. Hawke; "but, my dear Miss Brooke, wouldn't it be as well for you and me to go at once to the Charing Cross Hotel, and get all the information we can out of her respecting Miss Monroe and her movements—she may be bolting, you know?"

"I do not think she will. She is waiting there patiently for an answer to a telegram she dispatched more than two hours ago to her mother, Mrs. O'Grady, at 14, Woburn Place, Cork."

"Dear me! dear me! How is it possible for you to know all this."

===Chinese===
"是，是，"霍克先生打断道，"可是，亲爱的布鲁克小姐，您和我何不马上同去查令十字饭店，把她所知关于门罗小姐及其行踪的一切尽量套出来——您要知道，她没准儿正在溜之大吉呢？"

"我想她不会的。她此刻正在那儿耐心等着回电——两个多钟头前，她给在科克沃本坊（Woburn Place）十四号的母亲奥格雷迪太太（Mrs. O'Grady）发了一封电报。"

"天哪！天哪！您怎么可能知道这一切。"

===Original===
"Oh, that last little fact was simply a matter of astuteness on the part of the man whom I have deputed to watch the young lady's movements today. Other details, I assure you, in this somewhat intricate case, have been infinitely more difficult to get at. I think I have to thank those 'drawn daggers,' that caused you so much consternation, for having, in the first instance, put me on the right track."

"Ah‑h," said Mr. Hawke, drawing a long breath; "now we come to the daggers! I feel sure you are going to set my mind at rest on that score."

"I hope so. Would it surprise you very much to be told that it was I who sent to you those three daggers this morning?"

===Chinese===
"哦，最后这一桩小事实，无非是我今天派去监视那位小姐行踪的人机灵罢了。我向您保证，这桩颇为曲折的案子里，其余的细节查起来，可要难上千百倍。我想，倒要多谢那些害您大惊失色的'出鞘的匕首'，是最先把它们引上了正路。"

"啊——"霍克先生长长地舒了一口气，"这回总算说到匕首了！我深信您这就要让我在这方面放下心来了。"

"但愿如此。我若告诉您，今早那三把匕首是我寄给您的，您会非常吃惊吗？"

===Original===
"You! Is it possible?"

===Chinese===
"您！这可能吗？"

===Original===
"Yes, they were sent by me, and for a reason that I will presently explain to you. But let me begin at the beginning. Those roughly-drawn sketches, that to you suggested terrifying ideas of blood-shedding and violence, to my mind were open to a more peaceful and commonplace explanation. They appeared to me to suggest the herald's office rather than the armoury; the cross fitchée of the knight's shield rather than the poniard with which the members of secret societies are supposed to render their recalcitrant brethren familiar. Now, if you will look at these sketches again, you will see what I mean." Here Loveday produced from her writing-table the missives which had so greatly disturbed Mr. Hawke's peace of mind. "To begin with, the blade of the dagger of common life is, as a rule, at least two-thirds of the weapon in length; in this sketch, what you would call the blade, does not exceed the hilt in length. Secondly, please note the absence of guard for the hand. Thirdly, let me draw your attention to the squareness of what you considered the hilt of the weapon, and what, to my mind, suggested the upper portion of a crusader's cross. No hand could grip such a hilt as the one outlined here. After your departure yesterday, I drove to the British Museum, and there consulted a certain valuable work on heraldry, which has more than once done me good service. There I found my surmise substantiated in a surprising manner. Among the illustrations of the various crosses borne on armorial shields, I found one that had been taken by Henri d'Anvers from his own armorial bearings, for his crest when he joined the Crusaders under Edward I, and which has since been handed down as the crest of the Danvers family. This was an important item of information to me. Here was someone in Cork sending to your house, on two several occasions, the crest of the Danvers family; with what object it would be difficult to say, unless it were in some sort a communication to someone in your house. With my mind full of this idea, I left the Museum and drove next to the office of the P. and O. Company, and requested to have given me the list of the passengers who arrived by the *Colombo*. I found this list to be a remarkably small one; I suppose people, if possible, avoid crossing the Bay of Biscay during the Equinoxes. The only passengers who landed at Plymouth besides Miss Monroe, I found, were a certain Mrs. and Miss O'Grady, steerage passengers who had gone on board at Ceylon on their way home from Australia. Their name, together with their landing at Plymouth, suggested the possibility that Cork might be their destination. After this I asked to see the list of the passengers who arrived by the packet following the *Colombo*, telling the clerk who attended to me that I was on the lookout for the arrival of a friend. In that second list of arrivals I quickly found my friend—William Wentworth Danvers by name."

===Chinese===
"是的，是我寄的，至于缘由，我待会儿便向您说明。请容我从头说起。那些粗粗画就的图样，在您看来意味着流血与暴行的可怖联想，在我看来却容许一个更为平和、更为寻常的解释。它们似乎在暗示纹章院，而非兵器库；暗示骑士盾牌上的尖头十字，而非秘密结社成员据称用以让桀骜同伙领教的那种匕首。喏，您若把这些图样再看一遍，便会明白我的意思。"说到这里，洛芙迪从写字台上取出那些曾大大搅扰霍克先生心境的信件。"其一，寻常生活中的匕首，其刀身一般至少占全器的三分之二；而在这幅画里，您称之为刀身的部分，并不比刀柄长。其二，请注意图中没有护手。其三，请您留意您所以为的刀柄那种方正的形状——在我看来，那分明是十字军十字架的上半截。世上没有哪只手能握得住这里描出来的这样一把刀柄。昨日您离去之后，我驱车前往大英博物馆（British Museum），在那里查阅了一部关于纹章学的珍贵典籍，它不止一次为我效过劳。在那里，我的揣测以一种令人惊叹的方式得到了印证。在饰于盾徽之上的种种十字插图中，我找到了一种，乃是由亨利·丹弗斯（Henri d'Anvers）取自自家的盾徽、作为他加入爱德华一世（Edward I）麾下十字军时的顶饰，自此便作为丹弗斯家族（Danvers family）的家徽世代相传。这对我来说是一条重要的情报。原来是科克有人两度将丹弗斯家族的家徽寄到您府上；所为何来，殊难断言，除非这算作是与您府上某人之间的一种通信。我脑子里装满这一念头，离开了博物馆，接着驱车前往半岛东方轮船公司办事处，请他们把'科伦坡号'抵达旅客的名单拿给我看。我发现这份名单小得出奇；我想人们若有可能，总会避免在春秋分时节横渡比斯开湾（Bay of Biscay）。在普利茅斯登岸的旅客，除门罗小姐之外，只有某位奥格雷迪太太和奥格雷迪小姐——两名统舱乘客，她们是在锡兰上船、从澳大利亚取道返英的。她们这个姓氏，再加上又是在普利茅斯登岸，便令人想到科克说不定正是她们的目的地。这之后，我又请他们把'科伦坡号'之后那班班轮的抵达旅客名单拿给我看，对那位招待我的办事员说，我在留心一位朋友的到来。在那第二份抵达名单里，我很快就找到了我的'朋友'——名叫威廉·温特沃斯·丹弗斯（William Wentworth Danvers）。"

===Original===
"No! The effrontery! How dared he! In his own name, too!"

"Well, you see, a plausible pretext for leaving Peking could easily be invented by him—the death of a relative, the illness of a father or mother. And Sir George, though he might dislike the idea of the young man going to England so soon after his daughter's departure, and may, perhaps, write to you by the next mail on the matter, was utterly powerless to prevent his so doing. This young man, like Miss Monroe and the O'Gradys, also landed at Plymouth. I had only arrived so far in my investigation when I went to your house yesterday afternoon. By chance, as I waited a few minutes in your drawing-room, another important item of information was acquired. A fragment of conversation between your nephew and the supposed Miss Monroe fell upon my ear, and one word spoken by the young lady convinced me of her nationality. That one word was the monosyllable 'Hush.'"

===Chinese===
"不！竟有这般厚颜无耻！他怎敢！还是用自己的真名！"

"您想，离京的一个像样借口，他大可轻而易举地编造出来——什么亲戚去世啦，父母抱恙啦。而乔治爵士纵然不喜欢这年轻人在自己女儿动身之后这么快就跑去英国，也许还会趁下一班邮船写信给您过问此事，可要阻止他成行，爵士却是全然无能为力。这个年轻人，和门罗小姐、奥格雷迪母女一样，也是在普利茅斯登岸的。我昨天下午登门造访时，查访才推进到这一步。我恰巧在您客厅里候了那么几分钟，便又得到了一条重要情报。您侄子与那位假冒的门罗小姐之间的一段话，飘进了我的耳朵，而那小姐口中的一个词，便叫我看清了她的国别。那个词，便是单音节的'Hush'（嘘）。"

===Original===
"No! You surprise me!"

"Have you never noted the difference between the 'hush' of an Englishman and that of an Irishman? The former begins his 'hush' with a distinct aspirate, the latter with as distinct a *W*. That *W* is a mark of his nationality which he never loses. The unmitigated 'whist' may lapse into a 'whish' when he is is transplanted to another soil, and the 'whish' may in course of time pass into a 'whush,' but to the distinct aspirate of the English 'hush,' he never attains. Now Miss O'Grady's was as pronounced a 'whush' as it was possible for the lips of a Hibernian to utter."

"And from that you concluded that Mary O'Grady was playing the part of Miss Monroe in my house?"

===Chinese===
"不会吧！这可叫我吃惊！"

"您难道从未留意过英国人的'hush'与爱尔兰人的'hush'之间有何分别么？前者发'hush'时，起首是一个清晰的送气音；后者起首则是一个同样清晰的 W 音。那个 W，正是他身上永不会磨灭的民族印记。那彻头彻尾的'whist'，一旦移植到异乡的土壤，或许会渐次演作'whish'，那'whish'日久天长，又会变成'whush'，可他无论如何也达不到英国人'hush'那种清晰的送气音。而奥格雷迪小姐那一句，乃是爱尔兰人（Hibernian）的双唇所能发出的最为地道的'whush'了。"

"单凭这一点，您便断定玛丽·奥格雷迪在我府上扮演的是门罗小姐了？"

===Original===
"Not immediately. My suspicions were excited, certainly; and when I went up to her room, in company with Mrs. Hawke's maid, those suspicions were confirmed. The orderliness of that room was something remarkable. Now, there is the orderliness of a lady in the arrangement of her room, and the orderliness of a maid, and the two things, believe me, are widely different. A lady, who has no maid, and who has the gift of orderliness, will put things away when done with, and so leave her room a picture of neatness. I don't think, however, it would for a moment occur to her to pull things so as to be conveniently ready for her to use the next time she dresses in that room. This would be what a maid, accustomed to arrange a room for her mistress's use, would do mechanically. Miss Monroe's room was the neatness of a maid—not of a lady, and I was assured by Mrs. Hawke's maid that it was a neatness accomplished by her own hands. As I stood there, looking at that room, the whole conspiracy—if I may so call it—little by little pieced itself together, and became plain to me. Possibilities quickly grew into probabilities, and these probabilities once admitted, brought other suppositions in their train. Now, supposing that Miss Monroe and Mary O'Grady had agreed to change places, the Peking heiress, for the time being, occupying Mary O'Grady's place in the humble home at Cork and vice versa, what means of communicating with each other had they arranged? How was Mary O'Grady to know when she might lay aside her assumed role and go back to her mother's house. There was no denying the necessity for such communication; the difficulties in its way must have been equally obvious to the two girls. Now, I think we must admit that we must credit these young women with having hit upon a very clever way of meeting those difficulties. An anonymous and startling missive sent to you would be bound to be mentioned in the house, and in this way a code of signals might be set up between them that could not direct suspicion to them. In this connection, the Danvers crest, which it is possible that they mistook for a dagger, suggested itself naturally, for no doubt Miss Monroe had many impressions of it on her lover's letters. As I thought over these things, it occurred to me that possibly dagger (or cross) number one was sent to notify the safe arrival of Miss Monroe and Mrs. O'Grady at Cork. The two daggers or crosses you subsequently received were sent on the day of Mr. Danvers's arrival at Plymouth, and were, I should say, sketched by his hand. Now, was it not within the bounds of likelihood that Miss Monroe's marriage to this young man, and the consequent release of Mary O'Grady from the onerous part she was playing, might be notified to her by the sending of three such crosses or daggers to you. The idea no sooner occurred to me than I determined to act upon it, forestall the sending of this latest communication, and watch the result. Accordingly, after I left your house yesterday, I had a sketch made of three daggers of crosses exactly similar to those you had already received, and had it posted to you so that you would get it by the first post. I told off one of our staff at Lynch Court to watch your house, and gave him special directions to follow and report on Miss O'Grady's movements throughout the day. The results I anticipated quickly came to pass. About half-past nine this morning the man sent a telegram to me from your house to the Charing Cross Hotel, and furthermore had ascertained that she had since despatched a telegram, which (possibly by following the hotel servant who carried it to the telegraph office), he had overheard was addressed to Mrs. O'Grady, at Woburn Place, Cork. Since I received this information an altogether remarkable cross-firing of telegrams has been going backwards and forwards along the wires to Cork."

===Chinese===
"并非立刻断定。我的疑心固然被撩起了；而当我由霍克太太的女仆陪着上楼进了她的房间，那些疑虑便得到了证实。那间屋子的整洁程度，着实不寻常。须知，一位女士整理房间的整洁，与一名女仆的整洁，两码事，请相信我，二者截然不同。一位没有女仆、又天生爱整洁的女士，会把用过的东西随手收好，使房间呈现出一派整洁的图景。然而，我可不信她会动心思把物件一一挪到方便她下次在这间屋梳妆时随手取用的位置。这种事，才会是一名惯于为女主人收拾房间的女仆下意识做出的举动。门罗小姐那间屋子，是女仆式的整洁——而非女士式的，而霍克太太的女仆也向我保证，那份整洁正是出自她本人之手。我站在那里，望着那间屋子，整桩阴谋——姑且这么叫它吧——便一点一点在我眼前自行拼合起来，水落石出。种种可能性迅即化作种种或然性，这些或然性一旦成立，便又牵出了旁的推测。那么，假定门罗小姐与玛丽·奥格雷迪约定互易身份：那位北京的继承人暂且占了玛丽·奥格雷迪在科克那寒舍里的位置，反之亦然——她们之间又安排了怎样的联络之法呢？玛丽·奥格雷迪又怎会得知，何时可以卸下这乔装的角色、回母亲家去？这种联络的必要不容否认；而其中的难处，对两个姑娘而言想必也 equally 显而易见。说到这里，我想我们必须承认，得归功于这两位年轻女子想出了一个非常巧妙的法子来应对这些难处。一封匿名而骇人的信函寄给您，注定会在家中被人提起，如此一来，她们之间便可以此建立一套暗号，而又不会招来对她们的怀疑。想到这里，丹弗斯家的家徽便自然而然浮上心头——她们说不定把它错认成匕首了——因为她情人信上无数枚火漆印记，门罗小姐自然见过不少。我顺着这些念头想下去，便觉得头一把匕首（或曰十字）也许正是为了通报门罗小姐与奥格雷迪太太平安抵达科克。您其后收到的那两把匕首或十字，则是在丹弗斯先生抵达普利茅斯那天寄出的，我敢说，是出自他的手笔。那么，门罗小姐与此青年成婚、玛丽·奥格雷迪由此得以卸下她所扮演的繁重角色一事，再给您寄上三枚这样的十字或匕首以为通报，难道不在情理之中么？这念头刚一冒头，我便决意依计而行，抢先一步把这一封最新的通信寄出去，再看结果。于是，昨日离开您府上之后，我便让人画了三把匕首或十字，与您已收到的那些一模一样，并赶在头班早邮之前寄给了您。我又从林奇大院的同人里拨出一人监视您府上，特意嘱咐他跟踪并报告奥格雷迪小姐全天的动向。我所预料的那些结果，很快便应验了。今早九点半左右，那人从您府上拍了一封电报到查令十字饭店给我，而且进一步查明，她随后又拍发了一封电报，他（大概是跟踪了把电报送去电报局的那名旅馆侍者）偷听出那是寄给科克沃本坊奥格雷迪太太的。自从我接到这一情报，电报局通往科克的电线上，便展开了一场堪称奇观的电报交叉对射。"

===Original===
"A cross-firing of telegrams! I do not understand."

"In this way. So soon as I knew Mrs. O'Grady's address I telegraphed to her, in her daughter's name, desiring her to address her reply to 1154 Gower Street, not to Charing Cross Hotel. About three-quarters of an hour afterwards I received in reply this telegram, which I am sure you will read with interest."

Here Loveday handed a telegram—one of several that lay on her writing-table—to Mr. Hawke.

===Chinese===
"电报交叉对射！我不明白。"

"是这样的。我一得知奥格雷迪太太的地址，便以她女儿的名义给她拍了一封电报，请她把回电发到高尔街 1154 号，而非查令十字饭店。约莫三刻钟之后，我收到了这封回电，我相信您读了一定会有兴趣。"

说着，洛芙迪把一封电报——写字台上那几封里的其中一封——递给了霍克先生。

===Original===
He opened it and read aloud as follows:

"Am puzzled. Why such hurry? Wedding took place this morning. You will receive signal as agreed tomorrow. Better return to Tavistock Square for the night."

"The wedding took place this morning," repeated Mr. Hawke blankly. "My poor old friend! It will break his heart."

===Chinese===
他拆开封，朗声念道：

"困惑。何必如此匆忙？婚礼已于今晨举行。明日你将收到约定的信号。今夜最好回塔维斯托克广场过夜。"

"婚礼已于今晨举行，"霍克先生茫然地重复道，"我可怜的老友！这会叫他心碎的。"

===Original===
"Now that the thing is done past recall we must hope he will make the best of it," said Loveday. "In reply to this telegram," she went on, "I sent another, asking as to the movements of the bride and bridegroom, and got in reply this:"

Here she read aloud as follows:

"They will be at Plymouth tomorrow night; at Charing Cross Hotel the next day, as agreed."

===Chinese===
"事已至此，无可挽回，我们只能盼他能往好处想了。"洛芙迪说，"作为对这封电报的回复，"她接着说，"我又发了一封，打听新婚夫妇的行踪，得到了这封回电："

说着她朗声念道：

"明日夜间在普利茅斯；次日按约定在查令十字饭店。"

===Original===
"So, Mr. Hawke," she added, "if you wish to see your old friend's daughter and tell her what you think of the part she has played, all you will have to do will be to watch the arrival of the Plymouth trains."

"Miss O'Grady has called to see a lady and gentleman," said a maid at that moment entering.

"Miss O'Grady!" repeated Mr. Hawke in astonishment.

"Ah, yes, I telegraphed to her, just before you came in, to come here to meet a lady and gentlemen, and she, no doubt thinking that she would find here the newly-married pair, has, you see, lost no time in complying with my request. Show the lady in."

===Chinese===
"所以，霍克先生，"她接着说，"您若想见一见老友的女儿，把她演的这出戏数落一番，您只需去盯着从普利茅斯开来的火车到站便是。"

"奥格雷迪小姐来访，要见一位先生和一位女士。"这时一名女仆走进来说。

"奥格雷迪小姐！"霍克先生诧异地重复道。

"啊，是的，就在您进来之前，我拍电报叫她到这儿来见一位先生和一位女士，而她——无疑以为在此能见到那对新婚夫妇——您瞧，一刻也没耽搁就来赴我的约了。请那位小姐进来。"

===Original===
"It's all so intricate—so bewildering," said Mr. Hawke, as he lay back in his chair. "I can scarcely get it all into my head."

His bewilderment, however, was nothing compared with that of Miss O'Grady, when she entered the room and found herself face to face with her late guardian, instead of the radiant bride and bridegroom whom she had expected to meet.

She stood silent in the middle of the room, looking the picture of astonishment and distress.

===Chinese===
"这桩桩件件实在太曲折——太叫人糊涂了。"霍克先生仰靠在椅子上说，"我简直没法把它都装进脑子里。"

然而他的糊涂，比起奥格雷迪小姐进门后、发觉与自己面对面的竟是旧日的监护人，而非她原以为会见到的那容光焕发的一对新人时所感到的糊涂来，便算不得什么了。

她默然立在屋子正中，那副神情活脱是一幅惊愕与窘迫的写照。

===Original===
Mr. Hawke also seemed a little at a loss for words, so Loveday took the initiative.

"Please sit down," she said, placing a chair for the girl. "Mr. Hawke and I have sent to you in order to ask you a few questions. Before doing so, however, let me tell you that the whole of your conspiracy with Miss Monroe has been brought to light, and the best thing you can do, if you want your share in it treated leniently, will be to answer our questions as fully and truthfully as possible."

The girl burst into tears. "It was all Miss Monroe's fault from beginning to end," she sobbed. "Mother didn't want to do it—I didn't want to—to go into a gentleman's house and pretend to be what I was not. And we didn't want her hundred pounds—"

===Chinese===
霍克先生似乎也有点不知如何开口，于是洛芙迪先开了腔。

"请坐。"她说着，为姑娘摆好一把椅子，"霍克先生和我请您来，是为了问您几桩事。不过问之前，我得先告诉您：您与门罗小姐整桩的密谋都已败露，您若想自己所干的这份得到从宽发落，最好是尽可能详尽、如实地回答我们的问题。"

那姑娘泪如泉涌。"这事从头到尾都是门罗小姐的错。"她抽抽搭搭地说，"妈本不愿干的——我也不愿——不愿进一位绅士的家门，装成我不是的那个人。而且我们也不稀罕她那一百英镑——"

===Original===
Here sobs checked her speech.

"Oh," said Loveday contemptuously, "so you were to have a hundred pounds for your share in this fraud, were you?"

"We didn't want to take it," said the girl, between hysterical bursts of tears; "but Miss Monroe said if we didn't help her someone else would, and so I agreed to—"

===Chinese===
说到这里，呜咽哽住了她的话。

"哦，"洛芙迪轻蔑地说，"这么说，你们在这场骗局里的那份，是要得一百英镑的了？"

"我们本不愿拿的，"姑娘在歇斯底里的哭泣间歇里说道，"可门罗小姐说，我们要是不帮她，自有旁人肯帮，于是我便答应——"

===Original===
"I think," interrupted Loveday, "that you can tell us very little that we do not already know about what you agreed to do. What we want you to tell us is what has been done with Miss Monroe's diamond necklace—who has possession of it now?"

The girl's sobs and tears redoubled. "I've had nothing to do with the necklace—it has never been in my possession," she sobbed. "Miss Monroe gave it to Mr. Danvers two or three months before she left Peking, and he sent it on to some people he knew in Hong Kong, diamond merchants, who lent him money on it. Decastro, Miss Monroe said, was the name of these people."

===Chinese===
"我想，"洛芙迪打断道，"关于您答应做的事，您能告诉我们的，已经没多少是我们尚不知情的了。我们要您告诉我们的是：门罗小姐那串钻石项链如今下落如何——眼下在谁手里？"

姑娘的哭啼越发厉害了。"那项链与我不相干——它从未到过我手里。"她抽噎道，"门罗小姐在离京前两三个月就把它给了丹弗斯先生，他把它转寄给他在香港认识的几个人——做钻石生意的——他们以此借了一笔钱给他。门罗小姐说，那些人姓德卡斯特罗（Decastro）。"

===Original===
"Decastro, diamond merchant, Hong Kong. I should think that would be sufficient address," said Loveday, entering it in a ledger; "and I suppose Mr. Danvers retained part of that money for his own use and travelling expenses, and handed the remainder to Miss Monroe to enable her to bribe such creatures as you and your mother, to practice a fraud that ought to land both of you in jail."

The girl grew deadly white. "Oh, don't do that—don't send us to prison!" she implored, clasping her hands together. "We haven't touched a penny of Miss Monroe's money yet, and we don't want to touch a penny, if you'll only let us off! Oh, pray, pray, pray be merciful!"

Loveday looked at Mr. Hawke.

===Chinese===
"德卡斯特罗，钻石商，香港。我想这个地址便足够了。"洛芙迪说着，把它记入账册，"我猜，丹弗斯先生留下其中一部分钱作己用和盘缠，把余下的交给门罗小姐，好让她去收买您和您母亲这等人，来演一出本该把你们俩都送进大牢的骗局。"

姑娘霎时面如死灰。"哦，别这样——别把我们送进牢里！"她合掌哀求道，"门罗小姐的钱我们一文都还没碰，我们也不愿碰一文，只要您肯放我们一马！哦，求求您，求求您，求求您大发慈悲！"

洛芙迪看了看霍克先生。

===Original===
He rose from his chair. "I think the best thing you can do," he said, "will be to get back home to your mother at Cork as quickly as possible, and advise her never to play such a risky game again. Have you any money in your purse? No—well then here's some for you, and lose no time in getting home. It will be best for Miss Monroe—Mrs. Danvers I mean—to come to my house and claim her own property there. At any rate, there it will remain until she does so."

As the girl, with incoherent expressions of gratitude, left the room, he turned to Loveday.

"I should like to have consulted Mrs. Hawke before arranging matters in this way," he said a little hesitatingly; "but still, I don't see that I could have done otherwise."

===Chinese===
他从椅子上站起。"我想，您最好还是尽快回科克您母亲那儿去，"他说，"并劝她今后再别玩这种铤而走险的把戏。您钱包里有钱吗？没有——喏，这些给您，赶快动身回家吧。最好是让门罗小姐——我是说丹弗斯太太——上我府上去认领她自己的财物。无论如何，在彼之前，东西暂存我这里。"

姑娘语无伦次地道着谢，出了屋子；他随即转向洛芙迪。

"我本想先与霍克太太商量一下，再如此了结此事，"他略带迟疑地说，"不过，我看我也实在没有别的法子。"

===Original===
"I feel sure Mrs. Hawke will approve what you have done when she hears all the circumstance of the case," said Loveday.

"And," continued the old clergyman, "when I write to Sir George, as, of course, I must immediately, I shall advise him to make the best of a bad bargain, now that the thing is done. 'Past cure should be past care;' eh, Miss Brooke? And, think! what a narrow escape my nephew, Jack, has had!"

===Chinese===
"我深信，霍克太太一旦听悉此案的全部情由，定会赞同您这般处置的。"洛芙迪说。

"再者，"老牧师接着说，"待我写信给乔治爵士——此事我自当立刻去办——我会劝他既已成事，便将就着往好处想吧。'病入膏肓便无须再忧'；是吧，布鲁克小姐？您再想想，我那侄子杰克，险些儿栽得多惨呐！"

## Missing! / 失踪！

===Original===
"Now, Miss Brooke, if this doesn't inspire you I don't know what will," said Mr. Dyer. And, taking up a handbill that lay upon his writing-table, he read aloud as follows:

"Five hundred pounds reward.—Missing, since Monday, September 20th, Irené, only daughter of Richard Golding, of Langford Hall, Langford Cross, Leicestershire. Age 18, height five feet seven; dark hair and eyes, olive complexion, small features; was dressed when she left home in dark blue serge walking costume, with white straw sailor hat trimmed with cream ribbon. Jewellery worn: plain gold brooch, leather strap bracelet holding small watch, and on the third finger of left hand a marquise ring consisting of one large diamond, surrounded with twelve rubies. Was last seen about ten o'clock on the morning of the 20th leaving Langford Hall Park for the high road leading to Langford Cross. The above reward will be paid to anyone giving such information as will lead to the young lady's restoration to her home; or portions of the reward, according to the value of the information received. All communications to be addressed to the Chief Inspector, Police Station, Langford Cross."

===Chinese===
"好了，布鲁克小姐，要是这桩案子还激不起您的热忱，那我就不知道还有什么能了，"戴尔先生说道。说着，他拿起书桌上放着的一张告示，朗声念道：

"悬赏五百英镑。——自九月二十日，星期一起失踪：伊雷娜（Irené），理查德·戈尔丁（Richard Golding）之独女，家住莱斯特郡（Leicestershire）朗福德克罗斯（Langford Cross）朗福德庄（Langford Hall）。年十八，身高五英尺七英寸；深色头发、深色眼眸，肤色微现橄榄，五官纤巧；离家时所穿为深蓝色哔叽外出裙服，头戴缀奶白色缎带的白草编水手帽。所佩饰物：素金胸针一枚、系有小块手表的皮带手镯一只，左手无名指上戴一枚椭圆戒（marquise ring），中央一颗大钻石，四周环镶十二颗红宝石。最后被人见到，是在二十日上午十时前后，正离开朗福德庄园，走向通往朗福德克罗斯的大路。凡提供线索、使该年轻女士得以归家者，将获上述全额赏金；亦可按所提供线索之价值，支取部分赏金。一切来函请寄：朗福德克罗斯警察局，警长收。"

===Original===
"Was last seen on the 20th of September!" exclaimed Loveday, as Mr. Dyer finished reading. "Why, that is ten days ago! Do you mean to tell me that the reward has not stimulated the energies of the local police and long ago put them on the traces of the missing girl?"

"It has stimulated their energies, not a doubt, for the local papers teem with accounts of the way in which the whole country about Langford has been turned upside down generally. Every river, far and near has been dragged; every wood scoured; every railway official at every station for miles round has been driven nearly mad with persistent cross-questioning. But all to no purpose. The affair remains as great a mystery as ever. The girl, as the handbill says, was seen leaving the Park for the high road by some children who chanced to be passing, but after that she seems to have disappeared as completely as if the earth had opened to receive her."

===Chinese===
"最后一次见到她，竟还是九月二十号那天！"戴尔先生一念完，洛芙迪便失声叫道。"哎呀，那已经是十天前了！莫非您要告诉我，这笔赏金竟没能激起当地警方的干劲，让他们早早循着线索把失踪的姑娘找回来？"

"干劲倒确是激起出来了，这毋庸怀疑，因为本地报纸上满是种种报道，说朗福德一带整个乡野如何被翻了个人仰马翻。远近每一条河都被捞过，每一片树林都被梳过，方圆几英里内每一个车站上的铁路人员，都被没完没了的反复盘问逼得快要发疯。可全然无济于事。这桩案子至今仍是个天大的谜。正如告示所言，有几个孩子碰巧路过，看见姑娘离开庄园走向大路，可自那以后，她便消失得彻彻底底，仿佛大地裂开一道口子把她吞了下去。"

===Original===
"Cannot her own people suggest any possible motive for her thus suddenly leaving home?"

"It appears not; they seem absolutely incapable of assigning any reason whatever for her extraordinary conduct. This morning I received a letter from Inspector Ramsay, asking me to get you to take up the case. Mr. Golding will not have the slightest objection to your staying at the Hall and thoroughly investigating the matter. Ramsay says it is just possible that they have concentrated too much attention on the search outside the house, and that a promising field for investigation may lie within."

===Chinese===
"她自家人就一点都猜不出她这般突然离家的缘由吗？"

"看来猜不出；对她这般异乎寻常的举动，他们似乎完全说不出半点理由。今早我收到拉姆齐巡官（Inspector Ramsay）的一封信，求我请你接手此案。戈尔丁先生对于你住进庄园、彻底调查此事，绝不会有一丝一毫的异议。拉姆齐说，他们也许把注意力过多地集中在了宅子外面的搜寻上，而大有可为的调查方向，说不定正藏在宅子里面。"

===Original===
"They should have thought of that before," said Loveday sharply. "I hope you declined the case. Here's the country inspector to the backbone! He'll keep a case in his own hands so long as there's a chance of success; then, when it becomes practically hopeless, hand it over to you just to keep his own failure in countenance by yours."

"Ye-es," said Mr. Dyer, slowly. "I suppose that's about it. But still, business has been slack of late—expenses are heavy—if you thought there was a ghost of a chance—"

"After ten days!" interrupted Miss Brooke, "when the house has settled down into routine, and everyone has his story cut and dried, and all sorts of small details have been falsified or smudged over! Criminal cases are like fevers; they should be taken in hand within twenty-four hours."

===Chinese===
"他们早该想到这一层了，"洛芙迪语气尖利地说。"我盼着你回绝了这案子。这可真是地道的乡村巡官做派！只要还有一线成功的指望，他就把案子死死攥在自己手里；等到实在毫无希望了，才转手交给你，无非是想拿你的成功来给自己的失败遮遮脸罢了。"

"是——啊，"戴尔先生慢吞吞地说，"我想就是这么回事。不过话又说回来，近来生意清淡——开销又大——要是你觉得还有一线哪怕是蛛丝马迹的希望——"

"都过去十天了！"布鲁克小姐打断他，"宅子里早已安于日常，人人都把说辞编得滴水不漏，种种细枝末节不是被篡改就是被糊弄过去了！刑案这东西就跟发烧一样，必须在二十四小时之内着手才好。"

===Original===
"Yes, I know," said Mr. Dyer irritably, "but still, as I said before, business is slack—"

"Oh, well, if I'm to go, I'm to go, and there's an end of it," said Loveday resignedly. "I only say it would have been better, for the credit of the office, if you had declined such a hopeless affair. Tell me a little about this Mr. Richard Golding, who and what he is."

Mr. Dyer's temper grew serene again.

"He is a very wealthy man," he answered, "an Australian merchant; came over to England about a dozen years ago, and settled down at Langford Hall. He had, however, been living in Italy for some six or seven years previously. On his way home from Australia he did Italy, as so many Australians do, fell in love with a pretty girl, whom he met at Naples, and married her, and by her had this one child, Irené, who is causing such a sensation at the present moment."

===Chinese===
"是，我知道，"戴尔先生有些不耐烦地说，"可是，就像我方才说的，生意清淡——"

"罢了，既然非去不可，那就去，没什么好说的了，"洛芙迪认命似的说。"我不过是说，为了事务所的体面，你当初要是回绝了这么一桩毫无希望的案子就好了。跟我讲讲这位理查德·戈尔丁先生吧，他是什么人、做什么的。"

戴尔先生的脾气又和缓下来。

"他是个极有钱的人，"他答道，"做澳大利亚买卖的商人；大约十二年前来到英国，在朗福德庄安顿下来。不过在那之前，他先是在意大利住了六七年。他从澳大利亚回程时，像许多澳大利亚人那样顺道游历了意大利，在那不勒斯（Naples）结识了一位漂亮姑娘，爱上了她，娶她为妻，与她生下了这一个孩子——伊雷娜，正是眼下闹出这么大动静的这位。"

===Original===
"Is this Italian wife living?"

"No, she died just before Mr. Golding came to England. He has not yet married again, but I hear is on the point of so doing. The lady he contemplates making the second Mrs. Golding is a certain Mrs. Greenhow, a widow, who for the past year or so has acted as chaperon to his daughter and housekeeper to himself."

"It is possible that Miss Irené was not too well pleased at the idea of having a stepmother."

"Such is the fact. From all accounts she and her future stepmother did not get on at all well together. Miss Irené has a very hasty, imperious temper, and Mrs. Greenhow seems to have been quite incapable of holding her own with her. She was to have left the hall this month to make her preparations for the approaching wedding; the young lady's disappearance, however, has naturally brought matters to a standstill."

===Chinese===
"这位意大利太太还在世吗？"

"不在了，她就在戈尔丁先生来英国之前去世了。他至今尚未续弦，不过听说正打算这么做。他有意使之成为第二任戈尔丁夫人的，是位格林豪太太（Mrs. Greenhow），一位寡妇，过去一年里一直替他照管女儿、做他宅子里的管家。"

"伊雷娜小姐对于要有一位继母这事，怕是不会太乐意吧。"

"事实正是如此。据种种说法，她与未来的继母相处得极不融洽。伊雷娜小姐脾气急躁、专横任性，而格林豪太太似乎完全拿她没办法。这位太太本月本就要离开庄园，去为即将到来的婚礼做种种准备；然而这位年轻小姐的失踪，自然让一切都没法进行了。"

===Original===
"Did Miss Golding take any money away with her, do you know?"

"Ah, nobody seems sure on that head. Mr. Golding gave her a liberal allowance and exacted no accounts. Sometimes she had her purse full at the end of the quarter, sometimes it was empty before her quarterly cheque had been cashed a week. I fear you will have to do without exact information on that most important point.

"She had lovers, of course?"

"Yes; in spite of her quick temper she seems to have been a lovable and most attractive young woman, with her half-Australian half-Italian parentage, and to have turned the heads of all the men in the neighbourhood. Only two, however, appear to have found the slightest favour in her eyes—a certain Lord Guilleroy, who owns nearly all the land for miles round Langford, and a young fellow called Gordon Cleeve, the only son of Sir Gordon Cleeve, a wealthy baronet. The girl seems to have coquetted pretty equally with these two; then suddenly, for some reason or other, she gives Mr. Cleeve to understand that his attentions are distasteful to her, and gives unequivocal encouragement to Lord Guilleroy. Gordon Cleeve does not sit down quietly under this treatment. He threatens to shoot first his rival, then himself, then Miss Golding; finally, does none of these three things, but starts off on a three years' journey round the world."

===Chinese===
"你可知戈尔丁小姐离家时有没有带走钱？"

"唉，这一点似乎谁也说不准。戈尔丁先生给她一笔宽裕的零用钱，也从不要她报账。有时一个季度过完，她钱袋还鼓鼓囊囊的，有时季度的支票兑现还不到一星期，钱袋就空了。这一条最要紧的关节，我怕你只能在没有确切消息的情况下将就了。"

"她当然有追求者吧？"

"有；别看她脾气急，她似乎是个招人喜爱、极有魅力的年轻女子，凭着她那一半澳洲、一半意大利的血统，把附近一带的男人都搅得神魂颠倒。只不过，似乎只有两位在她眼里稍稍入了眼——一位是吉勒罗伊勋爵（Lord Guilleroy），朗福德方圆几英里内几乎所有的土地都归他所有；另一位是个名叫戈登·克利夫（Gordon Cleeve）的年轻人，是富有的戈登·克利夫爵士（Sir Gordon Cleeve）独子。这姑娘似乎对这两位一视同仁地卖弄风情；后来忽然不知为了什么，她叫克利夫先生明白他的殷勤令她厌烦，却毫不含糊地给吉勒罗伊勋爵以鼓励。戈登·克利夫可不肯善罢甘休。他扬言要先毙了他的情敌，再毙了他自己，最后毙了戈尔丁小姐；到头来，这三件事他一件也没做，倒是动身去作为期三年的环球旅行了。"

===Original===
"Threatens to shoot her; starts off on a journey round the world," summed up Loveday. "Do you know the date of the day on which he left Langford?"

"Yes, it was on the 19th; the day before Miss Golding disappeared. But Ramsay has already traced him down to Brindisi; ascertained that he went on board the *Buckingham*, en route for Alexandria, and has beaten out the theory that he can, by any possibility, be connected with the affair. So I wouldn't advise you to look in that quarter for your clue."

"I am not at all sanguine about finding a clue in any quarter," said Loveday, as she rose to take leave.

She did not feel in the best of tempers, and was a little disposed to resent having a case, so to speak, forced upon her under such disadvantageous conditions.

===Chinese===
"扬言要毙了她，却又动身去环游世界，"洛芙迪概括道，"你可知他离开朗福德那天是几号？"

"知道，是十九号，也就是戈尔丁小姐失踪的前一天。不过拉姆齐已经一路追查他到了布林迪西（Brindisi），查实他登上了开往亚历山大（Alexandria）的'白金汉号'（*Buckingham*），由此彻底排除了他与本案有任何瓜葛的可能。所以我不劝你朝那个方向去找线索。"

"不论哪个方向，我对找到线索都不抱什么指望，"洛芙迪说着，起身告辞。

她这会儿心情不大好，多少有些不痛快，因为这么一桩案子，可以说是在如此不利的情形下硬塞给她的。

===Original===
Her last words to Mr. Dyer were almost the first she addressed to Inspector Ramsay when, towards the close of the day, she was met by him at Langford Cross Station. Ramsay was a lanky, bony Scotchman, sandy-haired and slow of speech.

"Our hopes centre on you; we trust you'll not disappoint us," he said, by way of a greeting.

His use of the plural number made Loveday turn in the direction of a tall, good-looking man, with a remarkably frank expression of countenance, who stood at the inspector's elbow.

"I am Lord Guilleroy," said this gentleman, coming forward. "Will you allow me to drive you to Langford Hall? My cab is waiting outside."

===Chinese===
她临走时对戴尔先生说的最后那番话，几乎就是将近黄昏时分、她在朗福德克罗斯车站见到拉姆齐巡官时对他说的开场白。拉姆齐是个瘦长干瘪的苏格兰人，沙色头发，说话慢吞吞的。

"我们的希望全寄托在您身上了；盼望您别让我们失望，"他以此作为招呼。

他用的是"我们"这个复数，惹得洛芙迪朝一个高个子、相貌堂堂的男人转过脸去——此人神情格外坦率，正站在巡官肘边。

"我是吉勒罗伊勋爵，"这位先生说着上前一步。"可否容我驾车送您去朗福德庄？我的马车就在外头等着。"

===Original===
"Thank you; one moment," answered Loveday, again turning to Ramsay. "Now, do you wish," she said, addressing him, "to tell me anything beyond the facts you have already communicated to Mr. Dyer?"

"No-o," answered the inspector, slowly and sententiously. "I would rather not bias your mind in any direction by any theory of mine." ("It would be rather a waste of time to attempt such a thing," thought Loveday.) "The only additional fact I have to mention is one you would see for yourself so soon as you arrived at the Hall, namely, that Mr. Golding is keeping up with great difficulty—in fact, is on the verge of a breakdown. He has not had half an hour's sleep since his daughter left home—a serious thing that for a man at his age."

===Chinese===
"多谢；稍等片刻，"洛芙迪答道，又转向拉姆齐。"那么，您可愿，"她对着他说，"在您已经告知戴尔先生的那些情形之外，再跟我说点什么？"

"不——不，"巡官慢条斯理、一本正经地答道。"我可不愿用我自己的什么推断，把您的思路往任何一个方向上带。"（"想办到这种事，倒还真是在浪费时间呢，"洛芙迪心想。）"我唯一要补充的一点，是您一到庄园便自会看出来的——那就是，戈尔丁先生正十分艰难地强撑着，事实上已濒临崩溃。自打女儿离家，他连半个钟头的觉都没睡过——对他这把年纪的人来说，这可不是小事。"

===Original===
Loveday was favourably impressed with Lord Guilleroy. He gave her the idea of being a man of strong commonsense and great energy. His conversation was marked by a certain reserve. Although, however, he evidently declined to wear his heart upon his sleeve, it was easy to see, from a few words that escaped him, that if the search for Miss Golding proved fruitless his whole life would be wrecked.

He did not share Inspector Ramsay's wish not to bias Loveday's mind by any theory of his own.

"If I had a theory you should have it in a minute," he said, as he whipped up his horse and drove rapidly along the country road; "but I confess at the present moment my mind is a perfect blank on the matter. I have had a dozen theories, and have been compelled, one by one, to let them all go. I have suspected every one in turn, Cleeve, her own father (God forgive me!), her intended stepmother, the very servants in the house, and, one by one, circumstances have seemed to exonerate them all. It's bewildering—it's maddening! And most maddening of all it is to have to sit here with idle hands, when I would scour the earth from end to end to find her!"

===Chinese===
洛芙迪对吉勒罗伊勋爵印象颇佳。他给她一种头脑极有常识、精力又十分充沛的感觉。他的谈吐带着几分矜持。不过，尽管他显然不愿把心事挂在脸上，从他不经意间漏出的片言只语里，却很容易看出：倘若寻访戈尔丁小姐终归徒劳，他这一生便算毁了。

他可不像拉姆齐巡官那样，不愿用自己的推断去影响洛芙迪的判断。

"我要是有什么推断，准保立马就告诉您，"他说着，策马扬鞭，沿着乡间大路疾驰起来；"可我得承认，眼下我对这事脑子里是一片空白。我先后有过不下十来种推断，却不得不一个一个地全都放弃。我挨个儿怀疑过每一个人——克利夫、她亲生父亲（上帝宽恕我！）、她那位准继母，乃至宅子里的仆人们——可情形似乎又一个一个地把他们全都洗脱了。真叫人发懵——真叫人发疯！而最叫人发疯的，是不得不闲着两手坐在这儿，而我本愿意把大地从头到尾搜个遍去找她！"

===Original===
The country around Langford Hall, like most of the hunting districts in Leicestershire, was as flat as if a gigantic stream-roller had passed over it. The Hall itself was a somewhat imposing Gothic structure, of rough grey stone. Very grey and drear it showed in the autumn landscape as Loveday drove in through the park gates and caught her first glimpse of it between the all but leafless elms that flanked the drive. The equinoctial gales had set in early this year, and heavy rains had helped forward their work of wreckage and destruction. The green sward of the park was near akin to a swamp; and the trout stream that flowed across it at an angle showed swollen to its very banks. The sky was leaden with gathering masses of clouds; a flight of rooks, wheeling low and flapping their black wings, with their mournful cawing, completed the dreariness of the scene.

"A companion picture, this," Loveday thought, "to the desolation that must reign within the house with the fate of its only daughter unknown—unguessed at even."

===Chinese===
朗福德庄四周的乡野，正如莱斯特郡大部分猎狐乡一样，平坦得仿佛一台硕大无朋的蒸汽压路机从上面碾过似的。庄园本身是一座颇为气派的哥特式建筑，用粗粝的灰石砌成。在秋日的景色里，它显得那样灰暗、那样萧索——洛芙迪驾车穿过庄园大门，从车道两侧几乎落尽了叶子的榆树间隙里，头一眼瞥见它时便是这般光景。今年的秋分风暴来得格外早，大雨又助长了它们摧折破坏的势头。庄园里碧绿的草坪已近于一片沼泽；斜穿而过的那条鳟鱼溪，涨得与两岸齐平。天空阴沉沉的，堆积着大团大团的云；一群白嘴鸦低低地盘旋着，扑扇着黑色的翅膀，发出凄凉的聒噪，把这景象的萧瑟推到了极致。

"这幅画面，倒正与宅子里的凄凉相映成趣，"洛芙迪心想，"独女的生死尚且不明——甚至无从揣测——那宅子里头，必是一片愁云惨雾吧。"

===Original===
As she alighted at the hall door, a magnificent Newfoundland dog came bounding forth. Lord Guilleroy caressed it heartily.

"It was her dog," he explained. "We have tried in vain to make him track down his mistress—these dogs haven't the scent of hounds."

He excused himself from entering the house with Loveday.

"It's like a vault—a catacomb; I can't stand it," he said. "No, I'll take back my horse;" this was said to the man who stood waiting. "Tell Mr. Golding he'll see me round in the morning without fail."

===Chinese===
她在门廊前下车时，一条漂亮健壮的纽芬兰犬（Newfoundland dog）蹦跳着迎了出来。吉勒罗伊勋爵热切地抚摸着它。

"这是她的狗，"他解释道。"我们试着让它去追踪女主人，可白费力气——这种狗没有猎犬的鼻子。"

他借故不陪洛芙迪进屋。

"里头活像个墓穴——像座地下墓室；我受不了，"他说。"不，我把马牵回去；"这话是对站在一旁等候的马夫说的。"告诉戈尔丁先生，明早我准过来一趟。"

===Original===
Loveday was shown into the library, where Mr. Golding was waiting to receive her. In the circumstances no disguise as to her name and profession had been deemed necessary, and she was announced simply as Miss Brooke from Lynch Court.

Mr. Golding greeted her warmly. One glance at him convinced her that Inspector Ramsay had given no exaggerated account of the bereaved father. His face was wan and haggard; his head was bowed; his voice sounded strained and weak. He seemed incapable of speaking on any save the one topic that filled his thoughts.

"We pin our faith on you, Miss Brooke," he said; "you are our last hope. Now, tell me you do not despair of being able to end this awful suspense one way or another. A day or two more of it will put me into my coffin!"

===Chinese===
洛芙迪被引见进书房，戈尔丁先生正等着接待她。在此情形下，并无必要隐去她的姓名与身份，于是通报时只说她是从舰队街林奇大院（Lynch Court）来的布鲁克小姐。

戈尔丁先生热情地向她问好。只消看他一眼，洛芙迪便相信拉姆齐巡官对这位痛失爱女的父亲并无半点夸大。他面容憔悴枯槁，头垂得低低的，嗓音听上去又紧又弱。除了那件填满他心头的唯一一桩事，他似乎什么都说不了。

"我们把全部指望都寄托在您身上了，布鲁克小姐，"他说，"您是我们最后的希望。这下，请告诉我，您并不绝望于能以这样或那样的方式了结这可怕的煎熬。再多一两天，我就要给送进棺材里去了！"

===Original===
"Miss Brooke will perhaps like to have some tea, and to rest a little, after her long journey before she begins to talk?" said a lady, at that moment entering the room and advancing towards her. Loveday could only conjecture that this was Mrs. Greenhow, for Mr. Golding was too preoccupied to make any attempt to an introduction.

Mrs. Greenhow was a small, slight woman, with fluffy hair and green-grey eyes. Her voice suggested a purr; her eyes, a scratch.

"Cat-tribe!" thought Loveday; "the velvet paw and the hidden claw—the exact antithesis, I should say, to one of Miss Golding's temperament."

===Chinese===
"布鲁克小姐远道而来，开谈之前，想必愿意先用点茶、稍事歇息吧？"这时一位女士说着，走进屋来，朝她迎上前去。洛芙迪只能揣测这便是格林豪太太了，因为戈尔丁先生心不在焉，压根儿没顾得上替她们引见。

格林豪太太是个娇小纤细的女人，一头蓬松的软发，一双灰绿色的眼睛。她的嗓音听着像猫在打呼噜，她的眼神却像猫在挠人。

"猫族！"洛芙迪心想，"丝绒般的肉掌，藏着不露的利爪——若叫我猜，这恰恰是与戈尔丁小姐那种性情截然相反的一类人。"

===Original===
Mr. Golding went back to the one subject he had at heart.

"You have had my daughter's photograph given to you, I've no doubt," he said; "but this I consider a far better likeness." Here he pointed to a portrait in pastels that hung above his writing-table. It was that of a large-eyed, handsome girl of eighteen, with a remarkably sweet expression about the mouth.

Mrs. Greenhow again interposed. "I think, if you don't mind my saying so," she said, "you would slightly mislead Miss Brooke if you led her to think that that was a perfect likeness of dear René. Much as I love the dear girl," here she turned to Loveday, "I'm bound to admit that one seldom or never saw her wearing such a sweet expression of countenance."

===Chinese===
戈尔丁先生又回到他唯一挂心的那桩事上。

"我女儿的相片想必已经给您看过了，"他说，"可我觉得这一幅要像得多。"说着，他指向书桌上方挂着的一幅粉彩画像。画的是个十八岁的大眼睛漂亮姑娘，嘴角带着一种格外甜美的神情。

格林豪太太又插了进来。"我想，要是我这么说您不介意的话，"她道，"您若是让布鲁克小姐以为那就是亲爱的蕾妮（René）毫厘不差的画像，多少会把她引入歧途。我虽疼爱这好姑娘，"她转向洛芙迪，"却不得不承认，人们难得——简直从不——见她脸上露出这般甜美的神情。"

===Original===
Mr. Golding frowned, and sharply changed the subject.

"Tell me, Miss Brooke," he said, "what was your first impression when the facts of the case were submitted to you? I have been told that first impressions with you are generally infallible."

Loveday parried the question.

"I am not at present sure that I am in possession of all the facts," she answered. "There are one or two questions I particularly want to ask—you must forgive me if they seem to you a little irrelevant to the matter in hand. First and foremost, I want to know if any formal goodbye took place between your daughter and Mr. Gordon Cleeve?"

"I think not. A sudden coolness arose between them, and the young fellow went away without so much as shaking hands with me."

===Chinese===
戈尔丁先生皱起眉头，生硬地岔开了话头。

"告诉我，布鲁克小姐，"他说，"案情呈交到您面前时，您的头一个印象是什么？我听人说，您的头一个印象向来是万无一失的。"

洛芙迪避开了这个问题。

"我眼下还不敢说已经掌握了全部情形，"她答道。"有一两个问题我特别想问——您若觉得它们与手头这案子扯不上什么关系，还请多多见谅。头一件，我想知道，令爱与戈登·克利夫先生之间，可曾有过什么正式的道别？"

"我想没有。他俩之间突然冷淡下来，那年轻人走的时候，连跟我握个手都没有。"

===Original===
"I fear an irreparable breach has occurred between the Cleeves and yourself on account of dear René's extraordinary treatment of Gordon," said Mrs. Greenhow sweetly.

"There was no extraordinary treatment," said Mr. Golding, now almost in anger. "My daughter and Mr. Cleeve were good friends—nothing more, I assure you—until one day René saw him cruelly thrashing one of his setters, and after that she cut him dead—would have nothing whatever to do with him."

"Maddalena told Inspector Ramsay," said Mrs. Greenhow, sweetly still, "that on the evening before Gordon Cleeve left Langford dear René received a note from him—"

"Which she tossed unopened into the fire," finished Mr. Golding.

===Chinese===
"亲爱的蕾妮那样怠慢戈登，怕是叫克利夫一家和您之间生出了一段无可挽回的嫌隙吧，"格林豪太太甜甜地说。

"根本没有什么怠慢，"戈尔丁先生说，这会儿几乎要动怒了。"我女儿和克利夫先生一直是好朋友——仅止于此，我向您保证——直到有一天，蕾妮瞧见他残忍地鞭打他的一条塞特犬，自那以后她就不理他了——说什么也不肯再跟他往来。"

"马达莱娜（Maddalena）告诉过拉姆齐巡官，"格林豪太太依旧甜甜地说，"戈登·克利夫离开朗福德的前一天傍晚，亲爱的蕾妮收到了他写的一张便条——"

"她连拆都没拆，就扔进了火里，"戈尔丁先生接过去把话补完。

===Original===
"Who is Maddalena?" interrupted Loveday.

"My daughter's maid. I brought her over from Naples twelve years ago as nurse, and as René grew older she naturally enough fell into her duties as René's maid. She is a dear, faithful creature; her aunt was nurse to René's mother."

"Is it possible for Maddalena to be told off to wait upon me while I am in the house?" asked Loveday, turning to Mrs. Greenhow.

"Certainly, if you wish it. At the same time, I warn you that she is not in a particularly amiable frame of mind just now, and will be very likely to be sullen and disobliging," answered the lady.

"Maddalena is not generally either one or the other," said Mr. Golding deprecatingly; "but just now she is a little unlike herself. The truth is, all the servants have been a little too rigorously cross-examined by the police on matters of which they could have absolutely no knowledge, and Ramsay made such a dead set at Lena that the girl felt herself insulted, grew sullen, and refused to open her lips."

===Chinese===
"马达莱娜是谁？"洛芙迪插嘴问。

"我女儿的女仆。十二年前我把她从那不勒斯带过来做奶娘，蕾妮渐渐长大，她自然而然也就接着做了蕾妮的女仆。她是个好心肠、忠心耿耿的人；她姨妈当初就是蕾妮母亲的奶娘。"

"在我住在此处期间，可否让马达莱娜拨过来伺候我？"洛芙迪转向格林豪太太问道。

"当然可以，只要您愿意。不过我提醒您，她这会儿心情可不大好，十有八九会沉着脸、不情不愿的，"那位太太答道。

"马达莱娜平日里既不沉脸也不别扭，"戈尔丁先生不以为然地说，"只是眼下她有点不像平常的自己。实情是，所有的仆人都被警方盘问得太狠了些，问的又都是他们绝不可能知道的事，而拉姆齐偏偏死盯着莉娜（Lena）不放，弄得那姑娘觉得自己受了辱，便沉下脸来，闭口不说话了。"

===Original===
"She must be handled judiciously. I suppose she was brokenhearted when Miss Golding did not return from her morning's walk?"

A reply was prevented by the entrance of a servant with a telegram in his hand.

Mr. Golding tore it open, and, in a trembling voice, read aloud as follows:

"Someone answering to the description of your daughter was seen yesterday in the Champs Élysées, but disappeared before she could be detained. Watch arrivals at Folkestone and Dover."

The telegram was dated from Paris, and was from M. Dulau, of the Paris police. Mr. Golding's agitation was pitiable.

===Chinese===
"对她可得拿捏好分寸。戈尔丁小姐早晨出去散步一直没回来，她想必伤心欲绝吧？"

话没答上来，因为一个仆人手里拿着电报走了进来。

戈尔丁先生一把撕开，用发抖的声音朗声念道：

"昨日有人于香榭丽舍（Champs Élysées）见一女子，形貌与令爱相符，然未及扣留便已不见踪影。请留意福克斯通（Folkestone）与多佛（Dover）之抵埠人员。"

电报发自巴黎（Paris），拍发者是巴黎警方的迪洛先生（M. Dulau）。戈尔丁先生的激动之状，实在令人怜悯。

===Original===
"Great heavens! is it possible?" he cried, putting his hand to his forehead as if stunned. "I'll start for Dover—no, Paris, I think, at once." He staggered to his feet, looking around him in a dazed and bewildered fashion. He might as well have talked of starting for the moon or the north star.

"Pardon me," said Loveday, "Inspector Ramsay is the right person to deal with that telegram. It should be sent to him at once."

Mr. Golding sank back in his chair, trembling from head to foot.

"I think you are right," he said faintly. "I might break down and lose a possible chance."

===Chinese===
"老天爷！竟有这可能？"他叫道，一手按住额头，仿佛遭了重击。"我这就动身去多佛——不，我想是去巴黎，马上就走。"他摇摇晃晃地站起身，神情迷乱茫然地四下张望。他这副样子，大可以说要去启程奔向月亮或是北极星了。

"恕我直言，"洛芙迪说，"这封电报交由拉姆齐巡官去办才妥当。该立刻给他送去。"

戈尔丁先生颓然坐回椅中，从头到脚都在发抖。

"我想您说得对，"他有气无力地说，"我若去了，只怕要撑不住，反倒错失一个可能的机会。"

===Original===
Then he turned once more to the man, who stood waiting for orders, and desired him to take the fastest horse to the stables and ride at once with the telegram to the Inspector.

"And," he added, "on your way back call at the Castle, see Lord Guilleroy, and give him the news." He turned a pleading face towards Loveday. "This is good news—you consider it good news, do you not?" he asked piteously.

"It won't do to depend too much on it, will it?" said Mrs. Greenhow. "You see, there have been so many false alarms—if I may use the word."—This was said to Loveday.—"Three times last week we had telegrams from different parts of the country saying dear René had been seen—now here, now there, I think there must be a good many girls like her wandering about the world."

===Chinese===
于是他又转向那个站着等候吩咐的仆人，命他去马厩牵上最快的一匹马，立刻带着电报骑去见巡官。

"还有，"他补上一句，"回来路上顺道去一趟城堡，见着吉勒罗伊勋爵，把这消息告诉他。"他转过一张恳求的脸朝向洛芙迪。"这是好消息——您也觉得这是好消息，是不是？"他可怜巴巴地问。

"可不能太把它当真，是不是？"格林豪太太说，"您也瞧见了，空欢喜已经有过那么许多回了——要是我能用这个词的话。"——这话是对洛芙迪说的。——"上礼拜就有三次，从国内各处拍来电报，说瞧见了亲爱的蕾妮——一会儿在这儿，一会儿在那儿，我想这世上想必有不少跟她相像的姑娘在外头四处游荡吧。"

===Original===
"The dress has something to do with it, no doubt," answered Loveday; "it is not a very distinctive one. Still, we must hope for the best. It is possible, of course, that at this very moment the young lady may be on her way home with a full explanation of what has seemed extraordinary conduct on her part. Now, if you will allow me, I will go to my room. And will you please give the order that Maddalena shall follow me there as quickly as possible?"

Loveday's thoughts were very busy when, in the quietude of her own room, she sank into an easy chair beside the fire. The case to which she had so unwillingly devoted her attention was beginning to present some interesting intricacies. She passed in view the dramatis personae of the little drama which she could only hope might not end in a tragedy. The brokenhearted father; the would-be-stepmother, with her feline affinities; the faithful maid; the cruel-tempered lover; the open-faced, energetic one; each in turn received their meed of attention.

"That man would be one to depend on in an emergency," she said to herself, allowing her thoughts to dwell a little longer upon Lord Guilleroy than upon the others. "He has, I should say, a good head on his shoulders and—"

===Chinese===
"这里头跟衣着不无关系，那是肯定的，"洛芙迪答道，"她那身打扮并不怎么显眼。不过，我们总得往好处想。当然，也许此时此刻，这位年轻小姐正走在回家的路上，随身带着对那番看似离奇的举动的完满解释呢。那么，要是您允许，我这就回房去了。还烦您吩咐一声，让马达莱娜尽快到我房里来？"

洛芙迪在自家房中的静谧里，坐进壁炉旁的一张安乐椅时，心思转得飞快。她当初极不情愿地接手的这桩案子，此刻开始显出几分别有意趣的曲折来了。她把这一出小戏里的各色人物在脑中一一过了一遍——她只能盼望它莫要以悲剧收场。那心碎的父亲；那带着猫一般习性的准继母；那忠心的女仆；那性情暴戾的情人；那相貌坦荡、精力充沛的一位——每一个人依次领受了她的一番端详。

"那样一个男人，遇着紧要关头是大可倚仗的，"她自言自语，让心思在吉勒罗伊勋爵身上比旁人多停留了一会儿。"他这人，依我看，肩膀上头顶着一个管用的好脑袋，而且——"

===Original===
But here a tap-tap at the door brought her thinking to a standstill, and in response to her "come in" the door opened and the maid Lena entered.

She was a tall, black-eyed, dark-skinned woman of about thirty, dressed in a neat black stuff gown. Twelve years of English domestic life had considerably modified the outer tokens of her nationality; a gold dagger that kept a thick coil of hair in its place, and a massive Roman-cut cameo ring on the third finger of her right hand, were about the only things that differentiated her appearance from that of the ordinary English lady's maid. Possibly as a rule she wore a pleasant, smiling expression of countenance. For the moment, however, her face was shadowed by a sullen scowl, that said plainly as words could: "I am here very much against my will, and intend to render you the most unwilling of services."

Loveday felt that she must be taken in hand at once.

"You are Miss Golding's maid, I believe?" she said in a short, sharp tone.

"Yes, madame." This in a slow, sullen one.

===Chinese===
可就在这时，门上一阵笃笃的轻敲打断了她的思绪，她应了一声"进来"，门开了，女仆莉娜走了进来。

她是个高挑的黑眼睛、深肤色女人，约莫三十岁，穿一身素净的黑料子衣裙。十二年的英国家居生活已大大磨去了她身上那一国人外在的痕迹；一支别住一大绺盘发的金色匕首形发簪，加上右手中指上一枚厚重的罗马式浮雕戒指，几乎是她与寻常英国太太家贴身女仆仅有的不同之处。平日里她或许总带着一副和悦含笑的神情。可这会儿，她脸上却罩着一层阴沉的怒色，明明白白地像言语一般宣告着："我是万分不情愿地到这儿来的，而且打算把这份差事办得最最不情愿。"

洛芙迪觉得，此人非得立刻下手拿捏不可。

"你是戈尔丁小姐的女仆吧？"她用一种短促、尖利的口气说。

"是的，夫人。"这一声答得又慢又沉。

===Original===
"Very well. Kindly unstrap that portmanteau and open my dressing-bag. I am glad you are to wait upon me while I am here. I don't suppose you ever before in your life acted maid to a lady detective?"

"Never, madame." This in a still more sullen tone than before.

"Ah, it will be a new experience to you, and I hope that it may be made a profitable one also. Tell me, are you saving up money to get married?"

Lena, on her knees unstrapping the portmanteau, started and looked up.

"How does madame know that?" she asked, Loveday pointed to the cameo ring on her third finger. "I only guessed at such a possibility," she answered. "Well, now, Lena, I am going to make you an offer. I will give you fifty pounds—fifty, remember, in English gold—if you will procure for me certain information that I require in the prosecution of my work here."

===Chinese===
"很好。劳驾解开那只旅行箱的皮带，把我的梳妆袋打开。你能在我住这儿的时候伺候我，我很高兴。我猜，你这辈子怕是还没给一位女侦探做过贴身女仆吧？"

"没有过，夫人。"这一声比先前还要沉郁。

"啊，这对你将是桩新鲜经历，我也盼着它能成为一桩有赚头的经历。告诉我，你可是攒着钱准备成家？"

莉娜正跪着解旅行箱的皮带，闻言一惊，抬起头来。

"夫人怎么知道的？"她问。洛芙迪指了指她中指上那枚浮雕戒指。"我不过是猜着有这种可能罢了，"她答道，"好啦，莉娜，眼下我要跟你做一桩交易。我给你五十英镑——记住，五十，实打实的英国金币——只要你肯替我弄到某些我在此处办事所需要的情况。"

===Original===
The sullen look on Lena's face deepened.

"I am a servant of the house," she answered, bending lower over the portmanteau; "I do not sell its secrets even for English gold."

"But it is not the secrets of your master's house I am wanting to buy—no, nor anybody else's secrets; I only want you to procure for me certain information that I could easily have procured for myself if I had been a little sooner on the scene. And the information I want relates to no one inside the house, but someone outside of it—Mr. Gordon Cleeve."

The sullen look on Lena's face gave place to one of intense, unutterable relief.

"Mr. Gordon Cleeve!" she repeated. "Oh-h, for fifty pounds, I will undertake to bring madame a good deal of information about him; I know some of the servants in Sir Gordon's house. I know, too, the mother of Mr. Cleeve's valet who has started with him on his journey round the world."

===Chinese===
莉娜脸上的阴沉之色更深了。

"我是这宅子里的仆人，"她答道，把身子伏得更低，凑近旅行箱；"即便为了英国金币，我也不出卖这宅子的秘密。"

"可我要买的，并非你东家宅子里的秘密——不，也不是任何旁人的秘密；我只不过要你替我弄到某些情况罢了，那些情况，要是我到得早一些，本可以轻而易举地自己弄到手。而我要的情况，与宅子里的任何人都无干，倒是与宅子外头的一个人有关——戈登·克利夫先生。"

莉娜脸上那层阴沉，顿时化作了一种浓烈得无法言说的释然。

"戈登·克利夫先生！"她重复道，"噢——为了五十英镑，我保准能给夫人弄来不少关于他的消息；戈登爵士府上几个仆人我认得的。克利夫先生的贴身男仆陪着他动身去环游世界了，那男仆的母亲，我也认识。"

===Original===
"Good. So, then, it is a bargain. Now, Lena, tell me truly, is this Mr. Cleeve a great favourite with you?"

"With me! Ah, the good God forbid, madame! I never liked him; I used to say to Miss René when I brought her his flowers and his notes: 'Have nothing to do with him, he is cruel—bad at heart.'"

"Ah, yes; I read all that in your face when I mentioned his name. Now what I want you first and foremost to do for me is to find out how this young man spent the last day that he was at Langford. I want you to bring me a report of his doings—as exact a report as possible—on the 18th of this month."

"I will do my best, madame."

===Chinese===
"好。那么，这就说定了。现在，莉娜，你老老实实告诉我，这位克利夫先生可是你十分中意的人？"

"我中意他！啊，老天不容，夫人！我从来就不喜欢他；从前我替蕾妮小姐送他那许多花和便条过去时，总跟她说：'别理他，这人残忍——心地坏。'"

"啊，是这样；我一提他的名字，你脸上就把这些全写出来了。眼下我要你头一件替我办的事，是查清楚这个年轻人待在朗福德的最后一天是怎么过的。我要你把他在本月十八号那天的所作所为给我带一份禀报来——越详尽越好。"

"我尽力而为，夫人。"

===Original===
"Very good. Now, there is something else. Would you be greatly surprised if I told you that the young man did not sail in the *Buckingham* from Brindisi as is generally supposed?"

"Madame! Inspector Ramsay said he had ascertained beyond a doubt that Mr. Cleeve went on board the *Buckingham* at Brindisi!"

"Ah, to go on board is one thing; to sail is another! Now, listen, Lena, very carefully to what I am going to say. I am expecting daily to receive some most important information respecting this gentleman's movements, and I may want someone to set off at a minute's notice for Paris, perhaps; or, perhaps, Florence or Naples, to verify that information: would you do this for me?—of course, I would supply you with money and full details as to your journey."

A flush of pleasure passed over Lena's face.

"Yes, madame," she answered; "if you could get my master's permission for me to go."

"I will undertake to do so." She pondered a moment, then added a little tentatively, and closely watching Lena's face as she asked the question, "I suppose Miss Golding resembled her mother in appearance—I do not see any likeness between her portrait and her father."

===Chinese===
"很好。还有一件事。要是我告诉你，这位年轻人并非如众人所设想的那样，从布林迪西乘'白金汉号'启航的，你会不会大吃一惊？"

"夫人！拉姆齐巡官可说，他已经查得确凿无疑，克利夫先生是在布林迪西登上的'白金汉号'！"

"啊，登船是一回事；启航可又是另一回事！这下你仔细听着，莉娜，把我接下来说的话听得一字不漏。我每日都盼着收到一些关于这位先生行踪的极要紧的消息，到时候我也许需要一个人说走就走，一接到通知就动身去巴黎，也许是佛罗伦萨，也许是那不勒斯，去核实那些消息：你肯替我跑这一趟吗？——自然，盘缠和一路上详尽的情形，都由我给你备齐。"

一抹喜色掠过莉娜的脸。

"肯的，夫人，"她答道，"只要您能替我向东家告下假来。"

"这件事我包下了。"她沉吟片刻，又带着几分试探地补了一句，一边紧盯着莉娜的脸色发问，"我想，戈尔丁小姐在长相上像她母亲吧——我从她的画像和她父亲之间，瞧不出半点相像。"

===Original===
Lena's sullenness and stateliness had vanished together now, and once upon the topic of her nursling she was the warmhearted, enthusiastic Italian woman once more. She became voluble in her description of her dear Miss René, her beauty, her fascinating ways, which she traced entirely to the Italian blood that flowed in her veins; and anecdote after anecdote she related of the happy time when they lived among the lakes and mountains of her native land.

The room grew dark and darker, while she gossiped apace, and presently the dressing-bell clanged through the house.

"Light the candles now," said Loveday, rising from her seat beside the fire; "draw down the blinds and shut out that dreary autumn scene, it sets me shivering!"

===Chinese===
莉娜的阴沉与矜持此刻一道消散了，一旦谈起她奶大的小姐，她又变回了那个热心肠、满腔热忱的意大利女人。她说起她亲爱的蕾妮小姐来滔滔不绝，说她的美貌、她那迷人的做派，把这些统统归到她血管里流淌的那一半意大利血统上去；又一件接一件地讲起从前她们住在她故国那些湖光山色之间的快活时日的旧事。

她絮絮地说着，屋里越来越暗，不一会儿，更衣的钟声响遍了整座宅子。

"这会儿把蜡烛点上吧，"洛芙迪从壁炉旁的座上起身说道，"把窗帘放下来，把那萧瑟的秋景挡在外头，瞧得我直打哆嗦！"

===Original===
It might well do so. The black clouds had fulfilled their threat, and rain was now dashing in torrents against the panes. A tall sycamore, immediately outside the window, creaked and groaned dismally in response to the wind that came whistling round the corner of the house, and between the swaying and all but leafless elms Loveday could catch a glimpse of the grey, winding trout stream, swollen now to its limits and threatening to overflow its banks.

Dinner that night was in keeping with the gloom that overhung the house within and without; although the telegram from Paris had seemed to let in a ray of hope, Mr. Golding was evidently afraid to put much trust in it.

"As Mrs. Greenhow says, 'we have had so many disappointments,'" he said sadly, as he took his place at table. "So many false clues—false scents started. Ramsay has at once put himself in communication with the police at Boulogne and Calais, as well as at Dover and Folkestone. We can only pray that something may come of it!"

===Chinese===
那是难怪她要打哆嗦的。乌云兑现了它们的威胁，大雨正倾盆而下，猛打着窗玻璃。窗户外头一株高高的悬铃木，凄凉地嘎吱作响、哀吟不止，应和着从宅子墙角呼啸卷来的风；从那些摇来晃去、几乎落尽叶子的榆树间隙里，洛芙迪能瞥见那条灰色的、蜿蜒的鳟鱼溪，此刻已涨到了极处，眼看就要漫出两岸。

那天晚上的晚餐，正与里里外外笼罩着这宅子的阴郁相称；虽说巴黎来的电报似乎透进了一线希望，戈尔丁先生却显然不敢怎样相信它。

"正如格林豪太太所说，'我们已经有过那么多回的失望了，'"他入座时忧伤地说，"那么多假线索——那么多假气味给人放了出去。拉姆齐已经立刻跟布洛涅（Boulogne）和加来（Calais）的警方接上了头，多佛和福克斯通那边也一样。我们只能祷告，但愿能查出点什么名堂来！"

===Original===
"And dear Lord Guilleroy," chimed in Mrs. Greenhow, in her soft, purring voice, "has started for Paris immediately. The young man has such a vast amount of energy, and thinks he can do the work of the police better than they can do it for themselves."

"That's hardly a fair way of putting it, Clare," interrupted Mr. Golding irritably; "he is working heart and soul with the police, and thinks it advisable that someone representing me should be in Paris, in case an emergency should arise; also he wants himself to question Dulau respecting my daughter's sudden appearance and disappearance in the Paris streets. Guilleroy," here he turned to Loveday, "is devotedly attached to my daughter, and—why, Dryad, what's the matter, old man? down, down! Don't growl and whine in that miserable fashion."

He had broken off to address these words to the Newfoundland, who, until that moment, had been comfortably stretched on the hearthrug before the fire, but who now had suddenly started to his feet with ears erect, and given a prolonged growl, that ended in something akin to a whine.

===Chinese===
"还有亲爱的吉勒罗伊勋爵，"格林豪太太用她那软绵绵、如猫打呼般的嗓音附和道，"已经立刻动身去巴黎了。这年轻人精力可真叫旺盛，觉得警方的活儿他干起来比警方自己还强。"

"这说法可不怎么公道，克莱尔（Clare），"戈尔丁先生不耐烦地打断她，"他是跟警方一条心、拧成一股绳地干，他觉得理当有一个代表我的人在巴黎，以防有什么急事；再者他也想亲自向迪洛查问查问，我女儿怎会在巴黎街头忽然现身又忽然不见。吉勒罗伊，"他转向洛芙迪，"对我女儿一片痴心，而且——咦，德里亚德（Dryad），你怎么了，老伙计？趴下，趴下！别那么可怜巴巴地又吼又叫。"

他这番话是冲那条纽芬兰犬说的——直到方才，它还舒舒服服地摊在炉火前的地毯上，这会儿却忽然竖着耳朵一跃而起，发出一声拖长的低吼，末了又变成近乎呜咽的声息。

===Original===
"It may be a fox trotting past the window," said Mrs. Greenhow, whipping at the dog with her lace handkerchief. But Dryad was not to be so easily subdued. With his nose to the ground now he was sniffing uneasily at and around the heavy curtains that half draped the long French windows of the room.

"Something has evidently disturbed him. Why not let him out into the garden?" said Loveday. And Mr. Golding, with a "Hey, Dryad, go find!" unfastened the window and let the dog out into the windy darkness.

Dinner was a short meal that night. It was easy to see that it was only by a strong effort of will that Mr. Golding kept his place at table, and made even a pretence of eating.

===Chinese===
"许是有一只狐狸打窗前溜过去，"格林豪太太说着，用她的蕾丝手帕朝狗甩了一下。可德里亚德不是这么容易安抚下去的。这会儿它把鼻子贴着地，在那半掩着这间屋子一长排落地窗的厚重帷幔四下里不安地嗅来嗅去。

"准是什么东西惊着它了。何不放它到花园里去？"洛芙迪说。于是戈尔丁先生喊一声"嘿，德里亚德，去找！"便开了窗，把狗放进了风声呼啸的黑夜里。

那天晚上的晚餐吃得很快。不难看出，戈尔丁先生全凭着强硬的意志力才坐得住，连做个吃东西的样子都勉强。

===Original===
At the close of the meal Loveday asked for a quiet corner, in which to write some business letters, and was shown into the library by Mr. Golding.

"You'll find all you require here, I think," he said, with something of a sigh, as he placed a chair for her at a lady's davenport. "This was René's favourite corner, and here are the last flowers she gathered—dead, all dead, but I will not have them touched!" He broke off abruptly, set down the vase of dead asters which he had taken in his hand, and quitted the room, leaving Loveday to the use of René's pen, ink, paper, and blotting-pad.

Loveday soon became absorbed in her business letters. Time flew swiftly, and it was not until a clock on the mantelpiece chimed the hour—ten o'clock—that she gave a thought as to what might be the hour for retiring at the Hall.

===Chinese===
饭毕，洛芙迪要了一个清静的角落来写几封公事信，戈尔丁先生便把她领进了书房。

"您要用的一切，这儿想必都齐备了，"他替她在一张女士用的达文波特小写字台（davenport）前摆好椅子，略带一声叹息地说，"这是蕾妮最爱待的角落，这几是她最后一次亲手摘来的花——都枯了，全枯了，可我不许人动它们！"他骤然住口，放下手里端着的那瓶枯萎的紫菀，转身出了屋子，把蕾妮的笔、墨、纸张和吸墨纸板都留给洛芙迪使唤。

洛芙迪很快就专心写起她的公事信来。时光飞逝，直到壁炉架上的钟报了时——十点——她才头一回想起，这宅子里几时就该就寝。

===Original===
Something else beside the striking of the clock almost simultaneously caught her ear—the whining and scratching of a dog at one of the windows. These, like those of the dining-room, opened as doors into an outside verandah. They were, however, closely shuttered, and Loveday had to ring for a servant to undo the patent fastener.

So soon as the window was opened Dryad rushed into the room, plastered with mud, and dripping with water from every hair.

"He must have been in the stream," said the footman, trying to collar the dog and lead him out of the room.

"Stop! one moment!" cried Loveday, for her eye had caught sight of something hanging in shreds between the dog's teeth. She bent over him, patting and soothing him, and contrived to disentangle those shreds, which a closer examination proved to be a few tattered fragments of dark blue serge.

===Chinese===
与钟声几乎同时钻进她耳朵的，还有旁的一桩动静——一条狗在其中一扇窗前呜咽、抓挠。书房的窗户和餐厅的一样，都像门似的朝外头的游廊敞开。可这会儿窗板都关得严严实实，洛芙迪只好摇铃叫个仆人来开那特制的窗栓。

窗子一开，德里亚德便一头冲进屋里，浑身的毛都糊满了泥，每一根都在往下滴水。

"它准是下到溪里去了，"男仆说着，想去揪住狗的颈圈，把它牵出屋去。

"慢着！等一下！"洛芙迪叫道，因为她的眼角瞥见了狗嘴里衔着几缕挂下来的碎条。她俯下身，一面拍它、抚它、安抚它，一面把那些碎条解了下来；凑近一看，原来是几小片撕得破破烂烂的深蓝色哔叽料子。

===Original===
"Is your letter-writing nearly ended, Miss Brooke?" asked Mr. Golding, at that moment entering the room.

For reply, Loveday held up the shreds of blue serge. His face grew ashen white; he needed no explanation; those shreds and the dripping dog seemed to tell their own tale.

"Great heavens!" he cried, "why did I not follow the dog out! There must be a search at once. Get men, lanterns, ropes, a ladder—the dog, too, will be of use."

A terrible energy took possession of him. "Find, Dryad, find!" he shouted to the dog, and then, hatless and thin-shoed as he was, he rushed out into the darkness with Dryad at his heels.

===Chinese===
"您的信写得差不多了吧，布鲁克小姐？"这当口戈尔丁先生走进屋来问道。

洛芙迪没有答话，只把那几缕蓝色哔叽的碎条举了起来。他脸色霎时变得惨白；他用不着任何人解释；那几缕碎条和那条滴着水的狗，似乎本身就在诉说它们的故事。

"老天爷！"他喊道，"我方才怎么没跟着狗出去！得马上派人去找。去叫人，提灯、绳子、梯子——这条狗也派得上用场。"

一股可怕的劲头攫住了他。"去找，德里亚德，去找！"他朝狗喊道，随即顾不得自己没戴帽子、脚上只穿着薄底鞋，便冲进了黑夜里，德里亚德紧紧跟在他脚后。

===Original===
