In less than five minutes afterwards the whole of the menservants of the house, with lanterns, ropes, and a ladder long enough to span the stream, had followed him. The wind had fallen, the rain had ceased now, and a watery half-moon was struggling through the thin, flying clouds. Loveday and Mrs. Greenhow, standing beneath the verandah, watched the men disappear in the direction of the trout stream, whither Dryad had led the way. From time to time shouts came to them, through the night stillness, of "This way!" "No, here!" together with Dryad's sharp bark and the occasional distant flash of a bull's-eye lantern. It was not until nearly half an hour afterwards that one of the men came running back to the house with a solemn white face and a pitiful tale. He wanted something that would serve for a stretcher, he said in a subdued tone—the twofold oak screen in the hall would do—and please, into which room was "it" to be brought?—

===Chinese===
不出五分钟，宅子里所有的男仆便提着灯、带着绳子，和一架长得足以跨过小溪的梯子，都跟了上去。风已歇，雨也住了，一弯水汽濛濛的半月正费力地穿过疾飞的薄云。洛芙迪和格林豪太太站在游廊底下，望着那些人朝着鳟鱼溪的方向——德里亚德领着路往那边去了——消失不见。夜里一片寂静中，不时传来"这边！""不，这边！"的喊声，夹杂着德里亚德短促的吠叫，以及偶尔一闪的、远远的牛眼信号灯的光。直到将近半个钟头之后，才有一个男仆跑回宅子来，脸色煞白，神情凝重，带着一个令人心碎的消息。他压低嗓门说，他想找一样能充当担架的东西——门厅里那架两面橡木屏风倒可以将就——还有，请问"那个"要抬进哪一间屋子？——

===Original===
On the following evening Mr. Dyer received a lengthy dispatch from Miss Brooke, which ran as follows:—

"Langford Hall.

"This is to supplement my telegram of an hour back, telling you of the finding of Miss Golding's body in the stream that runs through her father's grounds. Mr. Golding has himself identified the body, and has now utterly collapsed. At the present moment it seems rather doubtful whether he will be in a fit state to give evidence at the inquest, which will be held tomorrow. Miss Golding appears to be dressed as she was when she left home, with this notable exception—the marquise ring has disappeared from the third finger of her left hand, and in its stead she wears a plain gold wedding-ring. Now this is a remarkable circumstance, and strikes a strange keynote to my mind. I am writing hurriedly, and can only give you the most important points in this very singular case. The maid, Lena, a reserved, self-contained woman, gave way to a passion of grief when the young lady's body was brought in and laid upon her own bed. She insisted on performing all the last sad offices for the dead, however, in spite of her grief, and is now, I am glad to say, calmer and capable of a little quiet conversation with me. I keep her continually in attendance on me, as I am rather anxious to keep my eye on her just now. I have telegraphed to Lord Guilleroy, asking him, in spite of the terrible news which will in due course reach him, to be good enough to remain in Paris awaiting directions from me, which may have to be carried out at a minute's notice. I hope to have further news to send a little later on."

===Chinese===
第二天傍晚，戴尔先生收到布鲁克小姐一封长长的急件，内容如下：——

"朗福德庄。

"此信为我一个钟头前那封电报的补充——那封电报告诉您，戈尔丁小姐的遗体已在流经她父亲园子的小溪里寻获。戈尔丁先生已亲自认了尸，此刻已彻底崩溃。眼下看来，他明天出席验尸庭审时是否能处于适宜作证的状态，颇成疑问。戈尔丁小姐身上的穿戴，似乎与她离家时一般无二，只有一处显眼的例外——左手无名指上的那枚椭圆戒不见了，取而代之的，是一枚素金的结婚戒指。这是一桩异乎寻常的情形，在我心里敲出了一个古怪的调子。我匆匆落笔，只能把这桩极不寻常的案子中最要紧的几点奉告。女仆莉娜是个沉稳内敛、不轻易动声色的女人，可当小姐的遗体被抬进来、安放在她自家床上时，她却悲恸欲绝。然而她不顾悲伤，坚持要亲手为亡者尽最后那些凄凉的礼数；所幸她如今已平静下来，能跟我稍稍安安静静地谈上几句了。我让她时刻待在我身边伺候，因为我这会儿正急着要把她放在眼皮底下盯着。我已给吉勒罗伊勋爵拍了电报，求他尽管那可怕的消息迟早会传到他耳中，仍劳他大驾暂留巴黎，等候我的吩咐——这些吩咐也许要他一接到通知便立即照办。稍晚些时候，我希望能再有消息奉告。"

===Original===
Mr. Dyer laid aside the letter with a grunt of dissatisfaction.

"Well," he said to himself, "I suppose she expects me to be able to read between the lines, but I'm bothered if I can make head or tail of it all. She seems to me to be going a little wide of the mark just now; it might be as well to give her a hint." So he dashed off a few brief lines as follows:—

"I suppose you are concentrating now on finding out what were Miss Golding's movements while absent from her home. It seems to me this could be better done in Paris than at Langford Hall. The ring on her finger necessarily implies that she has gone through a marriage service somewhere, and as she was seen in Paris a day or two ago, it is as likely as not that the ceremony took place there. The Paris police could give you 'yea or nay' on this matter within twenty-four hours. As to the maid, Lena, I think you are laying too much stress upon her possible knowledge of her mistress's movements.

"If she had been tied down to secrecy by promise of reward, she would naturally, now that all such promises are rendered futile, reveal all she knows on the matter—she has nothing to gain by keeping the secrets of the dead."

===Chinese===
戴尔先生把信搁到一边，不满地哼了一声。

"哼，"他自言自语，"我猜她是指望我能从字里行间读出弦外之音，可我要是真见鬼了才摸得着头尾。依我看，她这会儿是有点儿偏到岔路上去了；不妨给她提个醒。"于是他潦草地写了几行简短的回信，如下：——

"我猜你眼下正集中精力，要查出戈尔丁小姐离家期间都去了些什么地方。依我看，这事在巴黎办比在朗福德庄办来得合适。她手指上那枚戒指，必然意味着她在某处行过婚礼，而既然一两天前有人在巴黎瞧见过她，那么这仪式十有八九就是在那边举行的。巴黎警方在二十四小时之内，就能给你个'是'或'否'的确切答复。至于女仆莉娜，我看你把她可能知晓女主人行踪这一点，看得过重了。

"她若是从前受人许下赏金的承诺、被拴住了舌头不许说，那么如今这类承诺既已统统落空，她自然会把她所知的和盘托出——替死者守秘密，于她再无半点好处。"

===Original===
This letter crossed on its road a telegram from Loveday running thus:—

"Inquest over. Verdict, 'Found drowned, but how deceased got into the water there is no evidence to show.' Funeral takes place tomorrow; Mr. Golding delirious with brain fever."

On the day following Mr. Dyer received a second letter from Loveday. Thus it ran:—

"The funeral is over; Mr. Golding is much worse. I have dispatched Lena to Paris, telling her I require her services there to follow up a clue I hold respecting Mr. Gordon Cleeve, and promising her rewards commensurate with the manner in which she carries out my orders. I have also written to Lord Guilleroy, telling him the sort of assistance I require from him. If he is the man I take him for he will be more useful to me than all the Paris police put together. I will answer your letter in detail in a day or two. The neighbourhood is still in a state of great excitement, and all sorts of wild reports are flying about. Ramsay and Dulau have traced a lady, dressed in dark blue serge, and answering in other respects to Miss Golding's description, from the Gare du Midi, Paris, step by step to her arrival at Langford Cross, whence, poor thing, she must have walked through the pouring rain to the Hall. I do not see, however, that this information helps us forward one step towards the solution of the mystery of the girl's disappearance. Ramsay is a little inclined to criticize what he calls my 'leisurely handling' of the case. Mrs. Greenhow, who is a terribly empty-headed, but at the same time essentially hard-natured little woman, appears disposed to follow suit, and has more than once thrown out hints that my stay in the house is being unnecessarily prolonged. As there is practically no further necessity for my remaining at the Hall, I have told her that I shall today take up my quarters at the Roebuck Inn (by courtesy hotel), at Langford Cross. I believe she is unfeignedly glad at what she considers the ending of the affair. The imperious yet fascinating young lady no doubt ruled her and the household generally with a rod of iron, and the little woman, I feel sure, if she had dared, would have ordered bonfires and a general rejoicing on the day of the funeral. Well, I have not much sympathy with her, and am preparing for her a shock to her not too-sensitive nerves which she little suspects. My chief anxiety at the present moment is Mr. Golding, who still remains unconscious. I have requested the doctors to send me two bulletins daily of his condition, which I fear is a most serious one."

===Chinese===
这封信在路上，与洛芙迪拍来的一封电报迎头错过去了。电报上写道：——

"验尸庭审已毕。裁决：'溺毙，然死者如何入水，并无证据可资表明。'葬礼明日举行；戈尔丁先生因脑炎高烧，神志昏迷。"

又过了一天，戴尔先生收到洛芙迪的第二封信。信里这样写道：——

"葬礼已毕；戈尔丁先生的病情更重了。我已把莉娜打发去巴黎，告诉她我在那边需要她效力，好顺着我手头所掌握的关于戈登·克利夫先生的一条线索追查下去，并许她以与她办事的卖力程度相称的赏金。我也已写信给吉勒罗伊勋爵，把我要他帮衬的那一类事告诉了他。他若真是我看走不了眼的那种人，那么全巴黎的警察加在一块儿，也抵不上他一个对我有用。一两天内，我会详细回您的信。左近一带依旧群情激奋，种种荒诞不经的传言四处乱飞。拉姆齐和迪洛已经追查出，有一位身穿深蓝色哔叽裙服、其余形貌也与戈尔丁小姐相符的女士，从巴黎的南方车站（Gare du Midi）起，一站一站、一步一步，直到她抵达朗福德克罗斯——可怜的人，从那儿她必定是冒着瓢泼大雨一路走到庄园的。然而我看不出，这一番情形在解开姑娘失踪之谜上，能助我们向前推进一步。拉姆齐略微有些非议我所称之为对这案子'不紧不慢的拿捏'。格林豪太太这个头脑空空如也、骨子里却生性凉薄的小女人，似乎也有意跟着说嘴，不止一次放出话来，说我在宅子里住得未免太久了，大可不必。既然我留在庄子里已无多少实际的必要，我便告诉她，我今日就要搬到朗福德克罗斯那家'雄鹿旅馆'（Roebuck Inn，姑且算是家客栈）去住。我相信，对于她所认定的这桩事的收场，她是打心眼里高兴的。那位专横却又迷人得紧的年轻小姐，从前无疑是拿铁腕管着她、管着这一整宅子的人家的；我敢说，这小女人若是胆子够大，出殡那天就要下令点起篝火、举宅欢庆了。哼，我对她没多少同情，正预备给她那一向不大敏感的神经来一下她万万料想不到的惊吓。眼下我最挂心的，是一直昏迷不醒的戈尔丁先生。我已请医生们每日给我送两回病情简报，我怕他的情形实在凶险得很。"

===Original===
There could be little doubt on this head. The doctors' verdict on the day that Loveday left Langford Hall for "The Roebuck" was: "Absolutely no hope." The bulletin brought to her on the following morning was "Condition remains unchanged." On the third day, however, the report was "Slight improvement." Then followed the welcome bulletins of "Improvement maintained," and "Out of danger," to be followed by the most welcome report of all: "Is making steady progress towards recovery."

"It is Mr. Golding's illness that has kept me here so long," said Loveday to Inspector Ramsay, as if by way of apology for her continued presence on the scene. "I think, however, I can see my way to departure now. Going to Paris? Oh, dear me, no. I have telegraphed to Mr. Dyer to expect me back the day after tomorrow; if you will like to come to me here, or will meet me at Langford Cross Station, I will give you a full report of all I have done since I took the case in hand. Now I am going to the Hall to ascertain at what hour tomorrow it will be convenient for me to say goodbye to Mr. Golding."

===Chinese===
这一点几乎无可置疑。洛芙迪离开朗福德庄搬去"雄鹿旅馆"那天，医生们的判断是："绝无指望。"次日早晨送到她手里的简报是"病情依旧"。可到了第三天，消息却是"略有起色"。随后便是那几条令人宽慰的简报："起色持续"，"脱离危险"，再接下去便是所有简报里最叫人宽慰的一条："正稳步走向痊愈。"

"正是戈尔丁先生的病，把我留在这儿这么久，"洛芙迪对拉姆齐巡官说，像是为她还逗留在场上而略致歉意。"不过我想，眼下我已看得出脱身的路子了。去巴黎？哎哟，才不呢。我已经给戴尔先生拍了电报，让他后天等我回去；你若是愿意上我这里来，或是到朗福德克罗斯车站来会我，我会把我自接手此案以来所做的一切，给你一份详尽的禀报。这会儿我要去一趟庄园，问问明白明天几时方便我去向戈尔丁先生告辞。"

===Original===
More than this Ramsay was unable to extract from Miss Brooke. His open strictures upon what he called her "leisurely handling of the case" had put her upon her mettle, and she had decided that Ramsay and his colleagues should be taught that Lynch Court had a special way of doing things, and could hold its own with the best.

On her way to the Hall Loveday called at the post-office, and there had a letter with a London postmark handed to her. This she at once opened and read, and then dispatched a reply to it by telegram. The reply was an enigmatic one to the village postmaster, for Loveday, after a few casual questions as to his knowledge of Continental languages, chose German as her medium of communication. The address, however, "to Lord Guilleroy, at Charing Cross Hotel," was plain reading enough.

===Chinese===
再多的话，拉姆齐也没法从布鲁克小姐嘴里套出来了。他公然非议她那所谓"不紧不慢的拿捏"，反倒激起了她的好胜之心，她已拿定主意，要让拉姆齐和他那班同僚领教领教：林奇大院办事自有其一套特别的门道，比起最高明的同行也毫不逊色。

洛芙迪去庄园的路上，顺道进了邮局，在那儿接到一封盖着伦敦邮戳的信递到了她手里。她立刻拆开读完，随即拍了一封电报作复。这封回电对村里那位邮局局长来说，未免像个谜——因为洛芙迪在随口问了他几句通不通晓欧陆语言之后，便选中德语作为她通信的工具。不过那收件地址——"查令十字饭店（Charing Cross Hotel），吉勒罗伊勋爵收"——倒是清清楚楚，一目了然。

===Original===
At the Hall Loveday found Mrs. Greenhow in an active state of mind. Mr. Golding, she informed her with a sweet effusiveness, would come downstairs for a short time on the following day, and she was doing all that lay in her power to put out of sight anything that might awaken painful recollections. "I have had dear René's harp removed to a lumber room, her portrait taken down from the library wall," she said, in her usual purring tones; "and her davenport is being wheeled into my own sitting-room. Poor dear René! If only she could have been taught to govern her willful temper a happier fate might have befallen her. What that fate was I suppose we shall never know now."

Loveday's only reply to this was to ask for an exact report of the doctor's opinion of Mr. Golding's condition. Mrs. Greenhow put her handkerchief to her eyes as she answered that Doctor Godwin's opinion was that, so far as bodily strength was concerned, he was considerably better, but that his mental condition was a serious one. His brain appeared to be in a state of semi-stupefaction, which it was possible might be indicative of the softening of its tissues.

===Chinese===
到了庄园，洛芙迪见格林豪太太正忙得不亦乐乎。她以一股甜甜的热络劲儿告诉洛芙迪，戈尔丁先生明天要下楼来坐一小会儿，她正竭尽全力，把一切可能勾起痛苦回忆的东西都收拾到看不见的地方去。"我已经把亲爱的蕾妮那架竖琴搬到杂物房去了，把她的画像从书房墙上摘下来了，"她用平日那种打呼噜似的腔调说，"她那张小写字台，也正给推到我自家的起居室里去。可怜的亲爱的蕾妮！要是当初能教她管住自己那任性的性子，落在她头上的命运也许就会好些。至于那命运到底是什么，我想我们如今是永远无从知晓了。"

洛芙迪对这番话唯一的回应，是索要一份医生对戈尔丁先生病情的确切意见。格林豪太太把手帕按到眼上，答道，戈德温医生（Doctor Godwin）的意见是：就体力而论，他已大有起色，只是他的精神状态堪忧。他的脑子似乎处在一种半昏迷的状态之中，这有可能正是脑组织软化的征兆。

===Original===
Loveday expressed a wish to see this doctor—to time her farewell visit to Mr. Golding on the following day with Dr. Godwin's daily call. In fact, she would like a little private talk with him before she went in to see his patient.

To all this Mrs. Greenhow offered no objection. Lady detectives, she said to herself, were a race apart, and had a curious way of doing things; but, thank Heaven, she would soon see the last of this one!

---

===Chinese===
洛芙迪表示想见一见这位医生——把她明天向戈尔丁先生告辞的那趟拜访，安排在戈德温医生每日出诊的时候。其实，她想在他进去看病人之前，先和他私下谈上一会儿。

对这一切，格林豪太太都没有异议。女侦探这种人——她在心里说——是自成一族的另类，办事的门道古怪得很；不过，谢天谢地，她马上就要把这个女侦探打发走了！

---

===Original===
The stormy autumnal weather had given place now to a brief spell of late summer sunshine, and on the last day of her visit to Langford, Miss Brooke had a cheerier view of the Hall and its surroundings than she had had on the day of her arrival there. The trout stream had retreated to its natural proportions, and showed like a streak of molten silver—not a grey, turbid flood—in the bright sunlight that played at hide-and-seek between the branches of the stript elms. Even the old rooks seemed to have a cheery undernote to their "caw, caw" as they wheeled about the old house; and Dryad himself, as he once more came bounding forth to greet her, appeared to her fancy to have a less dolorous ring in his noisy bark.

"That dog is a perfect nuisance—has been utterly spoilt. I must have him chained up," said Mrs. Greenhow, as she led the way into a room where Dr. Godwin sat awaiting Loveday. She introduced them one to the other. "Shall I remain, or do you wish to converse alone?" she asked.

And as Loveday answered with decision "Alone," the little woman had no choice but to withdraw, wondering once more over the vagaries of lady detectives.

===Chinese===
风暴似的秋天气象，如今已让位给一段短暂的、夏日尾声般的晴好时光。在洛芙迪造访朗福德的最后一天，她眼里所见的庄园及其四围，比她初到那天要明朗得多。那条鳟鱼溪已退回到它原本的模样，在落尽了叶子的榆树枝桠间明灭嬉戏的灿烂日光下，它看着像一道熔化的银液——再不是灰蒙蒙、浑浊浊的一片洪流了。就连宅子上空盘旋的那些老白嘴鸦，"呱、呱"的叫声里似乎也带着一丝轻快的底调；而德里亚德呢，它又一次蹦跳着迎出来见她，在她听来，连它那聒噪的吠声里，似乎也没了先前那种凄惶的调子。

"这条狗真是个大麻烦——简直给宠坏了。我非得把它拴起来不可，"格林豪太太说着，把路引进一间屋子，戈德温医生正坐在里头等着洛芙迪。她替双方引见了。"我留着呢，还是你们要单独谈？"她问。

洛芙迪斩钉截铁地答了声"单独"，这小女人便无可奈何，只得退了出去，心里又一次纳罕起女侦探这种人的种种怪脾气来。

===Original===
Half an hour afterwards the doctor, a clever-looking, active little man, led the way into the library where Mr. Golding was seated.

Loveday was greatly shocked at the change which a few days' illness had wrought in him. His chair was drawn close to the window, and the autumn sunshine that filled the room threw into pitiful relief his shrunken frame and pallid face, aged now by about a dozen years. His eyes were closed, his head was bent low on his breast, and he did not lift it as the door opened.

"You need not remain," said Dr. Godwin to the nurse, who rose as they entered; and Loveday and the doctor were left alone with the patient.

===Chinese===
半个钟头之后，医生——一个看去很精明、利落的小个子男人——领路走进了书房，戈尔丁先生正坐在里头。

几天的病把戈尔丁先生折磨成的这副模样，叫洛芙迪大吃一惊。他的椅子拉得离窗口很近，满屋的秋日阳光把他那消瘦的身形、苍白的脸照得分外惹人怜悯——那张脸如今像是凭空老了十来岁。他双眼紧闭，头低低地垂在胸前，门开了，他也没有抬一抬。

"你不必留着了，"戈德温医生对那个见他们进来便站起身去的护士说；于是洛芙迪和医生便单独和病人留在了屋里。

===Original===
Loveday drew near softly. "I am going back to town this evening, and have come to say goodbye," she said, extending her hand.

Mr. Golding opened his eyes, staring vaguely at the extended hand. "To say goodbye!" he repeated, in a dreamy, faraway tone.

"I am Miss Brooke," Loveday explained. "I came down from London to investigate the strange circumstances connected with your daughter's disappearance."

"My daughter's disappearance!" He started and began to tremble violently.

The doctor had his hand on his patient's pulse now.

===Chinese===
洛芙迪轻轻地走近。"我今晚就回城去了，特来告辞，"她说着，伸出手去。

戈尔丁先生睁开眼，茫然地盯着那只伸过来的手。"来告辞！"他重复道，声音像在梦里，飘飘忽忽的。

"我是布鲁克小姐，"洛芙迪解释道，"我从伦敦下来，是为了查清与令爱失踪有关的那些离奇情形。"

"我女儿的失踪！"他一惊，猛地剧烈地颤抖起来。

这会儿医生已把手搭在了病人的脉搏上。

===Original===
"I have conducted my investigations under somewhat disadvantageous circumstances," Loveday went on quietly, "and, for a time, with but little result. A few days back, however, I received important information from Lord Guilleroy, and today I have seen and communicated with him. In fact, it was his carriage that brought me to your house this afternoon."

"Lord Guilleroy!" repeated Mr. Golding slowly. His voice had a more natural ring in it; recollection, although, perhaps, a painful one, seemed to sound in it.

"Yes. He said he would wander about the park until I had seen and prepared you for his visit. Ah! there he is coming up the drive."

Here she drew back the curtain that half draped the open window.

This window commanded a good view of the drive, with its overarching elms, that led from the lodge gates to the house. Along that drive two persons were advancing at that moment in leisurely fashion; one of those two was undoubtedly Lord Guilleroy, the other was a tall, graceful girl, dressed in deep mourning.

===Chinese===
"我在一些颇为不利的情形下展开了我的调查，"洛芙迪平静地接着说，"有一阵子，几乎没什么结果。不过几天前，我从吉勒罗伊勋爵那里收到了要紧的消息，今天我又见着了他，和他通了话。其实，今天下午正是他的马车把我送到您府上的。"

"吉勒罗伊勋爵！"戈尔丁先生慢慢地重复道。他的嗓音里多了几分自然的声息；回忆——虽说也许是痛苦的回忆——似乎在其中回响起来。

"是的。他说他要在我见过您、为您做好准备之前，先在园子里散散步。啊！他这不正顺着车道走过来了。"

说着，她拉开了那半掩着敞开窗户的帷幔。

从这扇窗子望去，能把那条车道一览无余——车道两侧榆树成拱，从门房的大门一直通到宅子。此刻那车道上正有两个人慢悠悠地走来；其中一位无疑是吉勒罗伊勋爵，另一位是个高挑、身段婀娜的姑娘，一身重孝。

===Original===
Mr. Golding's eyes followed Loveday's at first with a blank, expressionless stare. Then, little by little, that stare changed into a look of intelligence and recognition. His face grew ashen white, then a wave of colour swept over it.

"Lord Guilleroy, yes," he said, panting and struggling for breath. "But—but who is that walking with him? Tell me, tell me quickly, for the love of Heaven!"

He tried to rise to his feet, but his limbs failed him. The doctor poured out a cordial, and put it to his lips.

"Drink this, please," he said. "Now tell him quickly," he whispered to Loveday.

"That young lady," she resumed calmly, "is your daughter René. She drove up with me and Lord Guilleroy from Langford Cross. Shall I ask her to come in and see you? She is only waiting for Dr. Godwin's permission to do so."

===Chinese===
戈尔丁先生的眼起初跟着洛芙迪的视线望去，茫然地、毫无表情地瞪着。渐渐地，那凝视化作了心领神会与辨认的神色。他脸色先是变得惨白，随即一阵红潮又掠过他的脸。

"吉勒罗伊勋爵，是的，"他喘着气，艰难地挣着气说，"可——可跟他一道走着的那是谁？告诉我，快告诉我，看在老天的份上！"

他挣扎着想站起来，可两条腿不听使唤。医生斟了一杯提神的药酒，送到他唇边。

"请把这个喝了，"他说，"这会儿快告诉他，"他低声对洛芙迪说。

"那位年轻小姐，"她沉着地接下去说，"就是令爱蕾妮。是她和我、还有吉勒罗伊勋爵，一同从朗福德克罗斯乘车过来的。可要我叫她进来见您？她只等戈德温医生一点头就进来了。"

===Original===
Time to grant or refuse that permission, however, was not accorded to Dr. Godwin. René—a sadder, sweeter-faced René than the one who had so impetuously discarded home and father—now stood outside in the "half-sun, half-shade" of the verandah, and had caught the sound of Loveday's last words.

She swept impetuously past her into the room.

"Father, father!" she said, as she knelt down beside his chair, "I have come back at last! Are you not glad to see me?"

---

===Chinese===
然而，准与不准这点工夫，戈德温医生也没得到。蕾妮——一个比那个如此性急地抛弃了家和父亲的蕾妮，更添了几分忧愁、面容也更温柔了些的蕾妮——此刻正站在外头游廊那"半阴半阳"的光影里，把洛芙迪最后那几句话听了进去。

她一阵风似的从她身边掠过，冲进了屋里。

"父亲，父亲！"她跪倒在他椅子旁，说道，"我终于回来了！您见到我，难道不高兴吗？"

---

===Original===
"I daresay it all seems very mysterious to you," said Loveday to Inspector Ramsay, as together they paced the platform of Langford Cross Station, waiting for the incoming of the London train, "but, I assure you, it all admits of the easiest and simplest of explanations—Who on earth was it that the inquest was held over, and who was buried about a week ago, do you say? Oh, that was Mr. Golding's wife, Irené, daughter of Count Mascagni, of Alguida, in South Italy, whom everyone believed to be dead. It is her history that holds the key to the whole affair from first to last. I will begin at the beginning, and tell you her story as nearly as possible as it was told to me. To be quite frank with you, I would have admitted you long ago into my confidence, and told you, step by step, how things were working themselves out, if you had not offended me by criticizing my method of doing my work."

"I'm sure I'm very sorry," here broke in Ramsay in a deprecating tone.

===Chinese===
"我敢说，这一切在你看来都十分蹊跷，"洛芙迪对拉姆齐巡官说——此时两人正在朗福德克罗斯车站的月台上一道踱着步，等候开来的那班伦敦火车——"可我向你保证，这一切都极容易、极简单地就能解释明白——你说，验尸庭审究竟是冲着谁来的？大约一个礼拜前入土为安的，又是谁？噢，那是戈尔丁先生的妻子，伊雷娜——意大利南部阿尔圭达（Alguida）的马斯卡尼伯爵（Count Mascagni）之女伊雷娜·马斯卡尼（Irené Mascagni）——一个人人都以为早已死了的人。正是她的来历，握着打头到尾整桩案的钥匙。我从头讲起，把她的事尽量照人家讲给我听的那样，原原本本讲给你听。坦白跟你说，要不是你非议我办事的法子、把我给得罪了，我早就把你引为知己，一步一步地把事情怎样水落石出讲给你听了。"

"那可真是对不住得很，"拉姆齐这时用一种告罪的口气插嘴道。

===Original===
"Oh, pray don't mention it. Let me see, where was I? Ah, I must go back some nineteen or twenty years in Mr. Golding's life in order to make things clear to you. The particulars which I had from Mr. Dyer, and which I fancy you supplied him with, respecting Mr. Golding's early life were so meager that directly I arrived at the Hall I set to work to supplement them; this I contrived to do in a before-dinner chat with Lena, Miss Golding's maid. I found out through her that Irené Mascagni was a typical Italian woman of the half-educated, passionate, beautiful, animal kind, and that Mr. Golding's early married life was anything but a happy one. Irené was motherless, and had been so spoilt from babyhood upwards by her old nurse, Lena's aunt, that she could not brook the slightest opposition to her whims and wishes. She was a great coquette also; lovers were an absolute necessity to her. Remonstrance on Mr. Golding's part was useless; Irené met it by appeals to her father for protection against what she considered her husband's brutality; in consequence, a serious quarrel ensued between the Count and Mr. Golding, and when the latter announced his intention of breaking up his Italian home and buying an estate in England, Irené, accompanied by her nurse, Antonia, left her husband and little daughter and went back to her father's house, vowing that nothing would induce her to leave her beloved Italy. At this crisis in his affairs, Mr. Golding was suddenly compelled to undertake a journey to Australia to adjust certain complicated matters of business. He took with him on this voyage his little girl, René, and her nurse—now her maid, Lena. The visit to Australia in all occupied about six months. During that time no communication of any sort passed between him and his wife or her father. He resolved, however, to make one more effort to induce Irené to return to her home and her duty; and, with this object, he went to Naples on his return to Europe and wrote to his wife from there, asking her to appoint a day for a meeting. In reply to this letter he received a visit from Antonia, who, with a great show of sorrow, informed him that Irené had caught a fever during his absence, and had died, and now lay buried in the family vault at Alguida. Mr. Golding's grief at the tidings was no doubt mitigated by the recollection of the unfortunate married life he had led. He made no attempt to communicate with Count Mascagni, started at once for England, and set up his establishment at Langford Hall. All this, with the exception of the name of Irené's father and that of his estate, was told me by Lena, who, I may mention in passing, laid great stress upon the wonderful likeness that existed between Miss Golding and her mother. She was, she said, the exact counterpart of what her mother had been at her age."

===Chinese===
"哎，千万别放在心上。让我想想，我说到哪儿了？啊，为了给你说个明白，我得倒回到戈尔丁先生一生里大约十九二十年前去。我从戴尔先生那里得来的——我想是你供给他的吧——关于戈尔丁先生早年生活的种种，实在太简略了，所以我一到庄园，便着手加以补充；这事我是在饭前与戈尔丁小姐的女仆莉娜闲谈时办成的。通过她我才知道，伊雷娜·马斯卡尼是个典型的意大利女人，属于那种半受教育、热情如火、美貌而近于兽性的一类，而戈尔丁先生早年的婚姻生活也一点都不幸福。伊雷娜自幼丧母，从小就被她的老奶娘——也就是莉娜的姨妈——娇纵惯了，以至于她丝毫不能忍受别人对她那许多兴致和心愿有半点违拗。她又是个极爱卖弄风情的人；追求者于她简直是必不可少的东西。戈尔丁先生那方面的规劝毫无用处；伊雷娜便向父亲求告，请父亲保护她、庇护她免受她所谓丈夫的粗暴对待；结果是，伯爵与戈尔丁先生之间爆发了一场严重的争吵，待到后者宣布要解散他那个意大利的家、到英国去买一份田产时，伊雷娜便由她的奶娘安东妮娅（Antonia）陪伴着，抛下丈夫和年幼的女儿，回到了父亲家中，发誓说什么也不能叫她离开她心爱的意大利。就在他这一摊子事的当口，戈尔丁先生忽然不得不为理清几桩繁杂的生意上的事，前往澳大利亚一趟。这趟远行他带上了小女儿蕾妮，和她的奶娘——如今的女仆莉娜。这趟澳大利亚之行前后约莫半年。这半年里，他与妻子、与岳父之间，没有通过任何音讯。然而他决心再尽最后一次力，劝伊雷娜回到她的家和她的本分上来；为此，他在返回欧洲时特意去了那不勒斯，从那里写信给妻子，请她定个日子见上一面。这封信的回音，是安东妮娅亲自登门造访；她装出一副悲痛欲绝的模样，告诉他伊雷娜在他外出期间染了一场热病，已经死了，如今就葬在阿尔圭达的家族墓穴里。戈尔丁先生闻讯后的悲伤，无疑因他回想自己那段不幸的婚后生活而有所冲淡。他没有再去与马斯卡尼伯爵联系，当即动身去了英国，在朗福德庄安下了家。这一切——除了伊雷娜父亲的名字和他那份田产的名字之外——都是莉娜讲给我听的；顺便提一句，莉娜还格外着重地强调，戈尔丁小姐与她的母亲长得惊人地相像。她说，她简直就是她母亲在她那个年纪时一模一样的翻版。"

===Original===
"It is marvelous to me how you contrived to get anything out of that woman Lena," said Ramsay; "she was most obstinately taciturn with me."

"Pardon me if I say that was because she had been most injudiciously handled. In the circumstances it would never have occurred to me to put a single direct question to her, although I, like you, felt convinced that she was the one and only person likely to be in her young mistress's confidence. So fully imbued was I with this idea that I felt certain that, if she could be sent out of the house on any pretext, by closely following her movements we should, sooner or later, come upon the traces of Miss Golding. To attain this end, I feigned suspicion of Mr. Gordon Cleeve, and promised her rewards if she would bring me tidings of his doings. This was to pave the way to dismissing her on a journey to Italy. It also had the most welcome effect of calming her mind and convincing her of my belief in her innocence. With her fears thus allayed, I found her no longer sullen but communicative to a degree."

===Chinese===
"你竟能从莉娜那个女人嘴里套出话来，我真觉得不可思议，"拉姆齐说，"她对我可是固执得不吱一声。"

"恕我直言，那是因为对付她的法子太不得当了。在那种情形下，我绝不会向她直统统地问上一句半句，虽说我和你一样，也认定她是唯一可能被她那位年轻女主引为心腹的人。我既如此深信这一点，便认定：只要能找个借口把她打发出门，紧紧盯着她的行踪，我们迟早会撞见戈尔丁小姐的踪迹。为达此目的，我佯装怀疑戈登·克利夫先生，许她赏金，要她把他的动静报给我。这一着既为日后把她打发去意大利铺了路，又起了最叫我如愿以偿的效果——安了她的心，让她相信我相信她是清白的。她既去掉了那层戒惧，我发现她便不再沉着脸，反倒话多得不得了。"

===Original===
"Pardon my interrupting you at this point, but will you kindly tell me what, in the first instance, aroused your suspicious as to the identity of the person 'found drowned' by the coroner's jury?"

"Lena's conduct when the body was brought into the house. I should, however, tell you that a keynote of suspicion as to the possibility of Mrs. Golding being still alive had been struck when, as I sat writing at Miss Golding's davenport, I found the words 'Mia Madre' scribbled here and there on her blotting-pad. Now what, I said to myself, could, after all these years, have turned her thoughts to her mother and her early Italian home? The wedding-ring on the lady's finger, coupled with Lena's statement as to Miss Golding's marvelous likeness to her mother, together with an exclamation of Mr. Golding's, after identifying the body, that his daughter had 'aged by a dozen years,' made these suspicions grow stronger. It was, however, Lena's own conduct that resolved them into positive certainty. I watched her narrowly after the body had been brought into the house. At first her grief was passionate and intense, and in it she let fall—in Italian—the extraordinary exclamation that a woman should break her heart for her lover, not for her mother. Then she, too, went into the room where the body lay—went in weeping, came out dry-eyed, and in the most methodical manner set to work to perform the last sad offices for the dead."

===Chinese===
"请原谅我在这儿打断你一下，可你能不能告诉我，起初究竟是什么，叫你起了疑心，怀疑验尸陪审团所裁定'溺毙'的那个人，身份有诈？"

"是遗体被抬进宅子之后，莉娜的举动。不过我该告诉你，早先还有一个叫我疑心戈尔丁夫人也许还活着的调子，已然敲响了——那时我坐在戈尔丁小姐的小写字台前写信，发现她的吸墨纸板上，东一处西一处地涂着'Mia Madre'（我的母亲）几个字。我心里便说，隔了这么多年，究竟是什么，又能叫她想起她母亲、想起她早年的那个意大利的家呢？那位女士手指上的结婚戒指，加上莉娜所说戈尔丁小姐与母亲惊人相像的那一番话，再配上戈尔丁先生认尸之后那一句脱口而出的话——说他女儿'老了十来岁'——这一切都叫我的疑心越发重了。然而，把疑心化作确凿把握的，到底还是莉娜本人的举动。遗体抬进宅子之后，我紧紧盯着她。起初她的悲痛炽烈而猛烈，悲痛之中，她脱口而出——是用意大利语——一句非同寻常的话：一个女人，原该为她的情人而心碎，不该为她的母亲。随后，她也走进了停放遗体的那间屋——她是流着泪进去的，出来时却已双目干涸，随即有条不紊地着手为亡者尽那最后凄凉的种种礼数。"

===Original===
"Ah, yes, I see. Pray go on."

"It was on the day of the funeral, if you remember, that I dispatched Lena to Paris. I had previously written to Lord Guilleroy, hinting my suspicions, and begging him, in spite of everything, to remain at Paris, and to carry out any directions I might send him to the very letter. On dispatching Lena, I again wrote to him, telling him when she would arrive, where she would put up, and bidding him keep his eye on her, and follow her movements step by step. From Paris, I sent Lena on to Naples, bidding her await further orders there, and, all unknown to her, the train that carried her thither, carried also Lord Guilleroy. Naples had been the only place she had mentioned to me by name in her gossip about her life in Italy, but I felt confident, from some casual remarks she had let fall, that Irené Mascagni's early home, as well, also, as the home of her own lover, was within easy reach of the city. It was only natural to conjecture that if I kept her waiting there for orders she would utilize the opportunity for paying a visit to her friends and relatives, and also to her young mistress, if she were, as I supposed, in that neighbourhood. The result proved my conjecture correct."

===Chinese===
"啊，是了，我明白了。请接着往下说。"

"你该记得，正是在出殡那天，我把莉娜打发去了巴黎。在那之前，我已写信给吉勒罗伊勋爵，向他暗示了我的疑心，求他无论如何留在巴黎，把我可能发给他的任何吩咐，一字不差地照办。打发莉娜走的时候，我又给他写了一封信，告诉他莉娜几时到、住哪家客栈，吩咐他盯着她，一步一步地跟着她的行踪。从巴黎，我又把莉娜打发去那不勒斯，让她在那儿等候进一步的吩咐；而她浑然不觉的是，载她去那儿的那班火车上，也载着吉勒罗伊勋爵。那不勒斯是她在闲聊意大利生活时唯一向我提过名字的地方，但从她不经意间漏出的片言只语里，我有把握断定，伊雷娜·马斯卡尼早年的家，连带莉娜她自己那个情人的家，都在离那座城不远的地方。由此很自然便可推测：我若把她晾在那儿等吩咐，她必定会借这机会去探望亲友，也去探望她那位年轻的女主——倘若正如我所料，女主就在那一带的话。后来的结果证明，我猜对了。"

===Original===
"And Lord Guilleroy, thus following her movements, step by step, came upon her and Miss Golding in company?"

"He did. I think Lord Guilleroy deserves high encomium for the way in which he performed his share in this somewhat intricate case. No trained detective could have done better. He tracked Lena home to Alguida, a small hamlet within fifteen miles of Naples, and came upon her talking to Miss Golding, who stood at the gate of her grandfather's château dressed in her mother's Neapolitan dress. Miss Golding was unfeignedly glad to be taken possession of, so to speak, by one of her father's English friends, for she was becoming nervous and distressed at the position in which she found herself. Her mother was dead; her grandfather, a man of a violent temper, refused to allow her to leave his château, as he alleged he required, in his old age, the attendance of one who was his own kith and kin. Also there was in her mind a natural shrinking from the story she would have to tell her father, and the fear lest he might not be willing to forgive her for the part she had played. Nothing could have been more opportune than Lord Guilleroy's arrival. Miss Golding accorded to him her full confidence, and from this point the story ceases to be mine and becomes Lord Guilleroy's as communicated to him by Miss Golding."

===Chinese===
"那么吉勒罗伊勋爵这样一步步跟着她的行踪，就撞见她跟戈尔丁小姐待在一块儿了？"

"正是。我以为，吉勒罗伊勋爵在这桩颇为曲折的案子里，把他那一摊事办得十分漂亮，值得大加称道。再训练有素的侦探也不能办得比他更好了。他一路追踪莉娜，追到了阿尔圭达——那不勒斯十五英里外的一个小村子——正撞见她在跟戈尔丁小姐说话，小姐就站在她外祖父城堡的大门口，身上穿着她母亲那身那不勒斯式的衣裙。戈尔丁小姐见有她父亲的一位英国朋友，可以说是把她接管了过去，那是打心眼里高兴的，因为她身处的那般境地，正叫她越来越不安、越来越苦恼。她的母亲已经死了；她的外祖父是个脾气暴烈的人，不肯放她离开城堡，说是一把年纪了，身边离不得一个与他血脉相亲的人伺候。再者，她心里也自然而然地畏缩着，怕把她不得不对父亲讲的那番话讲出口，又怕他不肯宽恕她所扮下的那个角色。吉勒罗伊勋爵的到来，真是再及时也没有了。戈尔丁小姐对他推心置腹；从这一处起，这故事就不再是我的，而成了吉勒罗伊勋爵的——是戈尔丁小姐讲给他听的。"

===Original===
"It is, in fact, the other half of the story that was told you by Lena?"

"It is; it starts from the period, twelve years back, when Mrs. Golding was supposed by her husband and child to be dead. Instead of dying, however, she had, after a month's stay at her father's lonely country house, joined a company of actors, then passing through Alguida. Her great personal beauty ensured her ready admission to the corps; and in her new life, no doubt, her vanity and innate love of coquetry found ample gratification. The faithful old nurse had followed her in her new career; the dramatic corps was actually in Naples when Mr. Golding arrived there, and the two women, neither of whom was disposed to enter upon the dull routine of English domestic life, had fabricated the lie in order the more effectually to retain their liberty. It is most probable that Count Mascagni knew nothing whatever of his daughter's movements at this period of her career. It is possible that, after a time, he may have believed her to be dead, for eleven years passed without his receiving any communication from her."

===Chinese===
"这么着，这其实是你从莉娜那里听来的那故事的另一半？"

"正是；它从十二年前那段日子讲起——那时戈尔丁夫人在丈夫和女儿心目中已经死了。然而她并没有死，她在父亲那座偏僻的乡间宅子里住了一个月之后，便加入了一个正路过阿尔圭达的戏班子。她那惊人的美貌，使她轻而易举地被班子收了进去；而在这种新生活里，她的虚荣心和她天性里对卖弄风情的爱好，无疑得到了充分的满足。那忠心的老奶娘也跟着她踏上了这条新路；戈尔丁先生到那不勒斯时，那个戏班子恰巧就在那不勒斯，这两个女人——哪一个都不情愿去过英国人家那种沉闷刻板的日子——便编出了那个谎，好把自己的自由抓得更牢。马斯卡尼伯爵对他女儿这一段的行踪，多半是一无所知。过了一些时日，他也许就信了她已死，因为整整十一年过去，他没有收到过她半个字的音讯。"

===Original===
"Eleven years! Was she on the stage the whole of that time?"

"I have not been able to ascertain—in fact, I have not been very keen in making inquiries on this point, for it really is of little or no importance to the case. So far as we are concerned, her career is of importance only after her return to her father's house, now about a year ago. She came back one day, attended by Antonia, evidently out of health and in great poverty. Her father received her back conditionally; she had disgraced him and his ancient name, he said; dead she was supposed to be by her friends, dead she must remain—she must go nowhere, she must see no one."

===Chinese===
"十一年！这十一年她都在唱戏吗？"

"这一点我没能查实——其实，我也没怎么热心去打听这一点，因为它对本案实在无足轻重。就我们而言，她那一段经历要紧的，只是她回到父亲家中以后的事——那大约是一年前。有一天她回来了，由安东妮娅陪着，瞧着身子骨很差，穷困得很。她父亲是有条件地重新收留她的；据他说，她辱没了他和他那古老的姓氏；亲友们既当她已经死了，她就得继续当死人——哪儿也不许去，谁也不许见。"

===Original===
"Ah, a sad story! And I suppose after a time the poor woman's thoughts flew to her husband and little daughter?"

"Yes. Antonia wrote to Lena that the mother was dying for the sight of her child, and implored her to tell René that her mother lived—a mother who had been cruelly treated alike by husband and father—and beg her, at all hazards, to come to her, that she might clasp her in her arms before the shadows of death closed in around her. This part of the story I had from René herself as we drove together to the Hall. The girl told me that when she read that letter all her blood was stirred within her. She was seized with a burning desire there and then to kiss that mother and to right her wrongs. For the moment she hated her father, felt that she must at once confront him and denounce him for his cruelty. Second thoughts suggested another course. Her father might forbid her all intercourse with her mother; she had plenty of money, why not start for Italy at once, and from her mother's lips dictate to her father the terms on which she would return to her English home? So the journey was planned, and Lena was promised by the young lady a pair of her handsome diamond earrings if she kept her secret till she herself gave her permission to speak. Not so much as a handbag was packed, for fear of exciting attention in the house; the undistinctive blue serge and sailor hat—supplemented subsequently by a thick veil—were selected as a travelling dress. Market-day at Langford, with a crowded railway station, was chosen for the day of departure, and the young lady walked the two miles that lay between it and her father's house in easy, leisurely fashion, as if she contemplated nothing more serious than a morning walk."

===Chinese===
"唉，一个伤心的故事！我想，过了一些时候，那可怜女人的心思，就飞到了她丈夫和小女儿身上吧？"

"是的。安东妮娅写信给莉娜，说那位母亲为了能看上孩子一眼，都快要死了，求她告诉蕾妮，她的母亲还活着——一个做母亲的，受尽了丈夫和父亲两方面的苛待——求她不顾一切地到她身边来，好赶在死亡的阴影合拢之前，把她抱在怀里。这一段故事，是我和蕾妮一同坐车去庄园时，她亲口讲给我听的。这姑娘告诉我，她读到那封信时，浑身的热血都翻腾起来了。她一下子被一股火一般的渴望攫住，恨不得立刻就亲吻那位母亲，替她申冤。那一时片刻，她恨透了她父亲，觉得非得立刻去当面质问他、声讨他的残忍不可。再一想，又另指了一条路子。她父亲说不定会禁止她与母亲有任何往来；她手头有的是钱，何不立时动身去意大利，从母亲口中，把她肯回英国那个家的条件，开给父亲听？于是这趟远行就这么定下了；年轻小姐许给莉娜，只要她替自己守住秘密、直到自己准她开口为止，便赏她一对自己那副漂亮钻石耳环。为了怕在宅子里惹人注意，连一只手提包都没收拾；那身毫不显眼的蓝色哔叽裙服和水手帽——后来又加了一层厚面纱——便被选作出门的行头。离家动身的日子，挑的是朗福德的集市日，那一天车站上人头攒动；这位年轻小姐把从车站到她父亲家这两英里的路，走得轻轻松松、不慌不忙，仿佛她盘算的不过是散一回早步罢了。"

===Original===
"Of course, so soon as she reached London all was plain sailing to her?"

"Yes. Lena, no doubt, supplied her with all necessary details respecting her journey. When she arrived at the Château Mascagni, she appears to have at once thoroughly succumbed to her mother's influence. Out of health although that mother was, René described her to me as the most fascinating woman she had ever met. I suppose the likeness between the two must have been something remarkable, for René said, after she had been a few days in the house and the mother had rallied a little in strength, the servants declared it was only by their dress that they could distinguish one from the other. On the fourth day after Miss Golding's arrival at the Château, her mother met her with a plan which, for fear of the effect that a refusal might have upon her health, she at once fell in with. It was to the effect that, instead of attempting negotiations with Mr. Golding through lawyers or by letter, she should herself go to him at his country house, throw herself upon his generosity, plead for forgiveness, and beg to be taken back to his heart once more."

===Chinese===
"当然啦，她一到伦敦，往后便一路顺风了吧？"

"是的。毫无疑问，是莉娜把一路上要用的种种细节都供给她的。她到了马斯卡尼城堡（Château Mascagni）之后，似乎一下子就完完全全地落在了她母亲的影响之下。那位母亲虽说身子骨不好，蕾妮却向我形容她，说是自己见过的最迷人的女人。我想，母女俩相像的程度，必定是非同小可的，因为蕾妮说，她在那屋里住了几天、母亲稍稍恢复了些气力之后，仆人们都说，只有凭着她们穿的衣裳，才能把两人分得清。戈尔丁小姐到城堡后的第四天，她母亲便拿着一个主意来见她，她生怕一口回绝会伤了母亲的身体，便一口应承下来。那主意的大意是：与其隔着律师或写信去跟戈尔丁先生交涉，倒不如她亲自到他的乡间宅子里去，把一切托付给他的宽宏大量，恳求他的宽恕，求他重新把她接回心坎里去。"

===Original===
"But why did not Miss René accompany her mother on this journey?"

"René was a force to be held in reserve. If her father refused her mother's request, she in her turn would refuse to return to her home, but would live on with her mother and grandfather at Alguida. The girl appears to have entertained bitter feelings against her father at this juncture—feelings possibly intensified by the thought of the sort of stepmother he intended to bestow upon her."

"Well, anyhow, so far as I can make out, Miss René's own mother hadn't much to boast of—in the way of common sense, at any rate. In fact, the two together appear to me to have acted more like a couple of schoolgirls than anything else. What made Mrs. Golding dress up in her daughter's clothes?"

===Chinese===
"可蕾妮小姐为什么不陪她母亲走这一趟呢？"

"蕾妮是要留着当一张底牌的。若是她父亲回绝了她母亲的请求，那么轮到她，就拒绝回家，而要跟着母亲和外祖父在阿尔圭达住下去。那姑娘在这一节骨眼上，似乎对她父亲怀着满腔怨愤——这种怨愤，也许是因想到他打算塞给她的那种继母，而越发强烈的。"

"哼，无论如何，据我看来，蕾妮小姐她这位亲妈，也实在没什么可夸的——至少在常识这一条上没有。说实在的，这娘儿俩凑在一块儿，干出的事倒更像一对女学生。戈尔丁夫人干嘛要换上她女儿的衣裳呢？"

===Original===
"That, I believe, was a matter of convenience merely. Mrs. Golding had no money, and her father was not overburdened with riches, and what little he had he held tightly. She had, for some reason or other, returned home with next to no wardrobe; René's dress was suitable for travelling, and not likely to attract attention. They neither of them seem to have given a thought to the possibility of rewards being offered for tidings of René; and thus, no doubt, while waiting for her train in Paris, Mrs. Golding did not hesitate to show herself in Paris streets. I need not go into the details of her journey to Langford; they are already known to you. The poor woman, not seeing any conveyance at the country station, must have walked in the drenching rain to the Hall. At the hall door, possibly, her courage suddenly failed her, and, instead of ringing for admission, she creeps to a window to get a glimpse of the home-life within. That glimpse is fatal. She sees her husband and the woman he intends to marry seated together at table. She takes in at a glance the refinement of the home, together with the rigid conventionality of English domestic life. A wave of memory, perhaps, brings before her episodes in her past career altogether out of tune with this home picture. She feels the impracticability of the mission on which she is bent; a fit of her old impetuosity seizes her; she rushes away in the darkness, takes a wrong turning, perhaps—who knows—?"

===Chinese===
"那一点，我想，纯粹是为了图个方便。戈尔丁夫人身无分文，她父亲也并不阔绰，仅有的一点还攥得死紧。不知为了什么缘故，她回家时几乎是两手空空、没带什么衣裳；蕾妮那身衣裳便于赶路，又不易惹人注目。这娘儿俩似乎谁也没往一处想：日后竟会有人为蕾妮的下落悬赏；正因如此，毫无疑问，戈尔丁夫人在巴黎等火车时，便毫不避讳地在巴黎街头露了面。她前往朗福德那一程的种种细情，我就不必细说了；那些你都已经知道。这可怜的女人，在那乡下小车站见不着任何车马，必定是冒着瓢泼大雨一路走到了庄园。到了门廊前，也许是她的勇气忽然垮了，于是她非但没有摇铃求见，反倒悄悄摸到一扇窗前，想瞧一眼里头的家居生活。这一眼，便要了她的命。她看见她丈夫和他打算迎娶的那个女人，并肩坐在桌前。她一眼便看尽了那份家居的雅致，连同英国家庭生活那刻板的体面。也许一阵回忆涌上来，把她过去经历中种种与此番温馨家居格格不入的片段，推到了她眼前。她感到自己执意要办的这趟差事，实在行不通；她那一向的急性子忽然发作；她在黑暗里夺路而逃，也许拐错了一个弯——谁知道呢？——"

===Original===
"Ah, yes; and the stream was there waiting for her, and she thought she would end it all. Poor soul!"

"Or it may be," said Loveday pityingly, "that some sweet story of sainthood and martyrdom that she had heard in her childish days came floating dimly into her brain as she made her way through the darkness, and she thought she would do her best to make atonement to the one whom she had so deeply injured by not standing in the way of his future happiness. Here is my train! Ah, yes; it is a sad, sad story!"

"Yes; for the present things are a trifle gloomy for the family at the Hall, I'll admit," said Ramsay, as he shut the carriage-door on Loveday; "but they'll turn over a new leaf there before long. There'll be a couple of weddings in the house before the year comes to an end, I'll be bound."

"No," said Loveday, as she steeled herself comfortably in a corner; "Mrs. Greenhow has shown herself in her true colours at this time of distress, and, from what I hear, will stand but little chance of becoming the second Mrs. Golding. Lord Guilleroy and the runaway René are the only two who will have to be congratulated as bride and bridegroom."

===Chinese===
"啊，是了；那条小溪就在那儿等着她，她便想着一了百了吧。可怜的人！"

"又或者，"洛芙迪满含怜悯地说，"她穿过黑暗一路走去时，孩提时听过的某个圣徒与殉道者的甜美故事，隐隐约约地浮上了她的脑际，于是她想，她要竭力向那个被她深深伤害过的人作出补偿——办法便是，不再挡在他日后幸福的路上。我的火车来了！唉，是啊；这是个悲伤、悲伤的故事！"

"是啊；眼下这一阵子，庄园里那一家的境况是有点黯淡，这我承认，"拉姆齐一面替洛芙迪关上车门，一面说，"可不出多久，他们就会翻过这一页的。我敢打赌，年内那宅子里准得办上两桩婚事。"

"不，"洛芙迪说着，把自己舒舒服服地安顿在一个角落里；"格林豪太太在这般患难之时，已露出了她的真面目，据我所闻，她想做成第二任戈尔丁夫人，希望怕是微乎其微了。吉勒罗伊勋爵和那位私奔出走的蕾妮，才是仅有的两位将以新郎新娘的身份受人道贺的。"

## The Black Bag Left on a Doorstep / 丢在门阶上的黑皮包

===Original===
“It’s a big thing,” said Loveday Brooke, addressing Ebenezer Dyer, chief of the well-known detective agency in Lynch Court, Fleet Street; “Lady Cathrow has lost £30,000 worth of jewellery, if the newspaper accounts are to be trusted.”

“They are fairly accurate this time. The robbery differs in few respects from the usual run of country-house robberies. The time chosen, of course, was the dinner-hour, when the family and guests were at table and the servants not on duty were amusing themselves in their own quarters. The fact of its being Christmas Eve would also of necessity add to the business and consequent distraction of the household. The entry to the house, however, in this case was not effected in the usual manner by a ladder to the dressing-room window, but through the window of a room on the ground floor﻿—a small room with one window and two doors, one of which opens into the hall, and the other into a passage that leads by the back stairs to the bedroom floor. It is used, I believe, as a sort of hat and coat room by the gentlemen of the house.”

===Chinese===
“这是桩大案子，”洛芙迪·布鲁克（Loveday Brooke）说道，正对着埃比尼泽·戴尔（Ebenezer Dyer）说话——戴尔是舰队街（Fleet Street）林奇大院（Lynch Court）那家赫赫有名的侦探事务所的所长；“要是报上所载可信的话，卡斯罗夫人（Lady Cathrow）丢了价值三万英镑的珠宝。”

“这回报上所载大致准确。这桩盗窃案与乡下宅邸惯常发生的盗窃，几乎没有什么不同。下手的时间，自然选在晚餐时分，彼时主客皆已入席，不当班的仆人也都在自己屋里寻欢作乐。又赶上那是圣诞前夜，照例更使全家上下忙乱不堪、心神分散。不过，此案的入室方式，并非如惯常那样架一架梯子攀上更衣室的窗户，而是从底层一间屋子的窗户钻进来——那是一间小屋，只有一扇窗、两扇门，一扇通向门厅，另一扇通向一条过道，由后楼梯可上到卧室那层。我想，宅子里的男主人们把它当作一种挂帽放衣的屋子来用。”

===Original===
“It was, I suppose, the weak point of the house?”

“Quite so. A very weak point indeed. Craigen Court, the residence of Sir George and Lady Cathrow, is an oddly-built old place, jutting out in all directions, and as this window looked out upon a blank wall, it was filled in with stained glass, kept fastened by a strong brass catch, and never opened, day or night, ventilation being obtained by means of a glass ventilator fitted in the upper panes. It seems absurd to think that this window, being only about four feet from the ground, should have had neither iron bars nor shutters added to it; such, however, was the case. On the night of the robbery, someone within the house must have deliberately, and of intention, unfastened its only protection, the brass catch, and thus given the thieves easy entrance to the house.”

===Chinese===
“我想，那便是这宅子的薄弱之处了？”

“正是。确是极薄弱的一处。克雷根苑（Craigen Court）是乔治·卡斯罗爵士（Sir George Cathrow）与卡斯罗夫人的宅邸，是一座造得奇形怪状的老房子，四面向外伸出。由于这扇窗子外面对着一堵光秃秃的墙，便镶上了彩色玻璃，用一只结实的铜插销牢牢扣住，日夜从不开启，全靠装在上层窗格里的一扇玻璃通风窗来透气。说起来简直荒唐：这扇窗离地面不过四英尺上下，竟既未加装铁栏，也未加装百叶窗；然而事实确是如此。盗窃案发生那晚，必定是宅子里有人存心、蓄意地拨开了它唯一的防护——那枚铜插销，于是给窃贼们大开了入室的方便之门。”

===Original===
“Your suspicions, I suppose, centre upon the servants?”

“Undoubtedly; and it is in the servants’ hall that your services will be required. The thieves, whoever they were, were perfectly cognizant of the ways of the house. Lady Cathrow’s jewellery was kept in a safe in her dressing-room, and as the dressing-room was over the dining-room, Sir George was in the habit of saying that it was the ‘safest’ room in the house. (Note the pun, please; Sir George is rather proud of it.) By his orders the window of the dining-room immediately under the dressing-room window was always left unshuttered and without blind during dinner, and as a full stream of light thus fell through it on to the outside terrace, it would have been impossible for anyone to have placed a ladder there unseen.”

===Chinese===
“那么，您的疑心是落在仆人们身上了？”

“毫无疑问；而要借重的，正是您在仆人饭厅里头的那番本事。无论窃贼是谁，他们对这宅子的情形都了如指掌。卡斯罗夫人的珠宝，平日收在她更衣室内的一只保险箱里；由于更衣室正位于餐厅楼上，乔治·卡斯罗爵士惯常说，那是全宅“最保险（safest）”的一间屋子。（请注意这个双关，爵士对它颇为得意。）按他的吩咐，就在更衣室窗子正下方的那扇餐厅窗户，每逢用餐时总不落百叶、不拉窗帘，于是一整束灯光便由此倾泻到外面的露台上，谁也不可能在那里架起梯子而不被发觉。”

===Original===
“I see from the newspapers that it was Sir George’s invariable custom to fill his house and give a large dinner on Christmas Eve.”

“Yes. Sir George and Lady Cathrow are elderly people, with no family and few relatives, and have consequently a large amount of time to spend on their friends.”

“I suppose the key of the safe was frequently left in the possession of Lady Cathrow’s maid?”

“Yes. She is a young French girl, Stephanie Delcroix by name. It was her duty to clear the dressing-room directly after her mistress left it; put away any jewellery that might be lying about, lock the safe, and keep the key till her mistress came up to bed. On the night of the robbery, however, she admits that, instead of so doing, directly her mistress left the dressing-room, she ran down to the housekeeper’s room to see if any letters had come for her, and remained chatting with the other servants for some time﻿—she could not say for how long. It was by the half-past-seven post that her letters generally arrived from St. Omer, where her home is.”

===Chinese===
“我从报上看到，乔治爵士有个雷打不动的规矩：每逢圣诞前夜，必把宅子挤得满满当当，摆上一桌盛大的晚宴。”

“是的。乔治·卡斯罗爵士与夫人年事已高，膝下无儿无女，亲戚也寥寥无几，因此有的是工夫花在朋友身上。”

“我想，保险箱的钥匙常常是交给卡斯罗夫人的贴身女仆保管的吧？”

“是的。她是个年轻的法国姑娘，名叫斯特凡妮·德尔克鲁瓦（Stephanie Delcroix）。她的职责是：女主人一离开更衣室，便立刻进去收拾；把随处搁着的珠宝收好，锁上保险箱，钥匙一直保管到女主人上楼就寝为止。然而盗窃案那晚，她承认自己并没有这样做——女主人前脚离开更衣室，她后脚就跑下楼到女管家的屋里，看有没有自己的来信，接着又留下来跟其他仆人闲扯了好一阵子——究竟多久，她说不上来。她的家在圣奥梅尔（St. Omer），寄给她的信件通常都是搭七点半那班邮班到的。”

===Original===
“Oh, then, she was in the habit of thus running down to enquire for her letters, no doubt, and the thieves, who appear to be so thoroughly cognizant of the house, would know this also.”

“Perhaps; though at the present moment I must say things look very black against the girl. Her manner, too, when questioned, is not calculated to remove suspicion. She goes from one fit of hysterics into another; contradicts herself nearly every time she opens her mouth, then lays it to the charge of her ignorance of our language; breaks into voluble French; becomes theatrical in action, and then goes off into hysterics once more.”

“All that is quite Français, you know,” said Loveday. “Do the authorities at Scotland Yard lay much stress on the safe being left unlocked that night?”

===Chinese===
“哦，这么说来，她往常也是这样跑下楼去打听信件的，想必无疑；而那些看来对这宅子了如指掌的窃贼，自然也熟知这一点。”

“也许吧；不过就眼下而言，我得说种种情形对这姑娘非常不利。况且受审时她的举止，也无助于消除嫌疑。她一阵歇斯底里接着又一阵歇斯底里；几乎每回一张口就自相矛盾，随后又把责任推到她不通我们语言上头；忽然滔滔不绝说起法语；接着手舞足蹈、做戏似的，然后又发起歇斯底里来。”

“这一切都很‘法兰西’，您要知道，”洛芙迪说，“苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）那边，很看重那晚保险箱没上锁这一节吗？”

===Original===
“They do, and they are instituting a keen enquiry as to the possible lovers the girl may have. For this purpose they have sent Bates down to stay in the village and collect all the information he can outside the house. But they want someone within the walls to hobnob with the maids generally, and to find out if she has taken any of them into her confidence respecting her lovers. So they sent to me to know if I would send down for this purpose one of the shrewdest and most clearheaded of my female detectives. I, in my turn, Miss Brooke, have sent for you﻿—you may take it as a compliment if you like. So please now get out your notebook, and I’ll give you sailing orders.”

===Chinese===
“他们确实看重，而且正在着力查访这姑娘可能有的那些情夫。为此，他们派了贝茨（Bates）下去，住在村子里，尽可能在宅子外头打听消息。可他们还需要一个人待在围墙里头，好跟一众女仆混熟，查清楚她有没有把哪一个引为心腹，吐露过自己情夫的事。于是他们差人来问我，肯不肯为此目的派手下一名最精明、头脑最清醒的女探员下去。布鲁克小姐，我呢，顺理成章地就把您给请了来——您不妨把这当作一句恭维。那么，现在就请您把笔记本拿出来，我来给您下达行动指令。”

===Original===
Loveday Brooke, at this period of her career, was a little over thirty years of age, and could be best described in a series of negations.

She was not tall, she was not short; she was not dark, she was not fair; she was neither handsome nor ugly. Her features were altogether nondescript; her one noticeable trait was a habit she had, when absorbed in thought, of dropping her eyelids over her eyes till only a line of eyeball showed, and she appeared to be looking out at the world through a slit, instead of through a window.

Her dress was invariably black, and was almost Quaker-like in its neat primness.

===Chinese===
洛芙迪·布鲁克在这一事业阶段，年方三十出头，要说清她的模样，最好用一连串的否定来形容。

她不高，也不矮；发色不深，也不浅；既称不上美貌，也算不上丑陋。她的五官全然平淡无奇、无可名状；她身上唯一惹眼的特征，是一个习惯——每逢凝神沉思之际，她便垂下眼睑，直到只露出一线眼白，看上去仿佛不是从窗口、而是从一道缝隙里向外观望这世道。

她的衣裳一成不变地是黑色，整洁、端庄得近乎贵格会教徒的模样。

===Original===
Some five or six years previously, by a jerk of Fortune’s wheel, Loveday had been thrown upon the world penniless and all but friendless. Marketable accomplishments she had found she had none, so she had forthwith defied convention, and had chosen for herself a career that had cut her off sharply from her former associates and her position in society. For five or six years she drudged away patiently in the lower walks of her profession; then chance, or, to speak more precisely, an intricate criminal case, threw her in the way of the experienced head of the flourishing detective agency in Lynch Court. He quickly enough found out the stuff she was made of, and threw her in the way of better-class work﻿—work, indeed, that brought increase of pay and of reputation alike to him and to Loveday.

Ebenezer Dyer was not, as a rule, given to enthusiasm; but he would at times wax eloquent over Miss Brooke’s qualifications for the profession she had chosen.

“Too much of a lady, do you say?” he would say to anyone who chanced to call in question those qualifications. “I don’t care twopence-halfpenny whether she is or is not a lady. I only know she is the most sensible and practical woman I ever met. In the first place, she has the faculty﻿—so rare among women﻿—of carrying out orders to the very letter: in the second place, she has a clear, shrewd brain, unhampered by any hard-and-fast theories; thirdly, and most important item of all, she has so much common sense that it amounts to genius﻿—positively to genius, sir.”

===Chinese===
大约五六年前，命运之轮猛地一转，洛芙迪被抛到了世上，身无分文，几乎举目无亲。她发觉自己没有任何可拿来谋生的才艺，索性不顾世俗成规，为自己择了一门职业，这门职业把她与从前的交游、与她原有的社会地位，一刀两断。五六年间，她在自己这一行的底层耐着性子苦苦熬着；后来，机缘——说得更确切些，是一桩错综复杂的刑事案件——使她得以接近林奇大院那家蒸蒸日上的侦探事务所那位经验丰富的所长。他很快就看出了她是块什么材料，于是把更高一等的差事派到她手里——那些差事，既给他也给洛芙迪，同样带来了报酬与声名的双丰收。

埃比尼泽·戴尔照例不是个容易激动的人；可一谈起布鲁克小姐对她所选这门行当的资质，他有时也会滔滔不绝、赞不绝口。

“您说她太像个上流女士了？”遇到有人碰巧质疑起这些资质，他便会对那人说道，“她是不是女士，我半个两便士都不在乎。我只知道，她是我所见过的最明白事理、最讲求实际的女人。其一，她有一种在女人中间殊为罕见的天赋——能够一字不差地照命令办事；其二，她头脑清醒、精明，不受任何僵化成见的束缚；其三，也是最重要的一点，她的常识丰富到了近乎天才的地步——货真价实的天才，先生。”

===Original===
But although Loveday and her chief as a rule, worked together upon an easy and friendly footing, there were occasions on which they were wont, so to speak, to snarl at each other.

Such an occasion was at hand now.

Loveday showed no disposition to take out her notebook and receive her “sailing orders.”

“I want to know,” she said, “if what I saw in one newspaper is true﻿—that one of the thieves before leaving, took the trouble to close the safe-door, and to write across it in chalk: ‘To be let, unfurnished’?”

“Perfectly true; but I do not see that stress need be laid on the fact. The scoundrels often do that sort of thing out of insolence or bravado. In that robbery at Reigate, the other day, they went to a lady’s Davenport, took a sheet of her notepaper, and wrote their thanks on it for her kindness in not having had the lock of her safe repaired. Now, if you will get out your notebook﻿—”

===Chinese===
不过，洛芙迪和她的所长虽说平日共事融洽友好，却也有些时候，他们照例会——可以这么说——彼此龇牙咧嘴一番。

眼下正是这么一个当口。

洛芙迪毫无掏出笔记本、领受她那“行动指令”的意思。

“我想知道，”她说，“我在某家报上看到的一则消息是真是假——其中一个窃贼临走之前，竟费心把保险箱的门关上，还用粉笔在门上写了一行字：‘招租，不配家具’？”

“千真万确；不过我看此事不必多加强调。那班无赖常常出于狂妄或卖弄，干出这等事来。前些日子雷盖特（Reigate）那桩盗窃案里，他们走到一位太太的达文波特小写字台（Davenport）前，抽出她一张信纸，在上面写下感谢的话，谢她好心没有把她保险箱上的锁修好。好了，您要是肯把笔记本拿出来——”

===Original===
“Don’t be in such a hurry,” said Loveday calmly: “I want to know if you have seen this?” She leaned across the writing-table at which they sat, one either side, and handed to him a newspaper cutting which she took from her letter-case.

Mr. Dyer was a tall, powerfully-built man with a large head, benevolent bald forehead and a genial smile. That smile, however, often proved a trap to the unwary, for he owned a temper so irritable that a child with a chance word might ruffle it.

The genial smile vanished as he took the newspaper cutting from Loveday’s hand.

“I would have you to remember, Miss Brooke,” he said severely, “that although I am in the habit of using dispatch in my business, I am never known to be in a hurry; hurry in affairs I take to be the especial mark of the slovenly and unpunctual.”

===Chinese===
“别这么急，”洛芙迪不动声色地说，“我想知道，您可曾见过这个？”她从皮夹里抽出一张剪报，隔着两人分坐两侧的写字台，探身递了过去。

戴尔先生是个身材高大、体格壮实的男子，头颅很大，前额光秃秃的，一副和善相，笑起来颇为亲切。然而那笑容往往成了粗心大意者的陷阱，因为他的脾气急躁得很，小孩子一句无心之语，都可能把它撩拨起来。

接过洛芙迪手里的剪报时，那亲切的笑容便消失了。

“我倒要请您记住，布鲁克小姐，”他严厉地说，“虽说我在公事上惯于讲求迅捷，可从不曾有人见我急急忙忙；办事急躁，在我看来，正是邋遢与不守时之人的特有标记。”

===Original===
Then, as if still further to give contradiction to her words, he very deliberately unfolded her slip of newspaper and slowly, accentuating each word and syllable, read as follows:﻿—

**“Singular Discovery.”**

“A black leather bag, or portmanteau, was found early yesterday morning by one of Smith’s newspaper boys on the doorstep of a house in the road running between Easterbrook and Wreford, and inhabited by an elderly spinster lady. The contents of the bag include a clerical collar and necktie, a Church Service, a book of sermons, a copy of the works of Virgil, a facsimile of Magna Charta, with translations, a pair of black kid gloves, a brush and comb, some newspapers, and several small articles suggesting clerical ownership. On the top of the bag the following extraordinary letter, written in pencil on a long slip of paper, was found:

===Chinese===
接着，仿佛要更进一步地驳倒她的说法似的，他煞有介事地展开她那片剪报，慢条斯理地、一字一顿、一音一节地念了起来：——

**“离奇的发现。”**

“昨天清晨，史密斯报摊的一个报童，在伊斯特布鲁克（Easterbrook）与雷福德（Wreford）之间那条路上的一户人家门阶上，发现了一只黑皮包，或者说一只旅行手提箱。该屋住着一位上了年纪的老小姐。皮包内的物品计有：一条牧师硬领和领带、一本《祈祷书》、一册布道集、一卷维吉尔（Virgil）著作、《大宪章》（Magna Charta）一件带译文的仿真件、一副黑色小羊皮手套、一把刷子和一把梳子、几份报纸，还有若干表明物主为神职人员的小物件。皮包的最上面，还发现了一封如下的离奇书信，是用铅笔写在一条长纸条上的：

===Original===
‘The fatal day has arrived. I can exist no longer. I go hence and shall be no more seen. But I would have Coroner and Jury know that I am a sane man, and a verdict of temporary insanity in my case would be an error most gross after this intimation. I care not if it is felo de se, as I shall have passed all suffering. Search diligently for my poor lifeless body in the immediate neighbourhood﻿—on the cold heath, the rail, or the river by yonder bridge﻿—a few moments will decide how I shall depart. If I had walked aright I might have been a power in the Church of which I am now an unworthy member and priest; but the damnable sin of gambling got hold on me, and betting has been my ruin, as it has been the ruin of thousands who have preceded me. Young man, shun the bookmaker and the racecourse as you would shun the devil and hell. Farewell, chums of Magdalen. Farewell, and take warning. Though I can claim relationship with a Duke, a Marquess, and a Bishop, and though I am the son of a noble woman, yet am I a tramp and an outcast, verily and indeed. Sweet death, I greet thee. I dare not sign my name. To one and all, farewell. O, my poor Marchioness mother, a dying kiss to thee. R.I.P.’

===Chinese===
‘那命定之日已经来临。我再也活不下去了。我由此而去，将不复为人所见。但我要让验尸官与陪审团知道，我是个神志健全之人，既有此申明，若仍将我的案子裁为暂时性精神错乱，便是天大的谬误。哪怕定为自杀罪（felo de se），我也并不在乎，因为彼时我已尽脱一切苦楚。请就近在我那可怜的无生气躯壳旁细细搜寻——在那冰冷的荒原上、那铁轨旁、或那座桥下的河流里——片刻之间，我便将决定以何种方式辞世。倘若我当初行得端正，本可在教会中成为举足轻重之人，而如今我不过是它一名不配的教士与成员；然而那可咒的赌博之罪攫住了我，下注成了我的毁灭，一如它是成千上万个先我而去之人的毁灭。年轻人，要像躲避魔鬼与地狱那样，躲避赌注经纪人和赛马场。永别了，麦格达伦学院（Magdalen）的旧友们。永别了，并以此为鉴。我虽能攀上一户公爵、一位侯爵和一位主教做亲戚，虽是位高贵妇人之子，然而千真万确，我不过是个流浪汉、一个被弃之人。甜蜜的死亡啊，我迎向你。我不敢签下自己的名字。向一切人，永别了。唉，我那可怜的侯爵夫人母亲啊，将临终一吻献与你。愿灵魂安息。’

===Original===
“The police and some of the railway officials have made a ‘diligent search’ in the neighbourhood of the railway station, but no ‘poor lifeless body’ has been found. The police authorities are inclined to the belief that the letter is a hoax, though they are still investigating the matter.”

In the same deliberate fashion as he had opened and read the cutting, Mr. Dyer folded and returned it to Loveday.

“May I ask,” he said sarcastically, “what you see in that silly hoax to waste your and my valuable time over?”

===Chinese===
“警方及数名铁路职员已在火车站附近作了一番‘细细搜寻’，却未见任何‘可怜的无生气躯壳’。警方倾向于认为此信乃一场恶作剧，不过仍在就此展开调查。”

戴尔先生以方才打开、念诵那剪报时同样的一丝不苟，把它折好，交还给洛芙迪。

“我可否请问，”他讥诮地说，“这无聊的恶作剧里，究竟有什么值得您如此浪费您我宝贵光阴的？”

===Original===
“I wanted to know,” said Loveday, in the same level tones as before, “if you saw anything in it that might in some way connect this discovery with the robbery at Craigen Court?”

Mr. Dyer stared at her in utter, blank astonishment.

“When I was a boy,” he said sarcastically as before, “I used to play at a game called ‘what is my thought like?’ Someone would think of something absurd﻿—say the top of the monument﻿—and someone else would hazard a guess that his thought might be﻿—say the toe of his left boot, and that unfortunate individual would have to show the connection between the toe of his left boot and the top of the monument. Miss Brooke, I have no wish to repeat the silly game this evening for your benefit and mine.”

“Oh, very well,” said Loveday, calmly; “I fancied you might like to talk it over, that was all. Give me my ‘sailing orders,’ as you call them, and I’ll endeavour to concentrate my attention on the little French maid and her various lovers.”

===Chinese===
“我想知道的，”洛芙迪仍以方才那般平稳的声调说，“是您可曾从中看出什么，能以某种方式把这桩发现，与克雷根苑那桩盗窃案联系起来？”

戴尔先生目瞪口呆、满眼错愕地瞪着她。

“我小时候，”他依旧讥诮地说，“常玩一种叫‘我的心思像什么’的游戏。某人会想出一样荒唐东西——比方说纪念碑的顶——另一人便瞎猜一通，说他那心思也许是——比方说他左脚靴子的鞋尖，于是那倒霉的家伙就得说清楚，他左脚靴子的鞋尖和纪念碑的顶之间有何瓜葛。布鲁克小姐，今儿晚上为您我二位着想，我可没半点重玩这无聊把戏的兴致。”

“哦，那便罢了，”洛芙迪不动声色地说，“我原以为您也许愿意把这事议一议，如此而已。请把您所谓的‘行动指令’给我吧，我自当尽力把心思集中到那法国小女仆和她那班形形色色的情夫身上去。”

===Original===
Mr. Dyer grew amiable again.

“That’s the point on which I wish you to fix your thoughts,” he said; “you had better start for Craigen Court by the first train tomorrow﻿—it’s about sixty miles down the Great Eastern line. Huxwell is the station you must land at. There one of the grooms from the Court will meet you, and drive you to the house. I have arranged with the housekeeper there﻿—Mrs. Williams, a very worthy and discreet person﻿—that you shall pass in the house for a niece of hers, on a visit to recruit, after severe study in order to pass board-school teachers’ exams. Naturally you have injured your eyes as well as your health with overwork; and so you can wear your blue spectacles. Your name, by the way, will be Jane Smith﻿—better write it down. All your work will be among the servants of the establishment, and there will be no necessity for you to see either Sir George or Lady Cathrow﻿—in fact, neither of them have been apprised of your intended visit﻿—the fewer we take into our confidence the better. I’ve no doubt, however, that Bates will hear from Scotland Yard that you are in the house, and will make a point of seeing you.”

“Has Bates unearthed anything of importance?”

“Not as yet. He has discovered one of the girl’s lovers, a young farmer of the name of Holt; but as he seems to be an honest, respectable young fellow, and entirely above suspicion, the discovery does not count for much.”

“I think there’s nothing else to ask,” said Loveday, rising to take her departure. “Of course, I’ll telegraph, should need arise, in our usual cipher.”

===Chinese===
戴尔先生又和悦了起来。

“我所希望您把心思放在上面的，正是这一点，”他说，“您最好搭明天头一班火车动身去克雷根苑——沿大东铁路线（Great Eastern line）下去大约六十英里。您得在赫克斯韦尔（Huxwell）这一站下车。苑里会派一名马夫在那里接您，驾车送您回宅子。我已同那里的女管家——威廉斯太太（Mrs. Williams），一位极贤德持重的人——商定好了：您在宅子里就充作她的一个外甥女，是为着备考学董会学校的教师资格，发愤苦读伤了身子，特地下乡将养将养。顺理成章地，您既因劳累伤了眼睛，又伤了身子；于是您可以戴上那副蓝眼镜。顺便说一句，您的名字就叫简·史密斯（Jane Smith）——最好把它记下来。您所有的差事，都在这宅子的仆人之中，并无必要去见乔治爵士或卡斯罗夫人——事实上，您此行并没有知会他们二位任何一位——我们信得过的人，越少越好。不过毫无疑问，贝茨会从苏格兰场那里得知您已进了宅子，必定会设法来见您。”

“贝茨可曾挖出什么要紧的东西？”

“眼下还没有。他查出了这姑娘的一个情夫，是个名叫霍尔特（Holt）的年轻农场主；不过此人看来是个老实正派的年轻人，全然无可怀疑，故这一发现算不得什么。”

“我想再没有什么要问的了，”洛芙迪说着，起身告辞，“当然，万一有事，我会照我们惯用的密码给您去电报。”

===Original===
The first train that left Bishopsgate for Huxwell on the following morning included, among its passengers, Loveday Brooke, dressed in the neat black supposed to be appropriate to servants of the upper class. The only literature with which she had provided herself in order to beguile the tedium of her journey was a small volume bound in paper boards, and entitled, *The Reciter’s Treasury*. It was published at the low price of one shilling, and seemed specially designed to meet the requirements of third-rate amateur reciters at penny readings.

Miss Brooke appeared to be all-absorbed in the contents of this book during the first half of her journey. During the second, she lay back in the carriage with closed eyes, and motionless as if asleep or lost in deep thought.

The stopping of the train at Huxwell aroused her, and set her collecting together her wraps.

===Chinese===
次日清晨，自主教门（Bishopsgate）开往赫克斯韦尔的头一班火车，其乘客之中便有洛芙迪·布鲁克，她一身整洁的黑衣，据说正合上层仆役的身份。她为排遣旅途烦闷而随身携带的唯一读物，是一本纸面装帧的小册子，书名《朗诵者宝库》（The Reciter’s Treasury）。此书定价仅一先令，似乎专为迎合那些在一便士朗诵会上登台的三流业余朗诵者的需要而编。

头半个旅程，布鲁克小姐似乎全神贯注于此书的内容之中。后半个旅程，她便仰靠在车厢里，闭着双目，纹丝不动，仿佛已然入睡，又或是陷入了沉思。

火车在赫克斯韦尔停下，这才惊醒了她，令她动手收拾起自己的披肩围巾。

===Original===
It was easy to single out the trim groom from Craigen Court from among the country loafers on the platform. Someone else beside the trim groom at the same moment caught her eye﻿—Bates, from Scotland Yard, got up in the style of a commercial traveler, and carrying the orthodox “commercial bag” in his hand. He was a small, wiry man, with red hair and whiskers, and an eager, hungry expression of countenance.

“I am half-frozen with cold,” said Loveday, addressing Sir George’s groom; “if you’ll kindly take charge of my portmanteau, I’d prefer walking to driving to the Court.”

The man gave her a few directions as to the road she was to follow, and then drove off with her box, leaving her free to indulge Mr. Bate’s evident wish for a walk and confidential talk along the country road.

===Chinese===
要在这月台上一群乡下闲汉之中，认出克雷根苑那位衣冠齐整的马夫来，本是轻而易举。然而在那位齐整的马夫之外，同一瞬间另有一人也落入了她的眼帘——是从苏格兰场来的贝茨，打扮成旅行推销员的模样，手里提着推销员那一式一样、合乎规矩的“样品提箱”。他是个瘦小精悍的汉子，头发和络腮胡都是红的，脸上一副急切而饥渴的神情。

“我都快冻僵了，”洛芙迪对乔治爵士的马夫说，“劳您驾替我照看一下手提箱，我倒宁可走着去苑里，不坐车。”

那人给她指了几句该走的路，便赶着车带上她的箱子走了，留下她自在地去遂贝茨先生那显而易见的心愿——沿着乡间大道边走边密谈。

===Original===
Bates seemed to be in a happy frame of mind that morning.

“Quite a simple affair, this, Miss Brooke,” he said: “a walk over the course, I take it, with you working inside the castle walls and I unearthing without. No complications as yet have arisen, and if that girl does not find herself in jail before another week is over her head, my name is not Jeremiah Bates.”

“You mean the French maid?”

“Why, yes, of course. I take it there’s little doubt but what she performed the double duty of unlocking the safe and the window too. You see I look at it this way, Miss Brooke: all girls have lovers, I say to myself, but a pretty girl like that French maid, is bound to have double the number of lovers than the plain ones. Now, of course, the greater the number of lovers, the greater the chance there is of a criminal being found among them. That’s plain as a pikestaff, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
那天早上，贝茨似乎心情甚佳。

“这件事再简单不过了，布鲁克小姐，”他说，“依我看，不过是走个过场罢了：您在城堡围墙里头办事，我在外头挖线索。至今尚无任何波折，要是那姑娘不出一个礼拜不进班房，我就不叫杰里迈亚·贝茨（Jeremiah Bates）。”

“您是说那个法国女仆？”

“嗐，那当然。依我看，保险箱和那扇窗子都是她开的，一身兼二任，这点几乎无可怀疑。您瞧我是这么看的，布鲁克小姐：姑娘家都有情夫，我对自己说，可像那法国女仆那样漂亮的姑娘，情夫自然比模样平庸的多上一倍。那么理所当然，情夫越多，其中混着一个作奸犯科之徒的可能便越大。这道理明摆着，不是吗？”

===Original===
“Just as plain.”

Bates felt encouraged to proceed.

“Well, then, arguing on the same lines, I say to myself, this girl is only a pretty, silly thing, not an accomplished criminal, or she wouldn’t have admitted leaving open the safe door; give her rope enough and she’ll hang herself. In a day or two, if we let her alone, she’ll be bolting off to join the fellow whose nest she has helped to feather, and we shall catch the pair of them ’twixt here and Dover Straits, and also possibly get a clue that will bring us on the traces of their accomplices. Eh, Miss Brooke, that’ll be a thing worth doing?”

“Undoubtedly. Who is this coming along in this buggy at such a good pace?”

The question was added as the sound of wheels behind them made her look round.

===Chinese===
“明摆得很。”

贝茨受了鼓励，继续说下去。

“那么，照着同样一路推下去，我对自己说，这姑娘不过是个漂亮的小傻瓜，并非老练的罪犯，否则她也不会承认自己没锁保险箱的门；给她足够的绳子，她自会把自己吊死。要是由着她去，不出一两天，她便会脚底抹油，跑去跟那个被她帮衬着肥了窝的家伙会合，我们便可在这一带与多佛海峡（Dover Straits）之间把他们一双拿获，说不定还能由此摸到一条线索，追到他们那些同伙的踪迹。呃，布鲁克小姐，这买卖可值得一做吧？”

“毫无疑问。驾着这辆轻便马车、这么好的脚程迎面过来的，是谁啊？”

这个问题是她听见身后车轮声响、回头张望时，顺口添上的。

===Original===
Bates turned also. “Oh, this is young Holt; his father farms land about a couple of miles from here. He is one of Stephanie’s lovers, and I should imagine about the best of the lot. But he does not appear to be first favourite; from what I hear someone else must have made the running on the sly. Ever since the robbery I’m told the young woman has given him the cold shoulder.”

As the young man came nearer in his buggy he slackened pace, and Loveday could not but admire his frank, honest expression of countenance.

“Room for one﻿—can I give you a lift?” he said, as he came alongside of them.

And to the ineffable disgust of Bates, who had counted upon at least an hour’s confidential talk with her, Miss Brooke accepted the young farmer’s offer, and mounted beside him in his buggy.

===Chinese===
贝茨也回过头去。“哦，这是小霍尔特（young Holt）；他父亲在离这儿两英里光景的地方种地。他是斯特凡妮的情夫之一，我估摸还是这一伙里头顶拔尖的一个。可他似乎并不算头号红人；据我所闻，必是另有其人暗地里占了先。听说自打那桩盗窃案以后，那年轻姑娘就一直对他冷冷的。”

那年轻人驾着轻便马车走近了些，放慢了速度，洛芙迪也不禁赞赏他那坦率诚恳的面容。

“还能再坐一个——载您一程可好？”他驶到他们身边时说道。

贝茨本指望至少能同她密谈上个把钟头，眼见此景，简直是说不出的懊恼；而布鲁克小姐却接受了这年轻农场主的好意，登上他的轻便马车，在他身旁坐了下来。

===Original===
As they went swiftly along the country road, Loveday explained to the young man that her destination was Craigen Court, and that as she was a stranger to the place, she must trust to him to put her down at the nearest point to it that he would pass.

At the mention of Craigen Court his face clouded.

“They’re in trouble there, and their trouble has brought trouble on others,” he said a little bitterly.

“I know,” said Loveday sympathetically; “it is often so. In such circumstances as these suspicions frequently fastens on an entirely innocent person.”

“That’s it! that’s it!” he cried excitedly; “if you go into that house you’ll hear all sorts of wicked things said of her, and see everything setting in dead against her. But she’s innocent. I swear to you she is as innocent as you or I are.”

===Chinese===
马车顺着乡间大道轻快地飞驰，洛芙迪向年轻人说明，自己的目的地是克雷根苑，又说她既是外地来的生面孔，便只得仰仗他，在最靠近苑子而又顺路的地方把她放下。

一听见“克雷根苑”三字，他的脸色便阴沉下来。

“那宅子里正遭着难，而他们的难，又连累别人也遭了难，”他略带怨愤地说。

“我知道，”洛芙迪同情地说，“事情往往如此。像这样的情形，怀疑常常会落到一个全然无辜的人头上。”

“正是这样！正是这样！”他激动地叫道，“您要是进了那宅子，便会听见种种说她的恶毒话，看见样样事情都对她不利。可她是清白的。我向您发誓，她跟您和我一样清白。”

===Original===
His voice rang out above the clatter of his horse’s hoots. He seemed to forget that he had mentioned no name, and that Loveday, as a stranger, might be at a loss to know to whom he referred.

“Who is guilty Heaven only knows,” he went on after a moment’s pause; “it isn’t for me to give an ill name to anyone in that house; but I only say she is innocent, and that I’ll stake my life on.”

“She is a lucky girl to have found one to believe in her, and trust her as you do,” said Loveday, even more sympathetically than before.

“Is she? I wish she’d take advantage of her luck, then,” he answered bitterly. “Most girls in her position would be glad to have a man to stand by them through thick and thin. But not she! Ever since the night of that accursed robbery she has refused to see me﻿—won’t answer my letters﻿—won’t even send me a message. And, great Heavens! I’d marry her tomorrow, if I had the chance, and dare the world to say a word against her.”

===Chinese===
他的声音盖过了马蹄的嘚嘚声，高高响起。他似乎忘了自己并没点名道姓，也忘了洛芙迪既是个生人，未必弄得清他指的是谁。

“谁是有罪之人，只有老天知道，”他顿了顿，又接着说，“那宅子里的人，我不该给任何一个安上恶名；可我只想说，她是清白的，这一点我拿性命作保。”

“她能遇上一个像您这般信她、信得过她的人，可真是个有福气的姑娘，”洛芙迪说着，比方才愈加同情。

“是吗？那我倒巴不得她沾一沾这福气的光呢，”他怨愤地答道，“大多数落到她这般境地的姑娘，能有个男人不顾一切地站在她们一边，都会满心欢喜。可她偏不！自打那桩该死的盗窃案那一晚起，她就拒不见我——不回我的信——连个口信也不肯捎给我。老天哪！只要有机会，我明天就娶她，看这世上谁敢说她半个不字。”

===Original===
He whipped up his pony. The hedges seemed to fly on either side of them, and before Loveday realized that half her drive was over, he had drawn rein, and was helping her to alight at the servants’ entrance to Craigen Court.

“You’ll tell her what I’ve said to you, if you get the opportunity, and beg her to see me, if only for five minutes?” he petitioned before he remounted his buggy. And Loveday, as she thanked the young man for his kind attention, promised to make an opportunity to give his message to the girl.

Mrs. Williams, the housekeeper, welcomed Loveday in the servants’ hall, and then took her to her own room to pull off her wraps. Mrs. Williams was the widow of a London tradesman, and a little beyond the average housekeeper in speech and manner.

===Chinese===
他一扬鞭催动小马。两旁的树篱仿佛朝身后飞去，洛芙迪还没回过神来，半程车路便已走完，他已勒住缰绳，扶她在克雷根苑仆人进出的侧门下了车。

“您要是得了机会，就把我对您说的这番话转告她，求她见我一面，哪怕五分钟也好，行吗？”他重新登上轻便马车之前，这样恳求道。洛芙迪一面谢这年轻人一路殷勤相待，一面答应一定找个机会把他的口信带到。

女管家威廉斯太太在仆人饭厅里迎候了洛芙迪，随后把她带回自己屋里去卸下披肩围巾。威廉斯太太是伦敦一位商人的遗孀，言谈举止比寻常女管家要略胜一筹。

===Original===
She was a genial, pleasant woman, and readily entered into conversation with Loveday. Tea was brought in, and each seemed to feel at home with the other. Loveday in the course of this easy, pleasant talk, elicited from her the whole history of the events of the day of the robbery, the number and names of the guests who sat down to dinner that night, together with some other apparently trivial details.

The housekeeper made no attempt to disguise the painful position in which she and every one of the servants of the house felt themselves to be at the present moment.

“We are none of us at our ease with each other now,” she said, as she poured out hot tea for Loveday, and piled up a blazing fire. “Everyone fancies that everyone else is suspecting him or her, and trying to rake up past words or deeds to bring in as evidence. The whole house seems under a cloud. And at this time of year, too; just when everything as a rule is at its merriest!” and here she gave a doleful glance to the big bunch of holly and mistletoe hanging from the ceiling.

“I suppose you are generally very merry downstairs at Christmas time?” said Loveday. “Servants’ balls, theatricals, and all that sort of thing?”

===Chinese===
她和蔼可亲，很爽快地便同洛芙迪攀谈起来。茶端了上来，两人彼此都觉得相处融洽。洛芙迪就在这般轻松愉快的闲谈之中，从她口中套出了盗窃案当天事情的全部经过，那晚入席宾客的人数与姓名，连同另外一些乍看微不足道的细枝末节。

女管家毫不掩饰她自己和宅子里每一个仆人，此刻都觉得自己处境难堪。

“眼下咱们彼此之间谁都不自在了，”她一面给洛芙迪倒着热茶，一面往熊熊的炉火里添柴，说道，“人人都疑心旁人在怀疑自己，想翻出从前说过的话、做过的事，拿来当作罪证。整座宅子都像罩在一片阴云之下。偏偏又赶上年关这个时候；往常这正是样样事最热闹的当口！”说到这里，她凄然瞥了一眼从天花板上垂下来的那一大捆冬青与槲寄生。

“我猜，往年圣诞节，你们楼下总是一派热闹吧？”洛芙迪说，“仆人舞会啦，演戏啦，诸如此类？”

===Original===
“I should think we were! When I think of this time last year and the fun we all had, I can scarcely believe it is the same house. Our ball always follows my lady’s ball, and we have permission to ask our friends to it, and we keep it up as late as ever we please. We begin our evening with a concert and recitations in character, then we have a supper and then we dance right on till morning; but this year!”﻿—she broke off, giving a long, melancholy shake of her head that spoke volumes.

“I suppose,” said Loveday, “some of your friends are very clever as musicians or reciters?”

“Very clever indeed. Sir George and my lady are always present during the early part of the evening, and I should like you to have seen Sir George last year laughing fit to kill himself at Harry Emmett dressed in prison dress with a bit of oakum in his hand, reciting the ‘Noble Convict!’ Sir George said if the young man had gone on the stage, he would have been bound to make his fortune.”

===Chinese===
“可不是嘛！我一想去年这会儿，大伙儿玩得有多开心，简直不敢相信这是同一座宅子。咱们的舞会总是跟在夫人那场舞会之后，上头准咱们请自己的朋友来，爱闹到多晚便闹到多晚。晚会先由一场音乐会和分角色朗诵开场，然后吃夜宵，然后一直跳到天明；可今年！”——她话音一断，长长地、凄然地摇了摇头，那意思胜过千言万语。

“我想，”洛芙迪说，“你们的朋友里头，有些人是很高明的乐师或朗诵者吧？”

“高明得很。晚会前半截，乔治爵士和夫人总是到场，您真该瞧瞧去年乔治爵士笑成什么样——哈里·埃米特（Harry Emmett）身穿囚服，手里捏着一截麻絮，朗诵那首《高贵的囚犯》（The Noble Convict），把他乐得要死！乔治爵士说，这年轻人要是下了舞台这一行，保准能发大财。”

===Original===
“Half a cup, please,” said Loveday, presenting her cup. “Who was this Harry Emmett then﻿—a sweetheart of one of the maids?”

“Oh, he would flirt with them all, but he was sweetheart to none. He was footman to Colonel James, who is a great friend of Sir George’s, and Harry was constantly backwards and forwards bringing messages from his master. His father, I think, drove a cab in London, and Harry for a time did so also; then he took it into his head to be a gentleman’s servant, and great satisfaction he gave as such. He was always such a bright, handsome young fellow and so full of fun, that everyone liked him. But I shall tire you with all this; and you, of course, want to talk about something so different;” and the housekeeper sighed again, as the thought of the dreadful robbery entered her brain once more.

“Not at all. I am greatly interested in you and your festivities. Is Emmett still in the neighbourhood? I should amazingly like to hear him recite myself.”

“I’m sorry to say he left Colonel James about six months ago. We all missed him very much at first. He was a good, kindhearted young man, and I remember he told me he was going away to look after his dear old grandmother, who had a sweet-stuff shop somewhere or other, but where I can’t remember.”

===Chinese===
“请再给我半杯，”洛芙迪递上茶杯说，“那么，这位哈里·埃米特是谁——是哪个女仆的情郎？”

“哦，他跟她们个个都调笑，可对谁都不是情郎。他是詹姆斯上校（Colonel James）的跟班，上校是乔治爵士的至交，哈里便常来常往，替他家老爷传话送信。我记得他父亲在伦敦赶马车，哈里有一阵子也干过这行；后来他心血来潮，要做绅士家的仆人，干这一行也是大受好评。他向来是个精神、英俊的年轻人，又满肚子风趣，谁都喜欢他。可我拿这些唠叨话烦您了；您自然是想谈点全然不同的事；”女管家又叹了口气，因为那桩可怕的盗窃案，又一闪进了她的脑海。

“哪里的话。我对您和你们的那些热闹，兴致大着呢。埃米特如今还在这一带吗？我真想自己听他朗诵一回呢。”

“可惜他大约半年前就离开詹姆斯上校了。起初大伙儿都挺想他。他是个心肠又好又厚的年轻人，我记得他跟我说过，他要走，是为了去照看他那位亲爱的老外婆，他外婆在什么地方开着一家糖果点心铺，究竟在哪儿，我可记不得了。”

===Original===
Loveday was leaning back in her chair now, with eyelids drooped so low that she literally looked out through “slits” instead of eyes.

Suddenly and abruptly she changed the conversation.

“When will it be convenient for me to see Lady Cathrow’s dressing-room?” she asked.

The housekeeper looked at her watch. “Now, at once,” she answered: “it’s a quarter to five now and my lady sometimes goes up to her room to rest for half an hour before she dresses for dinner.”

“Is Stephanie still in attendance on Lady Cathrow?” Miss Brooke asked as she followed the housekeeper up the back stairs to the bedroom floor.

“Yes, Sir George and my lady have been goodness itself to us through this trying time, and they say we are all innocent till we are proved guilty, and will have it that none of our duties are to be in any way altered.”

===Chinese===
这会儿洛芙迪已仰靠在椅背上，眼睑垂得极低，简直名副其实地是从“缝隙”里、而不是从眼睛里向外张望了。

她忽然、突兀地转了话头。

“我什么时候方便去看看卡斯罗夫人的更衣室？”她问。

女管家看了看表。“现在，立刻便可，”她答道，“眼下差一刻五点，夫人有时会在换晚装赴宴之前，上楼到自己房里歇上半个钟头。”

“斯特凡妮如今还在卡斯罗夫人身边伺候吗？”布鲁克小姐一边跟着女管家由后楼梯上到卧室那层，一边问。

“是的，乔治爵士和夫人在这段难熬的日子里对我们好到了极点，他们说，在证实有罪之前，我们个个都是清白的，并执意不许我们的任何差事有半点改动。”

===Original===
“Stephanie is scarcely fit to perform hers, I should imagine?”

“Scarcely. She was in hysterics nearly from morning till night for the first two or three days after the detectives came down, but now she has grown sullen, eats nothing and never speaks a word to any of us except when she is obliged. This is my lady’s dressing-room, walk in please.”

Loveday entered a large, luxuriously furnished room, and naturally made her way straight to the chief point of attraction in it﻿—the iron safe fitted into the wall that separated the dressing-room from the bedroom.

It was a safe of the ordinary description, fitted with a strong iron door and Chubb lock. And across this door was written with chalk in characters that seemed defiant in their size and boldness, the words: “To be let, unfurnished.”

===Chinese===
“我猜，斯特凡妮怕是难胜任她那一份了吧？”

“怕是难胜任。侦探们下来后的头两三天，她几乎从早到晚都在犯歇斯底里，可如今她变得闷闷不乐，什么也不吃，除了不得已，对我们谁也不肯说半个字。这便是我家夫人的更衣室，请进。”

洛芙迪走进一间陈设豪华的大屋子，自然地径直走向其中最惹眼之处——那只嵌在更衣室与卧室之间那堵墙里的铁保险箱。

那是只寻常式样的保险箱，装着一扇厚实的铁门、一把查布锁（Chubb lock）。而就在这扇门上，有人用粉笔写着一行字，字迹之大、之放肆，俨然一副挑衅的气焰：“招租，不配家具。”

===Original===
Loveday spent about five minutes in front of this safe, all her attention concentrated upon the big, bold writing.

She took from her pocketbook a narrow strip of tracing-paper and compared the writing on it, letter by letter, with that on the safe door. This done she turned to Mrs. Williams and professed herself ready to follow her to the room below.

Mrs. Williams looked surprised. Her opinion of Miss Brooke’s professional capabilities suffered considerable diminution.

“The gentlemen detectives,” she said, “spent over an hour in this room; they paced the floor, they measured the candles, they﻿—”

“Mrs. Williams,” interrupted Loveday, “I am quite ready to look at the room below.” Her manner had changed from gossiping friendliness to that of the business woman hard at work at her profession.

===Chinese===
洛芙迪在这保险箱跟前足足花了五分钟，全部心思都集中在那几个又大又放肆的字上。

她从笔记本里取出一条窄窄的描图纸，把上面的笔迹同保险箱门上的一字一字母地比照了一番。比完，她转向威廉斯太太，声称自己已可随她下楼去看那间屋子了。

威廉斯太太面露诧异之色。她对布鲁克小姐那身专业本领的评价，着实大打折扣。

“那几位男侦探，”她说，“在这间屋里待了一个多钟头；他们踱来踱去量地板，他们量蜡烛，他们——”

“威廉斯太太，”洛芙迪打断她，“我很可以下楼去看那间屋子了。”她的神态，已由闲话家常的亲切，转为一个埋头本行公事的女干事模样。

===Original===
Without another word, Mrs. Williams led the way to the little room which had proved itself to be the “weak point” of the house.

They entered it by the door which opened into a passage leading to the backstairs of the house. Loveday found the room exactly what it had been described to her by Mr. Dyer. It needed no second glance at the window to see the ease with which anyone could open it from the outside, and swing themselves into the room, when once the brass catch had been unfastened.

Loveday wasted no time here. In fact, much to Mrs. Williams’s surprise and disappointment, she merely walked across the room, in at one door and out at the opposite one, which opened into the large inner hall of the house.

Here, however, she paused to ask a question:

“Is that chair always placed exactly in that position?” she said, pointing to an oak chair that stood immediately outside the room they had just quitted.

The housekeeper answered in the affirmative. It was a warm corner. “My lady” was particular that everyone who came to the house on messages should have a comfortable place to wait in.

===Chinese===
